# CratonVM - Full Repository Review

**Date:** 2026-06-20  -  **Method:** 38 parallel Opus agents (one per module + 5 cross-cutting), high reasoning effort, static review of ~860K LOC Rust across 19 workspace crates + docs/scripts.

**Findings tally:** 2 critical - 32 high - 71 medium - 90 low - 60 info.

> Coverage percentages below are **judged estimates, not instrumented** - no `cargo-llvm-cov`/tarpaulin run was performed (most VM tests need a JDK + the app gauntlet). Treat them as expert estimates and validate the sub-85% crates with real instrumentation.

---

## Part 1 - Code review

#### Detail - Critical

**[CRIT] `nb-crypto-security` - SecureRandom.nextBytes is a non-cryptographic splitmix64 DRBG keyed on a single 64-bit seed**  
`native-builtins/src/crypto_impl.rs:1089 (secure_random_fill), reached from native_secure_random_next_bytes:1147 and native_secure_random_generate_seed:1174` - vulnerability  
The registered java/security/SecureRandom.nextBytes / generateSeed natives route ALL output through secure_random_fill(), which derives bytes as splitmix64(seed ^ counter) over a per-instance state of a single u64 seed plus an incrementing u64 counter. The seed is taken from ONE 64-bit OS-entropy draw on first use (secure_random_entropy_seed:1066). splitmix64 is a trivially invertible, non-cryptographic PRNG: an attacker who observes ~8 bytes of output can recover the internal state and predict all past/future output, and the whole stream has at most 64 bits of entropy regardless of how many bytes are drawn. These last-write-wins registrations (comment at crypto_impl.rs:1288-1296) OVERRIDE securerandom.rs, so this is the effective SecureRandom for the interpreter path. Any AES key, GCM nonce, IV, RSA/EC keygen randomness, TLS secret, or token generated via SecureRandom.nextBytes is predictable. The CipherProbe in jca/cipher.rs:50-56 itself generates AES-256 keys and GCM IVs this way. NOTE the class-level SecureRandom::next_bytes (crypto_impl.rs:952) on its use_os_entropy path DOES draw straight from the OS each call and is fine — but the *registered natives* bypass it in favor of the splitmix64 DRBG.  
Fix: Replace secure_random_fill with a real CSPRNG: either (a) draw every nextBytes/generateSeed output directly from os_random_bytes() (BCryptGenRandom/getrandom) for unseeded instances, or (b) use a keyed cryptographic DRBG (HMAC-DRBG/CTR-DRBG over a >=256-bit OS-seeded state, e.g. the rand_chacha / aes crates already vendored) and fold setSeed material into that state. Never expose splitmix64 output as SecureRandom. Keep the splitmix64 path only for the explicitly deterministic java.util.Random semantics, never java.security.SecureRandom.

**[CRIT] `oss-crates-readiness` - Publishable cratonvm-vm has an unconditional dep on publish=false cratonvm-native-awt → blocks vm + all downstream crates from crates.io**  
`vm/Cargo.toml:67` - spec-compliance  
cratonvm-vm sets no publish gate (publishable) but declares `cratonvm-native-awt = { path = "../native-awt", version = "0.3.0" }` as a normal, non-optional dependency. native-awt/Cargo.toml:370 sets `publish = false`. The dep is used unconditionally at vm/src/vm/vm_init.rs:1938 (cratonvm_native_awt::register_awt_natives) with no #[cfg(feature)] guard. crates.io refuses to publish a crate whose normal (non-dev) dependencies are not on the registry, so cratonvm-vm can never publish, and by transitivity neither can cratonvm-cli, libcratonvm, or cratonvm-embed (all depend on vm). This refutes the workspace Cargo.toml assertion that 'every library crate is publishable.'  
Fix: Either (a) make native-awt publishable (remove its publish=false, give it a real description/keywords — it already has them — and publish it), or (b) gate the AWT registration behind an optional feature so the dep becomes `optional = true` and vm publishes without it, or (c) mark vm/cli/libcratonvm/cratonvm-embed publish=false too and only ship the leaf library crates at 0.3.0. Until resolved, correct the misleading workspace comment claiming all library crates are publishable.

#### Detail - High

**[HIGH] `types` - to_value()/is_object() can fabricate an ObjectRef from attacker-controlled long bits (context-free decode)**  
`types/src/compact_value.rs:886-907 (to_value SUB_OBJECT arm); :974-977 (is_object)` - vulnerability  
Because CompactValue::long stores i64 bits verbatim (compact_value.rs:375-377), a primitive long whose top 14 bits coincide with NANBOX_BITS, whose 3-bit sub-tag equals SUB_OBJECT, and whose 47-bit payload is non-zero and 8-byte aligned is bit-identical to a real heap reference. The unchecked to_value() then returns Value::Object(Some(ObjectRef::from_raw(payload))) — i.e. it dereferences/roots a pointer synthesized from a primitive long. Attacker-controlled bytecode (lxor/ladd producing a chosen 64-bit pattern, then lstore into a slot read by a context-free consumer) can drive this. The aligned+non-null filter is necessary but explicitly NOT sufficient. Memory safety of the whole decode path therefore depends entirely on every out-of-scope caller routing through to_value_checked/decode_by_descriptor — an invariant this crate cannot enforce. This is thoroughly documented (the Safety contract at :803-850) and a release-countable degradation counter exists, but the unchecked API remains a live UAF/over-root foot-gun if any caller forgets the heap predicate.  
Fix: Treat the unchecked context-free reference decoders as a hard audit boundary: grep every caller of to_value()/is_object() in the vm/gc/jit crates and confirm each either (a) has proven JVM type context that the slot is a reference, or (b) routes through the _checked variant. Consider making to_value() debug-only-callable or renaming it to_value_assuming_typed() to force call-site intent. At minimum, add a CI assertion that object_degradation_count()==0 across the regression-suite fuzz corpus.

**[HIGH] `native-collections` - Side-table prune function gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays is never called — overlay/registry leak fix is dead code**  
`native-collections/src/lib.rs:21718` - bug  
gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays() is the only mechanism that bounds the growth of the process-global side-tables: obj_key_shards registry, lhm_overlay, lhm_heap_backed, ll_overlay, cslm_comparator_table, and lhm_ptr_cache. Its own doc comment (lines 21712-21717) states the GC epilogue 'must call this with its is_live closure for the prune to take effect' and that the wiring 'lives outside this crate's scope'. A repo-wide grep (`grep -rn gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays --include=*.rs .`) finds ONLY the definition (lib.rs:21718) and a doc reference (lib.rs:30097) — NO caller. By contrast gc_scan_collection_overlay_roots and gc_update_collection_overlay_refs ARE wired into vm/src/memory/roots.rs:344 and vm/src/memory/gc.rs:319. Net effect: every LinkedList/LinkedHashMap/LinkedHashSet/TreeMap/TreeSet/CSLM object ever touched leaves a permanent side-table entry. This is a steady memory leak AND degrades every GC into an O(total-collections-ever-created) walk in for_each_overlay_ref. Matches the known LHM-overlay-GC-leak issue in project memory.  
Fix: Wire gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays into the GC collection epilogue in vm/src/memory/gc.rs immediately after gc_update_collection_overlay_refs, passing the GC's authoritative post-trace is_live predicate. Add a regression test that creates+drops many overlay-backed collections, runs a simulated GC with an is_live closure, and asserts the registry/overlay tables shrink.

**[HIGH] `native-collections` - GC root scan silently skips entire overlay tables on a poisoned lock → use-after-free of backing arrays**  
`native-collections/src/lib.rs:21572` - bug  
for_each_overlay_ref (the single funnel for BOTH the moving-GC root scan, for_rooting=true, and the post-GC remap) guards every table lock with `if let Ok(mut t) = ll_overlay().lock()` / tm_array_table() / tm_fast_table() / ts_array_table() / cslm_comparator_table() (lines 21572, 21581, 21608, 21621, 21631, 21645). std::sync::Mutex poisons if any thread panics while holding the lock. If any overlay lock is poisoned, the `if let Ok` arm is skipped, so during the root scan the backing ObjectRefs in that table are NOT pushed as roots — a moving GC then frees/relocates live backing arrays (TreeMap data array, LHM table, CSLM comparator) that are still reachable from Java, producing a dangling ObjectRef and a subsequent use-after-free / SIGSEGV. The same skip during remap leaves stale pre-move pointers. A native that panics mid-mutation under catch_unwind (the codebase uses catch_unwind elsewhere, e.g. ChmMonitorGuard::drop) makes poisoning reachable.  
Fix: Recover the guard on poison instead of skipping during a GC: use `.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner())` for every table in for_each_overlay_ref so the GC always sees the (possibly inconsistent but non-null) refs and roots them. Dropping a root must never be silent. Consider parking_lot::Mutex (already a workspace dep, used for CHM stripes) which cannot poison.

**[HIGH] `native-io-core` - SSRF: IPv4-mapped IPv6 literal bypasses the always-on cloud-metadata block**  
`native-io/src/outbound_policy.rs:297-320` - vulnerability  
The always-on default policy denies cloud-metadata/link-local addresses via is_link_local_metadata_ip(). For an IpAddr::V6 it dispatches to is_v6_link_local_or_metadata(), which only matches fe80::/10 and fd00:ec2::254 — it never unwraps IPv4-mapped IPv6 (::ffff:a.b.c.d) via to_ipv4_mapped(). So `::ffff:169.254.169.254`, `::ffff:169.254.170.2` (ECS task-role creds), etc. parse as V6, miss the v4 169.254.0.0/16 check, and default_policy returns Allow. On a dual-stack host the OS connect for an IPv4-mapped literal routes to the v4 IMDS, giving guest code an SSRF path to AWS/GCP/etc. metadata even with the default (no-env) posture. The fix for the SAME class of bug was already applied to the opt-in is_private_or_loopback_ip() (line 213, with a test at outbound_policy.rs:621-622) but NOT to the always-on metadata path. policy_connect's re-vet (outbound_policy.rs:381-390) and socket_channel resolve_and_vet re-format the resolved addr as [::ffff:169.254.169.254]:80 and still miss it, so the per-resolved-IP re-check does not close it either.  
Fix: In is_link_local_metadata_ip, mirror is_private_or_loopback_ip: for IpAddr::V6, first try v6.to_ipv4_mapped() and run is_v4_link_local() on the embedded v4 before falling through to is_v6_link_local_or_metadata(). Add tests for ::ffff:169.254.169.254 and ::ffff:169.254.170.2 asserting Deny.

**[HIGH] `native-io-channels` - SelectionKey attachment/channel/selector ObjectRefs in sk_table are remapped after GC but never scanned as GC roots (use-after-free / wrong-object)**  
`native-io/src/nio_selector.rs:1805-1869 (SkState + sk_table_update_after_gc); cross-ref vm/src/memory/roots.rs (absent)` - bug  
sk_table stores `channel`, `selector`, and user `attachment` as real ObjectRefs. The relocation hook sk_table_update_after_gc IS wired into vm/src/memory/gc.rs:238 (the #[allow(dead_code)]/'until wired' comment is stale), so addresses of SURVIVING objects are remapped after a moving compaction. However, sk_table is NOT in the GC root-scan set in vm/src/memory/roots.rs (which does scan lang_math, jboss_msc, net_phase_e, and collection-overlay side-tables). Marking therefore never visits these ObjectRefs. A user attachment installed via key.attach(obj) (sk_attach, line 2177) is stored ONLY in the Rust table — sk_attach does not write the SelectionKey object's field 4 — so if no Java-side reference keeps it alive, the moving GC reclaims it while sk_table still holds the ref. A subsequent SelectionKey.attachment() (line 2200) then returns an ObjectRef into freed/reused heap memory: wrong-object dispatch in the lucky case, wild dereference in the unlucky one. channel/selector are usually co-rooted from the Selector's Java key-set so are largely mitigated, but attachments have no such guarantee. Only affects the moving/compacting GC configuration.  
Fix: Add a gc_scan_selector_roots(&mut roots) function in nio_selector.rs that pushes every channel/selector/attachment ObjectRef (and per-selector key_obj) into the root set, and call it from vm/src/memory/roots.rs alongside the other native side-table scanners. The existing update_after_gc hook then only needs to remap survivors (which is correct). Alternatively, also mirror the attachment into the SelectionKeyImpl object field so it is reachable through normal heap tracing.

**[HIGH] `nb-phases-late` - X509Certificate.verify(PublicKey) and Certificate.verify are no-ops that never throw on a bad signature**  
`native-builtins/src/phases_late.rs:32636-32656, 32870-32889` - vulnerability  
Both `verify(Ljava/security/PublicKey;)V` natives are registered unconditionally but their bodies are entirely behind `#[cfg(feature = "legacy-synthetic-crypto")]`, so in the default build they do nothing and return Ok(None). Even WITH the feature enabled, the X509 path explicitly discards the result (`let _ = parsed.verify_signature(...)`) and the Certificate path documents 'we don't throw here', so a tampered/forged certificate whose signature does NOT verify is silently accepted. The JDK contract requires `verify()` to throw SignatureException/InvalidKeyException on mismatch. Any app relying on Certificate.verify() for trust decisions (chain validation, pinned-CA checks) is silently defeated. This is the synthetic-crypto path which may be superseded by the real X509CertImpl in some flows, but the registration is unconditional so it can shadow real verification.  
Fix: In the default (non-legacy) build, either do not register these natives (let the real java.security.cert bytecode/X509CertImpl handle verification) or implement a real signature check that throws SignatureException on failure. When `verify_signature` returns false, return Err(SignatureException) instead of discarding the result.

**[HIGH] `nb-phases-early` - Primary Class.forName overloads lack the control-byte / path-separator hardening the module overload enforces**  
`native-builtins/src/lang_class.rs:1345-1346 (native_class_for_name); 1613-1619 (native_class_for_name_3)` - vulnerability  
native_class_for_name reads the requested name and does only `dotted_name.replace('.', "/")` before passing `internal_name` to `ctx.ensure_class_initialized` (line 1535) / `loader.loadClass`. Unlike the sibling native_class_for_name_module (lines 1675-1680), it does NOT reject NUL bytes, control bytes (<0x20, 0x7F), forward slashes, or backslashes. The module overload's own comment (lines 1672-1674) states such names 'would let an attacker probe the filesystem.' Since ensure_class_initialized resolves names against the classpath/filesystem (`<name>.class`), an attacker-controlled name (e.g. from a deserialized/config-supplied string reaching Class.forName) such as a backslash- or `..`-laden path on Windows could drive filesystem probing or large/odd allocations. native_class_for_name_3 (the standard 3-arg forName) delegates straight to native_class_for_name, so the most commonly reached reflection entry point is unguarded. This is an inconsistency with a defense the codebase already has and tests for on the other overload.  
Fix: Extract the module overload's validation (reject empty, any byte <0x20 or ==0x7F, '/' and '\\' in the dotted name; reject '..' path segments) into a shared helper and apply it in native_class_for_name BEFORE building internal_name and before any loader dispatch. Add traversal/control-byte/backslash regression tests for the primary and 3-arg overloads mirroring the existing t19_h12_class_for_name_module_* tests.

**[HIGH] `nb-lang` - AtomicIntegerFieldUpdater/AtomicLongFieldUpdater getAndIncrement/getAndDecrement/addAndGet lose updates and return bogus 0 under contention**  
`native-builtins/src/atomic_updater.rs:678-697 (aifu_get_and_increment), 699-718 (aifu_get_and_decrement), 720-739 (aifu_add_and_get), 827-867 (alfu_get_and_increment / alfu_get_and_decrement), 869-887 (alfu_add_and_get)` - bug  
These six accessors use a BOUNDED CAS retry loop (`for _ in 0..1024`) and, on loop exhaustion under contention, fall through to `Ok(Some(Value::Int(0)))` / `Value::Long(0)`. This both (a) applies NO update and (b) returns a fabricated value 0 as if it were the previous/new field value — a silent lost update plus a wrong return. The author already identified and fixed exactly this anti-pattern for getAndAdd/incrementAndGet/decrementAndGet by switching to the unbounded atomic primitive (see comments at lines 641-648 and 651-661: 'previous bounded CAS loop silently returned 0 ... a lost update'), but left these six unconverted. AtomicXFieldUpdater is heavily used by Reactor/Netty/Kafka for lock-free state machines and backpressure counters; a lost increment corrupts those state machines.  
Fix: Replace the bounded loops with the unbounded atomic primitives already used elsewhere: getAndIncrement = atomic_fetch_add_{int,long}(target,slot,1); getAndDecrement = ...(-1); addAndGet = atomic_fetch_add returning prev.wrapping_add(delta). At minimum, make the loop unbounded (`loop { ... }`) so it never returns a fabricated 0.

**[HIGH] `nb-crypto-security` - RSA and ECDSA private-key operations use variable-time BigUint::modpow with no blinding or CRT (timing side-channel)**  
`native-builtins/src/crypto_impl.rs:1771 (BigUint::modpow); used for private ops at :2006 (Rsa::sign), :2483 (rsa_cipher_decrypt with d), :1902 (RSA keygen), and the EcdsaPrivateKey scalar path noted at :3072` - vulnerability  
modpow is plain left-to-right square-and-multiply over a non-constant-time BigUint (mul/div_rem/modulo are not constant-time and the per-bit branch is selected by exp.bit(i)). The doc comment (VULN(2), lines 1759-1770) honestly states it is NOT side-channel resistant and is used with the secret RSA exponent d and ECDSA nonce inverse. There is no message/exponent blinding and no RSA-CRT. A timing adversary co-located with a server doing RSA decryption/signing (e.g. TLS, JWT signing) can mount a Kocher-style timing attack to recover d. ECDSA scalar mul (EcPoint::scalar_mul) is similarly variable-time over a secret nonce, which can leak nonce bits and enable lattice key-recovery.  
Fix: Route every private-key RSA/EC operation through the audited constant-time crates already discussed in this tree (rsa, p256/ecdsa with RFC 6979) rather than the hand-rolled BigUint. Minimum mitigation: add RSA base blinding (r^e * c) and RSA-CRT, and a constant-time/Montgomery-ladder scalar multiply for ECDSA. Until then, document loudly that these primitives must not face a timing adversary.

**[HIGH] `nb-net` - Server-side request parser ignores Transfer-Encoding: chunked (request-smuggling / body desync)**  
`native-builtins/src/net_phase_e.rs:6064-6140 (parse_http_request)` - vulnerability  
The synthetic HttpServer request parser reads the body strictly via Content-Length (content_length defaults to 0 at line 6108). It never inspects the Transfer-Encoding header. A client/proxy that sends `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` (with no Content-Length, or with both) gets content_length=0, so the parser consumes zero body bytes and leaves the chunked payload unread in the socket buffer. When this server sits behind a proxy or reuses the connection, the unconsumed chunk data is interpreted as the start of the next request — the classic TE.CL / CL.TE smuggling desync. The client-side code does honor chunked (http_decode_chunked, line 3156) and the parser carefully rejects conflicting Content-Length (good), but the server path has no TE handling at all. Note for contrast: the JDK's own HttpServer rejects requests that carry both CL and TE.  
Fix: In parse_http_request, detect `Transfer-Encoding` after collecting headers: (a) if TE is present and not exactly `identity`, either decode the chunked body via the existing http_decode_chunked helper (after bounding accumulated size against http_max_request_body) or reject with 400/501; (b) reject any request that carries BOTH Transfer-Encoding and Content-Length with 400 (RFC 9112 §6.1). Add tests mirroring re10_conflicting_content_length_rejected_with_400 for TE+CL and for a chunked-only body.

**[HIGH] `nb-net` - IoFuture result and notifier ObjectRefs are not GC-rooted (UAF/stale-pointer under moving GC)**  
`native-builtins/src/xnio_async.rs:218-247, 1102-1114, 240 (FutureState.result / notifiers)` - bug  
NotifierEntry stores raw ObjectRefs (notifier + attachment) and FutureState.result is an Option<Value> that can hold Value::Object(ObjectRef). These are pushed into a cross-thread Arc<IoFutureInner> via add_notifier (line 1111) while the future is WAITING and held until it settles — i.e. across arbitrary subsequent VM allocations — and the future can complete on a different thread. There is NO gc_scan/gc_update hook for these refs (compare net_phase_e.rs gc_scan_re10_handler_roots / gc_update_re10_handler_refs which explicitly root+relocate stored handler ObjectRefs against the moving collector). The author was clearly aware of the hazard for the exception path (line 242-244 stores exception_message as a String specifically 'to avoid heap-tracing') but left result and notifiers as raw refs. Under the relocating GC path the stored pointers can dangle → use-after-free when fire_notifier/get dereferences them. Default GC is currently non-moving, so this is latent, but the moving path exists and is exercised in this VM.  
Fix: Treat IoFuture-held ObjectRefs the same way handler roots are treated: register a gc-scan that yields notifier/attachment/result refs as roots and a gc-update that rewrites them from the collector's pointer map. Alternatively, pin these objects for the future's lifetime. Add a moving-GC stress test that allocates heavily between add_notifier and completion.

**[HIGH] `nb-misc` - ScheduledThreadPoolExecutor pump stores a Java object as a raw integer pointer and reconstructs it later — stale-pointer UAF under moving GC**  
`native-builtins/src/scheduled_pump.rs:122 (store) and :167-176 (reconstruct+invoke)` - bug  
`ScheduledRegistry::register` stores the runnable as `runnable_ptr: runnable.as_ptr() as usize` in a process-global `Vec<Arc<ScheduledTask>>`. The pump later does `ObjectRef::from_raw(runnable_ptr)` and calls `ctx.invoke_virtual(runnable, "run", ...)`. The integer is NOT registered as a GC root and is NOT updated after compaction. The SAFETY comment claims the executor's strong reference + same-thread execution keep it valid, but neither prevents a compacting/moving GC from relocating the object between submission and a later periodic tick (GC can run at safepoints inside any intervening Java call). After a move the stored integer is stale → invoke_virtual dispatches on freed/moved memory (UAF / type confusion). This is the exact stale-OOP bug class the project's memory notes repeatedly cite. By contrast sunec_point.rs:199-288 demonstrates the correct discipline (pin_native_root/read_native_pin) for surviving GC across invokes. Default non-moving/selective-promote mode may mask it, like the other documented stale-OOP bugs.  
Fix: Do not store Java object identity as a raw integer across a GC boundary. Either (a) register the runnable as a native GC root for the task's lifetime and re-read it through the root after each safepoint, mirroring sunec_point's pin_native_root/read_native_pin pattern, or (b) store the runnable in a live, GC-tracked field of the ScheduledFuture/executor object and re-fetch via ctx.get_field at pump time. Never call ObjectRef::from_raw on a long-lived stored pointer.

**[HIGH] `jit-x64` - Register-only operand-stack oop is invisible to the default conservative GC root scan**  
`jit/src/x64.rs:5584-5599, 6855-6878, 12259-12310` - bug  
Operand-stack entries of kind StackSlot::CalleeSaved(R12-R15/RBX/RSI/RDI) are a 'zero-cost push: no code emitted until consumed' and are NOT flushed before a GC-capable call. flush_scratch_registers (12259) only spills Scratch(R8/R9) and Xmm(0-7) entries to frame slots; emit_pre_safepoint_spill (6787) flushes register-mapped LOCALS to their canonical frame slots but does not flush CalleeSaved operand-stack temporaries that no longer correspond to a live register-mapped local. collect_live_oop_homes (6857-6868) explicitly documents that operand-stack CalleeSaved/Scratch Reg homes 'are the only ones the stack scan can miss' — i.e. the default conservative frame-region scan (scan_one_frame_precise, register file not scanned) can drop such a root. This is the documented register-only missed-root UAF class (memory: ReflRepro/A2, fork6/A4, SB-CRASH-04). It is currently mitigated, not closed: (a) the precise/spill fixes are env-gated OFF by default (CRATONVM_JIT_SAFEPOINT_REG_SPILL, CRATONVM_SHADOW_STACK), and (b) gc_quiescence forces a non-moving young sweep while any thread is in JIT so an over-/under-retained slot cannot be relocated — but a genuinely lost (un-rooted) register-only oop can still be swept and reused, producing the historical rc=139 UAF.  
Fix: Make the full-register safepoint spill (CRATONVM_JIT_SAFEPOINT_REG_SPILL=all path, ALL_SPILL_GPRS) the DEFAULT for GC-capable safepoints, or flush CalleeSaved/Scratch operand-stack entries to frame slots in flush_scratch_registers before every call (not just Scratch/Xmm). The spill is conservative (scanner re-validates via heap.is_object_address) so it can only over-retain. Add a differential GC-stress test (allocate in a loop with a live ref held as a CalleeSaved operand-stack temporary across an invoke) under the default config.

**[HIGH] `classloading` - JSR/RET methods bypass bytecode type verification (structural-only fallback)**  
`classloading/src/verifier.rs:151-157, 761-851` - vulnerability  
Any pre-Java-7 (major<=50) method that uses jsr/jsr_w/ret is routed to verify_method_structural_only, which performs ONLY instruction decoding, branch-target bounds and exception-handler-range bounds checks — it does NO operand-stack / local type-state verification. The worklist inference verifier admittedly cannot model the distinct ReturnAddress values JVMS §4.10.2.5 requires (they collapse to Top), so the code deliberately skips type checking for these methods. HotSpot's real verifier performs full subroutine-inlining type checks here; this implementation does not. A crafted legacy classfile (e.g. via ASM/ByteBuddy emitting jsr) can therefore present type-unsound bytecode (e.g. iload of a slot holding a reference, getfield through an int) that passes verification. If the interpreter/JIT trusts verified types (the usual VM contract), this is a type-confusion → memory-unsafety primitive reachable from an attacker-supplied class. JVMS permits leniency for pre-7 inference, but 'lenient' in the spec still means type-sound subroutine inlining, not skipping the type pass.  
Fix: Either (a) implement JVMS §4.10.2.5 subroutine inlining (clone the subroutine body per jsr call site with a distinct ReturnAddress(pc) so the worklist does not merge them), or (b) refuse to load jsr/ret-using classfiles unless an explicit opt-in flag is set, or (c) require the downstream interpreter/JIT to treat type-unverified (structural-only) methods as untrusted and insert runtime type/bounds checks. Document the residual risk in readiness docs and gate it behind a config flag that defaults to reject for untrusted loaders.

**[HIGH] `gc-heap` - CompactHeader forwarding pointer silently truncates addresses above 8 GB**  
`gc/src/compact_header.rs:212-231` - bug  
set_forwarding_ptr/forwarding_ptr encode addr>>3 in only 30 bits (7 high bits @31-25 + 23 low bits @22-0), addressing at most 8 GB of object-aligned space. set_forwarding_ptr does `high = (shifted >> 23) & 0x7F`, masking away every bit of `addr>>3` above bit 29. The only debug_assert checks 8-byte alignment, NOT range. On a real 64-bit VM, heap arenas are Vec<u8> mmap'd at large addresses (e.g. 0x7f...), so any real forwarding address would be silently truncated and forwarding_ptr() would return a wrong, in-bounds-looking pointer -> moving-GC reads/writes the wrong object (heap corruption / UAF). LATENT: the live moving GC (gen_heap.rs forward_object, region.rs) uses the legacy 32-byte ObjectHeader.forwarding_ptr (a full *mut u8), and CompactHeader/CompactAllocator are only referenced from tests and a flag string (config.use_compact_headers defaults false, vm_init.rs:5036 only appends '-XX:+UseCompactObjectHeaders'). So it cannot corrupt the heap today, but the moment compact headers are wired into a moving collector this is an immediate memory-safety bug.  
Fix: Either (a) widen the forwarding encoding to cover the full pointer range (store the raw *mut u8 in a side table keyed by object address, like the identity-hash table, since 30 bits cannot represent a 48-bit pointer), or (b) add a hard range check in set_forwarding_ptr that panics/returns-error when addr>>3 >= 2^30 so a future wiring cannot silently corrupt. The forwarding_ptr_roundtrip test only exercises addresses up to 0x0FFF_FFF8 (256 MB) and so never catches this — add a >8GB address case.

**[HIGH] `gc-collectors` - SATB completeness depends on an unenforced external contract: per-thread SATB buffers are never drained by the collector**  
`gc/src/satb.rs:62-113, 329-371; gc/src/g1_concurrent.rs:392-397` - bug  
The SATB pre-barrier fast path writes overwritten references into a thread-local THREAD_SATB_BUFFER and only flushes to the global sharded SatbQueue when that buffer fills (~256 entries) or when the owning thread itself calls flush_thread_satb_buffer. deactivate_and_drain()/drain() only drain the global queue shards; they have NO visibility into other mutator threads' thread-local buffers. The remark happens-before proof in deactivate_and_drain only covers entries that reached a shard. If the VM safepoint protocol does not arrange for EVERY parked mutator thread to flush its own THREAD_SATB_BUFFER before remark, up to 255 live old-reference values per thread are lost, stay unmarked, and concurrent_sweep frees them while reachable -> use-after-free. The comment at g1_concurrent.rs:393-395 explicitly delegates this to 'the safepoint protocol' outside this module, with no in-module enforcement.  
Fix: Register each thread's SatbBuffer in a global registry so the collector can drain ALL mutator buffers during the remark safepoint; or add a debug_assert at remark that fires if any registered thread buffer is non-empty. Document the contract as a hard precondition on remark/cleanup and add a multi-mutator integration test.

**[HIGH] `vm-core` - JNI dispatch leaves dangling *mut JvmThread / *mut SharedVm in TLS if the native bridge unwinds (no RAII guard, no catch_unwind)**  
`vm/src/vm/vm_exec.rs:11406-11456 and 11479-11524 (also JNI_OnLoad at 5318-5322)` - vulnerability  
The JNI dispatch path stores raw `thread as *mut _` and `shared` into thread-local storage via `set_jni_thread`/`set_jni_context`, calls the unsafe `dispatch_jni_native`, then clears them MANUALLY with `clear_jni_thread`/`clear_jni_context`. Unlike the Rust-native callback path (line 482) which wraps the callback in `std::panic::catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(..))`, the JNI path has NO catch_unwind and NO Drop/RAII guard. If `dispatch_jni_native` (or any code between set and clear) unwinds — a Rust panic raised inside the JNI bridge, an internal assertion, or a future edit that adds a fallible call — the clears are skipped and a dangling `*mut JvmThread` pointing at a stack/Arc-borrowed thread stays in TLS. A later JNI callback (FindClass/CallMethod from native code) reads that stale pointer → use-after-free. The captured pointer also aliases `thread`/`shared` which are live borrows; soundness depends entirely on the synchronous clear running. The arity-mismatch early-returns (11430, 11499) DO clear correctly, so the gap is specifically the unwind path through the unsafe dispatch.  
Fix: Wrap the set/dispatch/clear span in an RAII guard whose Drop calls clear_jni_thread()/clear_jni_context() (so it runs on both success and unwind), OR wrap dispatch_jni_native in catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(..)) like the native-callback path at line 482 and clear in all arms. Prefer the guard — it also covers the JNI_OnLoad call at 5318 where untrusted library code runs.

**[HIGH] `vm-interpreter` - Unbounded global resolution cache → memory-exhaustion DoS**  
`vm/src/runtime/lockfree_resolve.rs:486-503` - vulnerability  
SharedResolutionState::cache_method and cache_field insert into global_methods/global_fields (RwLock<FxHashMap>) unconditionally with NO size bound and NO eviction policy. Only the per-thread ThreadLocalResolveCache is bounded (max_entries default 4096); the cross-thread shared maps grow without limit. Adversarial or dynamic-codegen workloads that mint many distinct (class,member,descriptor,loader_epoch) keys — Groovy IndyInterface, LambdaMetafactory proxies, ByteBuddy/CGLIB, ServiceLoader over hostile jars — keep promoting unique entries, so the shared maps grow to OOM. promoted_invokes has invalidate_promoted_for_class but no capacity cap either. Attacker-supplied bytecode controls the key space, making this remotely reachable in any app-server/plugin scenario.  
Fix: Bound global_methods/global_fields/promoted_invokes with an LRU or random-eviction policy and a configurable cap (mirror the thread-local max_entries), or add a watermark + bulk-evict. At minimum document the unbounded growth and gate it behind a size limit per ClassId so a single hostile class can't fill it.

**[HIGH] `vm-exec-init` - Concurrent <clinit> failure leaks the init claim + waiter, blocking other threads for the full 30s timeout**  
`vm/src/vm/vm_util.rs:646-651, 675, 709, 740 (vs finalize_init at 820-845, waiter insert at 442)` - bug  
ensure_class_initialized_shared() claims a class for initialization by setting class.initializing_thread and inserting a (Mutex,Condvar) waiter into shared.class_init_waiters (line 442), then calls initialize_class_shared(). Several early-error returns inside initialize_class_shared return Err WITHOUT calling the local finalize_init closure (which is the ONLY place that clears initializing_thread, removes the waiter entry, and cvar.notify_all()): the bytecode-verify failure (646-651 sets state=InitializationError and returns), prepare_class_shared(...)? (675), the superinterface ensure_class_initialized_shared(...)? (709), and ensure_system_stdin_object(...)? (740). When any of these fires, the waiter is never notified and initializing_thread is never cleared. A second thread that already blocked at line 387 (cvar.wait_for ... Duration::from_secs(30)) sleeps the full 30 seconds before timing out and re-checking state; meanwhile other threads observe init_thread.is_some() (effective_state=Initializing) and also block. This is a multi-second liveness stall / soft-deadlock on every class whose <clinit> fails under concurrent first-touch (common with frameworks that race static init across worker threads).  
Fix: Make initialize_class_shared infallible w.r.t. cleanup: wrap the body so that on ANY Err it calls finalize_init(shared, class_id, ClassState::InitializationError) (which clears initializing_thread, removes the waiter, and notify_all) before returning the error. Equivalently, move the claim+waiter lifecycle into a guard/RAII or have the single error tail at lines 1287+ run finalize_init for every failure path, not just the <clinit>-execution path.

**[HIGH] `vm-jni-jvmti` - ClassFileTransformer chain (TRANSFORMER_CHAIN) is not a GC root and is not remapped after a moving GC**  
`vm/src/runtime/instrument.rs:89-121,840` - bug  
The process-wide static `transformer_chain()` (RwLock<Vec<TransformerEntry>>) stores live Java transformer instances as raw `ObjectRef` (field `transformer_ref`). Grepping memory/roots.rs and memory/gc.rs shows NO code folds these ObjectRefs into the GC root set (collect_roots) nor rewrites them via any update_after_gc. Mockito/Jacoco register a ClassFileTransformer once, then GCs run between registration and the later redefineClasses0/retransformClasses0 call which does `ctx.invoke_virtual(entry.transformer_ref, "transform", ...)` (instrument.rs:840). A transformer reachable only through this chain can be reclaimed (use-after-free on invoke_virtual) or left pointing at a stale from-space address under a moving/compacting GC (the codebase ships moving GCs: evacuation, selective-promote).  
Fix: Add an instrument::collect_transformer_roots(&mut Vec<ObjectRef>) that pushes every entry.transformer_ref, and call it from memory/roots.rs::collect_roots alongside the JNI global/local roots (section 9). Add an instrument::update_transformers_after_gc(pointer_map) that rewrites each transformer_ref via the forwarding map, called from memory/gc.rs next to jni_global_refs.update_after_gc.

**[HIGH] `vm-jni-jvmti` - No implicit JNI local-ref frame around native calls; local refs created without an explicit PushLocalFrame are not GC roots**  
`vm/src/native/jni.rs:658-668,698-712; vm/src/vm/vm_exec.rs:11406-11456` - bug  
track_local_ref() only records a handle when JNI_LOCAL_FRAMES has a top frame (`stack.last_mut()`); with no active frame the ref is silently dropped (comment: 'untracked'). The native dispatch path in vm_exec.rs (set_jni_context/set_jni_thread ... dispatch_jni_native ... clear) never pushes an implicit top-level frame on native entry. Therefore a local ref a native method obtains via NewObject/NewStringUTF/FindClass/GetObjectField when it did NOT call PushLocalFrame is invisible to collect_local_ref_roots, so a GC (concurrent thread, or one triggered by an allocating JNI op in the same native call) can reclaim the object or, under a moving GC, leave the native code holding a stale handle. This defeats the very 'vm-jni-roots #1' fix, which only covers refs inside an explicit frame.  
Fix: Push an implicit local frame in the JNI entry sequence (right after set_jni_context in vm_exec.rs ~line 11407) and pop it in the clear sequence (~11455). Make track_local_ref push to a synthesized top frame when the stack is empty (the implicit frame), so every local ref handed to native code is rooted for the call duration.

**[HIGH] `vm-jni-jvmti` - GetPrimitiveArrayCritical / Get<Type>ArrayElements hand out a direct heap pointer with no GC pin under a moving GC**  
`vm/src/native/jni.rs:2361-2429,3091-3161,3215-3229` - bug  
The fast paths return `shared.heap.array_data_ptr(oref)` directly with is_copy=JNI_FALSE, justified by an inline comment 'our heap doesn't move objects between GC'. That assumption is false for this VM, which ships moving/compacting collectors (evacuation + selective-promote per project memory; GetStringCritical even delegates to GetStringChars on the same premise). Nothing records the array as pinned/non-movable for the duration of the critical section, and there is no entry in JNI_ARRAY_ELEM_BUFFERS for the direct path. If a moving GC relocates the array while native code holds the pointer (the GetXXXCritical contract explicitly permits long-held pointers), the native reads/writes hit freed/relocated memory: UAF / heap corruption.  
Fix: Either (a) pin the backing array (mark its region non-movable / add to a pinned set consulted by the moving collector) for the lifetime of every direct GetPrimitiveArrayCritical/GetStringCritical/no-copy GetArrayElements pointer and unpin on Release, or (b) force the copy path (is_copy=JNI_TRUE, register in JNI_ARRAY_ELEM_BUFFERS) whenever a moving GC is the active collector. Do not gate correctness on a heap-never-moves invariant the VM does not hold.

**[HIGH] `vm-jit-integ` - jit_anewarray_object / jit_multianewarray_2d call non-fallible heap.alloc_array — panic across extern "C" boundary (UB) and process abort on large length / OOM**  
`vm/src/jit/helpers.rs:1550 (jit_anewarray_object), 1890-1892 (jit_multianewarray_2d)` - bug  
Both helpers finish allocation with the non-fallible gc::heap::alloc_array (gc/src/heap.rs:376), which `assert!(length <= MAX_ARRAY_LENGTH)`, `.expect()`s on size overflow, and aborts via alloc_zeroed on genuine OOM. jit_newarray (line 1178-1217) was specifically hardened to use the fallible try_alloc_array + jit_newarray_oom path for exactly this reason, but these two siblings were not. For multianewarray, dim2 is only checked for <0; a valid int like 1_500_000_000 passes the negativity check yet exceeds MAX_ARRAY_LENGTH → assert! panic. Crucially the workspace builds with panic="unwind" (Cargo.toml:183), so a panic raised inside an `extern "C"` JIT helper unwinds into JIT-generated machine-code frames that have no unwind tables — that is undefined behavior (best case immediate abort). Reachable directly from JIT-compiled `anewarray`/`multianewarray` bytecode with attacker-influenced array sizes → DoS / UB. Note multianewarray also allocates dim1 inner arrays in a loop with no per-iteration GC retry or OOM path.  
Fix: Mirror jit_newarray: range/overflow-guard via array_data_size(...), use heap.try_alloc_array / alloc_array_checked, and on None return the jit_newarray_oom(...) catchable-OOME sentinel. For multianewarray, also throw NegativeArraySizeException for the negative-dim path (it currently returns 0 silently) and handle inner-loop allocation failure. Never allow a panic to cross the extern "C" boundary — wrap fallible allocation so the helper always returns a sentinel.

**[HIGH] `vm-runtime-misc` - GPU zero-copy direct-xfer path skips element-type/kind checks -> OOB read + heap corruption**  
`vm/src/runtime/gpu_marshal.rs:583-628 (macro direct_xfer!, instantiated 633-664)` - vulnerability  
The direct_xfer! zero-copy path (default ON; opt-out only via CRATONVM_GPU_NO_ZEROCOPY) reads only header.array_length then does std::slice::from_raw_parts(src as *const $ty, len) / from_raw_parts_mut(dst as *mut $ty, len) where src/dst = heap.array_data_ptr(obj). Unlike the staged fallback host_view_*/write_back_* (lines 63-245 / 308-471) it never asserts header.kind==Array nor header.element_type==$ty. array_data_ptr (gc/src/vm_heap.rs:665) does NOT validate type: it returns obj.as_ptr()+HEADER_SIZE for ANY object (only refusing G1-humongous). So passing e.g. a byte[N] to upload_obj_i32/download_obj_i32 reinterprets N*sizeof($ty) bytes: upload reads 4-8x past the real payload (heap info leak to device); download WRITES that many bytes (out-of-bounds heap corruption). Today the only caller dispatches by element_type (offload.rs:2720 resident_arm! match) so the right fn is usually chosen, but the fns are pub, the safety invariant is unchecked/undocumented, and any future or mis-detecting caller corrupts the heap.  
Fix: Before taking the zero-copy branch in both $up and $down arms, fetch the header once and assert header.kind==ObjectKind::Array && header.element_type matches $ty (thread the expected ArrayElementType through the macro). On mismatch, fall through to the staged path (which already asserts) rather than reinterpreting raw bytes. Also have array_data_ptr return None for non-arrays.

**[HIGH] `vm-misc` - ObjectStreamClass cache (osc_cache) holds heap ObjectRefs that are neither GC-rooted nor remapped after a moving GC**  
`vm/src/runtime/serialization/oscache.rs:57-106; vm/src/memory/roots.rs:48-405; native-builtins/src/serialization.rs:3084` - bug  
OscCache stores java.io.ObjectStreamClass descriptor ObjectRefs keyed by ClassId (insert_if_absent / get_or_insert_with). The descriptor (`desc`) created in build_object_stream_class (serialization.rs:3084) is a heap object whose only persistent reference, once the caller of ObjectStreamClass.lookup(cls) drops its result, is the cache entry. collect_roots() in roots.rs does NOT scan shared.osc_cache (sections 15-20 explicitly scan every OTHER process-global ObjectRef side-table — Integer cache, lambda callsites, collection overlays, loader singletons, locale, MSC, logmanager, annotation proxies, httpserver handlers — precisely because the default no-JIT path runs a MOVING young-gen GC), and there is no gc_update_osc_cache_refs remap companion in gc.rs. Under the moving collector a cached descriptor can be relocated (stale pointer) or reclaimed; the next lookup(cls) returns a dangling ObjectRef -> use-after-free / zero-header receiver SIGSEGV. This is the exact bug-class documented repeatedly in the project's GC-safety history (stale-pointer SIGSEGV on cached singletons). The module's own doc-comment asserts entries 'stay reachable from Java-side roots... GC will collect it at next cycle' — that reasoning is incorrect once the result is discarded by the app; serialization-heavy apps (the docs name Keycloak calling lookup thousands of times) are the trigger.  
Fix: Add osc_cache to the GC integration exactly like the other side-tables: (1) a scan in collect_roots that pushes every cached ObjectRef as a root (mark), and (2) a remap in gc.rs after a moving collection that rewrites each cached value through the pointer_map (relocation). Mirror the pattern of gc_scan_annotation_proxy_roots / gc_update_annotation_proxy_refs. Add a multi-GC-cycle regression test that looks up an OSC, drops the Java handle, forces several moving collections, and asserts identity + non-dangling on re-lookup.

**[HIGH] `libcratonvm` - Caller-supplied u64 handles are reconstructed into raw heap pointers and dereferenced with zero validation (UAF / OOB read)**  
`libcratonvm/src/lib.rs:612-619 (ref_from_handle), consumed at 1117,1178,1257,1309,1324,1425,1475,1528 and types/src/value.rs:116` - vulnerability  
Every flat-API entry that takes a `CratonRef`/`CratonClass` (cratonvm_invoke_virtual, cratonvm_object_class, cratonvm_field_count, cratonvm_get_field, cratonvm_set_field, the *_by_name variants) converts the raw u64 directly into an `ObjectRef` via `ObjectRef::from_raw(h as *mut u8)` and then dereferences it through `heap.class_id_of` / `heap.get_field` / `set_instance_field`. `ObjectRef::from_raw` performs NO validation in release builds (null/alignment are `debug_assert!`-only, types/src/value.rs:100-128). Unlike a real JNI local-ref table (which indirects through a per-thread handle table that can be range/liveness-checked), a CratonRef is the bare object address. Consequences: (a) any garbage or stale u64 from C yields an arbitrary-address read/write — a memory-safety hole reachable purely from the public API; (b) under a MOVING GC any handle that outlives a collection becomes a dangling pointer (the doc at lib.rs:609-611 even concedes 'the VM does not move objects out from under a handed-out handle within a single call sequence' — i.e. it WILL across call sequences). catch_unwind does not protect against a SIGSEGV/silent corruption from a bad dereference.  
Fix: Do not hand out raw object addresses as the public handle. Introduce a per-VM (or per-thread) handle table mapping opaque tokens -> ObjectRef with a generation/liveness check, mark those slots as GC roots, and validate every inbound handle against the table before deref (return JNI_ERR/ERROR on miss). At minimum, until that lands, document loudly that handles are invalidated by any GC and must not cross a call that can allocate, and add a cheap heap-bounds sanity check in ref_from_handle to reject out-of-arena addresses.

**[HIGH] `docs-governance` - Workspace crate count wrong everywhere: docs say 17+fuzz=18, Cargo.toml declares 19 members and excludes fuzz**  
`Cargo.toml:2-6 vs README.md:219, ARCHITECTURE.md:8, ARCHITECTURE.md:63, BUILD_GUIDE.md:94, CHANGELOG.md:54-56` - spec-compliance  
Cargo.toml [workspace].members lists 19 crates: reader, types, native-api, native-collections, native-io, native-builtins, native-awt, jit-api, jit, jit-cuda, cuda-bridge, classloading, craton-gpu, gc, vm, vm-cli, jfr, libcratonvm, cratonvm-embed. README, ARCHITECTURE (x2), and BUILD_GUIDE all state '17 member crates plus a fuzz harness (18 Cargo.toml files in total).' Two members (libcratonvm, cratonvm-embed) are entirely undocumented (absent from every crate table and the ARCHITECTURE layout/dependency graph). Worse, Cargo.toml:3-6 and fuzz/Cargo.toml:7-18 state fuzz is INTENTIONALLY NOT a workspace member (it is its own standalone workspace), yet CHANGELOG.md:55 claims 'fuzz/ is now a workspace member' and CHANGELOG.md:54 says 'all 18 workspace members, incl. fuzz.' The count, the membership of fuzz, and the omission of two real crates are all inconsistent with the manifest.  
Fix: Recount against Cargo.toml: 19 workspace members; fuzz is a separate (non-member) standalone workspace. Update the count and crate tables in README.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, BUILD_GUIDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, and the prose in ARCHITECTURE.md:63. Add libcratonvm and cratonvm-embed to the crate-layout tables and dependency graph (or explain why they're omitted). Fix CHANGELOG.md:54-56 to stop describing fuzz as a workspace member.

**[HIGH] `docs-technical` - CRYPTO_STATUS.md marks PBKDF2 as 'Stub-throws' but it is implemented natively**  
`docs/CRYPTO_STATUS.md:163-166` - spec-compliance  
The Key Derivation table says PBKDF2WithHmacSHA1/256/512 are 'Stub-throws' (NoSuchAlgorithmException). The code implements real native PBKDF2: native-builtins/src/jca/cipher.rs:1175-1199 registers SecretKeyFactory.getInstance/generateSecret -> crate::phases_early::pbkdf2_get_instance / pbkdf2_generate_secret. The TestPEMFile encrypted-key path depends on this. The doc tells users a present capability is absent.  
Fix: Reclassify PBKDF2WithHmacSHA1/256/512 to 'Implemented' (native PBKDF2 via phases_early::pbkdf2_*, routed through jca/cipher.rs SecretKeyFactory). Keep HKDF/Scrypt/Argon2 accurate.

**[HIGH] `docs-technical` - CRYPTO_STATUS.md marks ML-KEM as 'Stub-throws' but the real KEM SPI is wired**  
`docs/CRYPTO_STATUS.md:169-179` - spec-compliance  
The Post-Quantum table marks ML-KEM-512/768/1024 (and ML-DSA) as 'Stub-throws'. But native-builtins/src/jca/kem.rs wires javax.crypto.KEM encaps/decaps to the real SunJCE com/sun/crypto/provider/ML_KEM_Impls$K2/K3/K5 SPI (kem.rs:130-139, kem_new_encapsulator_*/kem_new_decapsulator_* at lines 321-413). ML-KEM encapsulate/decapsulate is functional, not a stub.  
Fix: Update ML-KEM rows to reflect that javax.crypto.KEM encaps/decaps is backed by the real ML_KEM_Impls SPI. Verify ML-DSA / KeyPairGenerator status separately and state it precisely rather than blanket 'Stub-throws'.

**[HIGH] `scripts-ci` - Parked jck.yml builds wrong cargo target / nonexistent binary**  
`.github/.wf/jck.yml:96,106` - bug  
Build step runs `cargo build --release -p vm-cli` and sets CRATONVM_BIN=target/release/vm-cli. The package is cratonvm-cli (vm-cli is only the directory name), and the produced binary is `cratonvm`. So -p vm-cli fails and target/release/vm-cli never exists. release.yml gets this right.  
Fix: Change to `cargo build --release -p cratonvm-cli` and CRATONVM_BIN=$(pwd)/target/release/cratonvm before this workflow is promoted.

**[HIGH] `oss-crates-readiness` - 595 compiled .class files and ~383 bench/ files are git-tracked despite being .gitignored**  
`.gitignore + git ls-files (bench/, **/*.class)` - quality  
`bench/` is listed in .gitignore but 383 files under it are already committed (gitignore does not untrack committed paths). 595 *.class bytecode files are tracked across bench/, bench-tornado/, and test fixtures. These are opaque binaries that bloat the repo and ship in clones. Note: the per-crate `exclude` rules in classloading/vm/vm-cli/Cargo.toml DO keep *.class out of `cargo package`, so they don't bloat published crates — but they do bloat the GitHub repo. The .git directory is 848MB.  
Fix: `git rm -r --cached bench/ **/*.class dd1.out test-infra/` (keep needed .java/.sh bench sources by re-adding selectively, or move benches to a separate repo). Decide intentionally whether docs/ (513 tracked files, also gitignored) should be tracked — the contradiction between .gitignore listing `docs` and 513 tracked docs files should be reconciled. Consider a history rewrite (git filter-repo) before going public to shed the 848MB history if it contains large removed blobs.

**[HIGH] `oss-crates-readiness` - dd1.out junk artifact is tracked at repo root**  
`dd1.out` - quality  
A 124-byte stray developer probe-output file `dd1.out` is git-tracked at the repo root. It is not referenced by build or tests and looks like an accidental commit.  
Fix: `git rm dd1.out` and add `*.out` (already partially covered by build-*.out) or an explicit `/dd1.out` to .gitignore.

### 1.2 Medium findings

| Module | Cat | Finding | Location | Fix |
|--------|-----|---------|----------|-----|
| `types` | quality | ObjectRef Send+Sync is sound only by accident of the single-threaded scheduler | `types/src/value.rs:207-208 (unsafe impl Send/Sync), rationale :160-206` | Before any genuinely preemptive multi-OS-thread Java execution path is enabled, re-derive the Send/Sync claim from first principles or replace ObjectRef with a handle indirection. Add a runtime/debug tripwire that asserts the single-OS-thread invariant the impls currently rely on, so a future threading change fails loudly rather than silently becoming unsound. |
| `native-collections` | bug | TreeMap/TreeSet array-mode side-tables are scanned but never pruned, even if prune were wired | `native-collections/src/lib.rs:21752` | Add tm_array_table, tm_fast_table, and ts_array_table to the dead-key removal sweep in gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays (they are keyed by the same packed widened_obj_key via tm_obj_key). Add an assertion in the gc_relocation_harness that these tables shrink after a dead-object prune. |
| `native-collections` | bug | widened_obj_key recycled-identity disambiguation can alias a new collection onto a dead collection's stale overlay state | `native-collections/src/lib.rs:168` | Do not silently inherit a slot on a pointer change without a positive liveness signal. Either (a) wire the prune so dead slots cannot linger, and/or (b) when last_ptr changed and the old address is known-dead (or unverifiable), allocate a fresh generation rather than rebinding. Add a test: create overlay-backed collection A, simulate A's death (don't prune), allocate B with A's identity hash + same class, assert B's overlay is EMPTY (not A's contents). |
| `native-collections` | bug | ArrayList silently drops elements past 1<<30 capacity and corrupts size → later get() returns uninitialized slot | `native-collections/src/lib.rs:1220` | When the requested capacity exceeds the cap, throw OutOfMemoryError (matching JDK) rather than returning the old buffer, OR at minimum do not increment size when the backing array could not accommodate the new element. Raise the cap toward Integer.MAX_VALUE to match JDK semantics. Add a test asserting add past the cap either throws or leaves size unchanged with no phantom slot. |
| `native-io-core` | vulnerability | Default policy does not resolve hostnames; alias hostnames reach metadata/private IPs when the embedder relies on the textual default | `native-io/src/outbound_policy.rs:136-143,348-401` | Document explicitly in the crate/module README that hosted/multi-tenant deployments MUST set CRATONVM_BLOCK_PRIVATE_NETS (or install a resolution-aware set_policy) in addition to the always-on metadata block, and fix the IPv4-mapped gap so the always-on block is actually exhaustive for link-local. |
| `native-io-channels` | vulnerability | DirectByteBuffer pool deallocates with a Layout whose size differs from the allocation Layout (GlobalAlloc UB) | `native-io/src/direct_buffer.rs:173-214 (pool_take/pool_put), 286-307 (dbb_free), 247-268 (dbb_allocate pool-hit arm)` | Round every allocation size up to its bucket's canonical size before alloc, and carry the *stored* entry.size (returned by pool_take) all the way to dealloc so alloc/dealloc layouts always match. Simplest fix: in dbb_allocate's pool-hit arm, record and use the block's actual size for the live buffer (and in dbb_free recover that exact size), rather than the caller-requested size. |
| `nb-phases-late` | vulnerability | Negative/huge length in write([BII)V and Mac.update([BII)V causes Vec::with_capacity allocation abort (DoS) | `native-builtins/src/phases_late.rs:12864-12866 (ZipOutputStream.write), 30766-30767 (Mac.update)` | Validate off/len against the source array length and reject negatives before allocating: read `let len = args.get(3).and_then(|v| v.as_int()).filter(|&n| n >= 0).unwrap_or(0) as usize;` and clamp/throw IndexOutOfBoundsException when off+len exceeds array_length. Or avoid pre-sizing: build the Vec without with_capacity and bound the loop by the array length. Audit the other with_capacity(len) sites at 12894, 13140, 13268, 13315 for the same issue. |
| `nb-lib` | bug | makeClassValueMap returns an uninitialized HashMap (<init> invoked with no receiver) | `native-builtins/src/shared_secrets_bridge.rs:667-675 (jlia_make_class_value_map)` | Replace both calls with a single ctx.new_object_initialized("java/util/HashMap", "()V", &[]) which returns the constructed, GC-pinned object. |
| `nb-lib` | bug | parseCookie fallback returns an uninitialized ArrayList (same no-receiver <init> pattern) | `native-builtins/src/shared_secrets_bridge.rs:1554-1555 (jnhc_parse_cookie else branch)` | Use ctx.new_object_initialized("java/util/ArrayList", "()V", &[]). Grep the crate for the invoke(<init>,&[]) + new_object pair generally; it is a recurring footgun. |
| `nb-lib` | bug | BigInteger.modPow strips the sign of base AND exponent, silently producing wrong results | `native-builtins/src/lib.rs:29452-29479 (registered native java/math/BigInteger.modPow)` | Do not strip the exponent sign. For negative exponent, compute base^{-1} mod m (via modInverse, throwing ArithmeticException when gcd!=1) then exponentiate by |exp|. Preserve base-sign handling so the result is the canonical non-negative residue. Differential-test against HotSpot for negative base/exp. |
| `nb-lib` | bug | BigInteger.modInverse returns 1 as a silent fallback for large operands and never throws on non-coprime input | `native-builtins/src/lib.rs:29482-29514 (registered native java/math/BigInteger.modInverse)` | Use the arbitrary-precision string extended-Euclidean already present in this file (bi_mod_inverse-style helpers near :28678) instead of i128; throw ArithmeticException('BigInteger not invertible') when gcd!=1. Add a KAT against HotSpot for 2048-bit operands. |
| `nb-lang` | spec-compliance | StrictMath delegates to platform libm, violating bit-reproducibility contract | `native-builtins/src/lib.rs:5389-5390 (register_math_natives for both "java/lang/Math" and "java/lang/StrictMath"); implementations native_math_sin/cos/tan/pow/exp/log etc. in lang_math.rs:1334-1460` | Provide a dedicated fdlibm-compatible implementation (or vendor a portable fdlibm port) for the StrictMath registrations; keep platform libm only for the relaxed java/lang/Math entries. Document the StrictMath divergence in readiness notes if a real fdlibm is not yet wired. |
| `nb-lang` | bug | AbstractStringBuilder.getChars silently no-ops on invalid destination bounds instead of throwing IndexOutOfBoundsException | `native-builtins/src/lang_string.rs:1829-1869 (native_sb_get_chars)` | Before the copy, validate `dst_begin >= 0` and `dst_begin + (src_end - src_begin) <= dst.length`, throwing StringIndexOutOfBoundsException/IndexOutOfBoundsException to match the spec. Do not ignore the Result of set_array_element — propagate it as the AIOOBE. |
| `nb-lang` | spec-compliance | MethodHandles.Lookup find* performs no access-control enforcement | `native-builtins/src/classloader.rs:3570-3699 (lk_find_virtual/find_static/find_constructor/find_getter/find_setter/find_static_getter/find_static_setter/find_special)` | Resolve the target member, read its access flags, and enforce them against the Lookup's allowedModes (the helpers read_lookup_allowed_modes already exist at classloader.rs:510 and lang_invoke.rs:2174), throwing IllegalAccessException on a violation. At minimum document the non-enforcement as a known deviation. |
| `nb-crypto-security` | vulnerability | Per-instance SecureRandom DRBG state is keyed on identity_hash_code, so distinct instances can collide and share/replay a stream | `native-builtins/src/crypto_impl.rs:1059 (SECURE_RANDOM_STATE map<i32,(u64,u64)>), keying at :1165, :1192, :1215, :1238, :1259` | Once #1 is fixed for unseeded instances this matters only for seeded/deterministic instances; key the state on a GC-stable unique object id rather than a 32-bit identity hash, or store DRBG state in a hidden object field. Add eviction or a weak map to bound growth. |
| `nb-crypto-security` | spec-compliance | X509TrustManager chain validation performs no hostname/endpoint-identity check and does not enforce KeyUsage keyCertSign / leaf EKU | `native-builtins/src/x509_manager.rs:857 (validate_chain) and tls.rs:1629 (validate_cert_chain); KeyUsage/EKU only consulted for alias selection per module doc x509_manager.rs:39-46` | Wire endpoint identification (SAN/CN matching) when an SSLParameters endpointIdentificationAlgorithm is set, and enforce keyCertSign in KeyUsage on every CA cert in the chain plus leaf EKU. Complete RFC 5280 4.2.1.10 name constraints. Document explicitly that hostname checking is the SSL-layer/HostnameVerifier responsibility for the bare TrustManager path. |
| `nb-net` | vulnerability | HTTP client replays all request headers (incl. Authorization/Cookie) verbatim on cross-host redirects | `native-builtins/src/http_client.rs:1098-1148 (perform_request redirect loop)` | When following a redirect whose resolved host (and/or scheme/port) differs from the current request's, filter out Authorization, Proxy-Authorization, and Cookie headers before the next hop (match JDK behavior). Add a test asserting Authorization is dropped on a cross-host Location. |
| `nb-net` | vulnerability | Unbounded buffering of chunk size-line and trailers in HTTP/1.1 client chunked reader (DoS) | `native-builtins/src/http_client.rs:558-571 (size-line loop) and 588-597 (trailer loop)` | Cap `prefix` length in both loops (e.g. reject if it exceeds a few KiB for a size line / a bounded trailer budget) and return an error when exceeded, mirroring the existing MAX_RESPONSE_BODY discipline. |

| `nb-serial-concurrency` | vulnerability | TC_ARRAY deserialization lacks filter-independent length cap → default-reachable allocation DoS | `native-builtins/src/serialization.rs:2121-2132` | Mirror the TC_LONGSTRING discipline in ois_read_array: clamp `length` to bytes-remaining (a primitive array can't have more elements than remaining bytes; a reference array needs >=1 tag byte/element) via ois_buf_remaining, and add a filter-independent hard cap constant (e.g. MAX_SERIAL_ARRAY_LEN) checked before new_array. | `nb-frameworks` | vulnerability | AOT cache 'integrity hash' is a keyless non-cryptographic mix — forgeable; latent code-injection vector if compiled_code is ever executed | `native-builtins/src/aot.rs:259` | Treat the AOT cache as trusted input only (document it) AND replace the integrity hash with a keyed MAC (e.g. HMAC-SHA256 with a per-VM key) or a real cryptographic digest before any path executes compiled_code. For cglib_enhancer, validate the candidate pointer against the live heap (ctx-provided is-valid-object check) before constructing an ObjectRef, or thread the real object through rather than a jlong; never trust an alignment check as proof of object validity. |

| `jit-x64` | stub | IR-path deopt frame reconstruction is dormant skeleton, not wired to live interpreter dispatch | `jit/src/deopt.rs:883-920` | Document deopt.rs as experimental/not-on-live-path in module readiness notes. | `jit-ir` | bug | aarch64 branch encoders and patchers silently truncate out-of-range offsets (no range check) → wrong-target branch / control-flow corruption | `jit/src/aarch64.rs:790,799,835,845,855,865,883,895,963,980,1015` | In each encoder and patch helper, compute the signed offset and verify it fits the field (e.g. `(-(1<<20)..(1<<20)).contains(&delta)` for imm19, `(-(1<<15)..(1<<15))` for imm14, `(-(1<<27)..(1<<27))` for imm26) BEFORE masking; on overflow, signal failure (return Option/Result or set the emitter's overflow flag) so codegen bails to the interpreter — mirroring the x86-64 rel8/rel32 DisplacementOverflow handling. Add differential/range tests that force a >32KB tbz and >1MB b.cond. |
| `jit-ir` | performance | Unbounded JIT code-cache growth: compiled code is intentionally leaked for process lifetime (no code-cache sweeper) | `jit/src/lib.rs:564-599` | This is the correct safe default for now, but it is a readiness gap, not a non-issue. Implement the documented long-term fix: a code-cache sweeper that, at a safepoint, finds and patches/invalidates inbound direct-call and IC slots before reclaiming a region (the JitCodeRegion list already gives the address ranges). Track and surface RETAINED_JIT_CODE_BYTES as a metric and consider a high-water cap that disables further compilation rather than OOMing. |
| `gpu` | bug | PTX module name hardcoded to "module" — second DeviceModule clobbers the first in cudarc's per-device module map | `cuda-bridge/src/lib.rs:274 and 278 (also backend_cuda.rs:300-326)` | Generate a unique module name per from_ptx call (e.g. a process-wide AtomicU64 counter formatted as "cratonvm_mod_{n}", or a hash of the PTX text), and thread it through to load_ptx/get_func. Keep the unique name in DeviceModuleInner.module_name so lookups stay consistent. |
| `gpu` | bug | is_reduction is detected by opcode presence, not dataflow — can emit atom.add of the wrong value | `jit-cuda/src/analyzer.rs:494-495 (shape probe) and jit-cuda/src/lowering/emit.rs:1468-1521 (scalar_return)` | Tighten the reduction recognition to a dataflow check: confirm the returned value's register traces back to the loop-carried accumulator that the recognized *add writes (the emitter already simulates the operand stack, so it can assert the popped return register is the accumulation result). If the dataflow does not match the canonical sum/dot pattern, reject with CountedLoopScalarReturn rather than emitting an atomic add. |
| `gpu` | quality | All jit-cuda unit tests are un-runnable on a clean checkout — fixture sources (test_classes/gpu/*.java) are absent and untracked | `jit-cuda/src/test_support.rs:30-76, jit-cuda/build.rs:44-51, repo root (no test_classes/ dir)` | Commit the fixture .java sources (and/or pre-compiled .class files) under test_classes/gpu/ so the tests are hermetic, or gate the fixture-dependent tests behind a clearly-documented feature/env flag and add a CI job that provisions the fixtures. At minimum document the external dependency in the crate README so reviewers know the suite is not self-contained. |
| `classloading` | spec-compliance | ldc of a category-2 Dynamic constant corrupts the verifier stack model (missing Top, no rejection) | `classloading/src/verify_insn.rs:1462-1473 (verify_ldc)` | In verify_ldc, reject a Dynamic (and any) constant whose resolved type is category-2 with a VerifyError ('ldc cannot load long/double; use ldc2_w'). Conversely ensure verify_ldc2w accepts only Long/Double. If category-2 dynamics ever need support, push via push_typed (which appends Top) — but the spec-correct action for ldc is rejection. |
| `classloading` | spec-compliance | invokespecial <init> does not verify the constructor owner matches the class named by `new` at the uninitialized offset | `classloading/src/verify_insn.rs:998-1013` | Track the class named by each `new` (keyed by bytecode offset) during verification and, on invokespecial <init> over Uninitialized(offset), assert the resolved constructor owner equals the `new` site's class name; otherwise raise VerifyError. |
| `gc-collectors` | bug | Non-atomic 16-byte Value reads during concurrent marking are a data race (UB) on slots a mutator may write | `gc/src/concurrent_mark.rs:667; gc/src/g1.rs:1289,1858,3542` | For any path that marks concurrently with mutators, read reference slots through an atomic-sized load (read the 8-byte reference word atomically rather than the whole 16-byte Value), or document that concurrent_mark.rs's ConcurrentMarker is STW-only and assert it. Align G1 and the standalone marker on one barrier/locking discipline. |
| `gc-collectors` | spec-compliance | ZgcRealHeap collect_garbage performs no weak/soft/phantom reference processing | `gc/src/zgc.rs:1744-1828` | Wire ReferenceProcessor::process_references into ZgcRealHeap::collect_garbage (after marking, before sweep) using the mark bitmap as the is_marked predicate, mirroring the G1/gen_heap integration; or explicitly document ZgcRealHeap as not supporting reference processing and gate it out of real-app modes. |
| `gc-collectors` | spec-compliance | Soft/weak reference clearing ignores liveness kept alive only through surviving soft referents (transitive closure gap) | `gc/src/reference.rs:369-420, 478-577` | After soft-ref processing, re-trace from surviving (uncleared) soft referents and fold that closure into the liveness predicate used for weak-ref clearing, matching HotSpot's phased keep-alive. Failing that, require callers to pass trace_from and route all production callers through the finalizer-trace entry point. |
| `vm-core` | vulnerability | copy_to_native_memory / copy_from_native_memory do a raw copy_nonoverlapping with only an `addr <= 0` guard | `vm/src/vm/vm_exec.rs:2169-2227 (unsafe copies at 2184 and 2224)` | Document the trust boundary explicitly (only trusted bytecode may reach Unsafe.copyMemory) and, if untrusted code is ever in scope, add region validation: reject addresses that fall inside the managed heap (heap.is_heap_addr) for the raw path, as the off-heap arena already does. At minimum keep the heap-overlap check non-gated for the write path. |
| `vm-core` | quality | Box::from_raw join-handle reconstruction has no double-consume guard — a duplicated raw pointer would double-free | `vm/src/vm/vm_exec.rs:4128-4130 (register_native_thread) and 4167-4169 (attach_join_handle_to_native_thread)` | Make the contract enforceable: have set_join_handle return/replace and detect an existing handle for the tid, or thread the ownership through a typed wrapper rather than a bare usize. At minimum, assert in attach_ that the registry slot has no handle yet before consuming the pointer. |
| `vm-interpreter` | bug | GC root scan/update asymmetry: smuggled jobject in a Long-tagged slot goes stale across moving GC | `vm/src/runtime/value_stack.rs:1222-1266 vs 1319-1343` | Make update_object_refs symmetric with scan_object_refs: add a CompactTag::Long arm that applies pointer_map (gated on the same heap.is_heap_addr check) so a rooted Long-slot smuggle is forwarded. Alternatively forbid rooting Long-tagged slots entirely (require the smuggle path to use a typed pin) so scan and update stay consistent. |
| `vm-interpreter` | spec-compliance | JIT-unknown-PC handler search skips catch-all/finally entries → finally blocks may not run | `vm/src/runtime/interpreter.rs:7083-7121 (find_exception_handler_pc_unknown) and route_jit_exception_through_method` | Have the JIT record the throw-site bytecode PC in JIT_PENDING_EXCEPTION so find_exception_handler_impl (the PC-aware path) can be used, enabling correct finally/catch-all matching. Until then, document that JIT'd methods with finally blocks and an unrecoverable throw PC may skip cleanup, and consider conservatively running catch-all entries whose region covers the whole method. |
| `vm-exec-init` | spec-compliance | java.lang.Class mirror is populated by hardcoded JDK-25 field slot indices | `vm/src/vm/vm_object.rs:537-600 (get_or_create_class_mirror), CLASS_MIRROR_NUM_FIELDS` | Resolve the field slot for each populated field by NAME via the loaded java.lang.Class (the same first_field_index + index walk used at vm_util.rs:1332-1349 for detailMessage) and cache the resolved indices, instead of hardcoding slot numbers. At minimum add a startup assertion that the resolved name/modifiers/primitive slots match the constants, so a JDK upgrade fails loudly rather than corrupting reflection. |
| `vm-jni-jvmti` | stub | DefineClass ignores the supplied bytecode buffer (loads from classpath by name instead) | `vm/src/native/jni.rs:2762-2782` | Route buf[..len] through the class_manager define_class_with_options path (the same one instrument.rs uses for redefine) so the supplied bytes are actually defined; or, until then, return 0 AND raise an UnsatisfiedLinkError/NoClassDefFoundError rather than silently substituting a classpath class. |
| `vm-jni-jvmti` | stub | All C-varargs JNI call slots are stubs (CallObjectMethod, CallStatic*Method, NewObject, ...) returning 0/null | `vm/src/native/jni.rs:5025-5037,4986-4987,5037` | Implement the varargs slots via the same naked-asm trampoline approach already used for the dispatch path (read the platform va_list / register-save area), or at minimum have the varargs stub raise UnsatisfiedLinkError so a native that calls them fails loudly instead of getting a fabricated 0. Document the limitation in readiness notes. |
| `vm-threading` | bug | AtomicOperations::get_and_{set,add,or,and,xor}_{int,long} operate on a throwaway stack-local atomic and never publish to the heap — not gated like their CAS siblings | `vm/src/threading/varhandle.rs:484-511 (i32) and 568-594 (i64)` | Apply the same treatment as the CAS helpers: mark all get_and_* read-modify-write helpers #[cfg(test)] + pub(crate) + #[deprecated] with the 'never publishes' note, and add `assert!(cfg!(test), ...)` runtime guards. Alternatively delete them outright since the real path is NativeContext / the Unsafe.getAndAdd natives. Either way they must not remain callable from production with an atomic-looking signature. |
| `vm-threading` | performance | schedule_wakeup spawns a brand-new OS thread per Thread.sleep() on a virtual thread, defeating the purpose of virtual threads | `vm/src/threading/virtual_threads.rs:817-859` | Replace per-sleep OS-thread spawning with a single process-wide timer wheel / min-heap of (deadline, vt_id) driven by one dedicated timer thread that park_timeouts on the queue head and resubmits expired VTs; make cancellation a flag flip instead of Condvar::notify_one. This is the fix the TODO already specifies. |
| `vm-jit-integ` | stub | runtime/gc_integration.rs is an unused parallel GC model with a fake address allocator — not wired to the real GC | `vm/src/runtime/gc_integration.rs:1-647 (esp. next_heap_addr:155, AllocationPath:159, SafepointManager, GcRootScanner, WriteBarrier, Tlab)` | Either delete gc_integration.rs (and the unused jit_integration.rs models below) or move both under a clearly-named `#[cfg(test)] mod sim` / examples module and document that they are non-production simulations. At minimum add a module-level doc banner stating they are unused models and add a `#[deprecated]`/`#[allow(dead_code)]` marker so accidental wiring is caught. |
| `vm-runtime-misc` | bug | write_all EINTR/partial-write retry in the SIGSEGV handler is incorrect | `vm/src/runtime/crash_handler.rs:1187-1208` | Loop on write while r<0 && errno==EINTR (read errno via libc::__errno_location, which is async-signal-safe in practice on glibc/musl); treat EAGAIN by retrying too (the fds are blocking here). Only bail on a hard error. Keep it allocation-free. |
| `vm-runtime-misc` | quality | Lock-order enforcement compiled out in release builds | `vm/src/runtime/lock_order.rs:264-294 (and Drop at 332-336)` | Offer an opt-in runtime-enabled mode (env flag, e.g. CRATONVM_LOCK_ORDER_CHECK=1) that keeps the tracking in release builds for soak/CI runs, or at minimum gate it on a cheap AtomicBool so the deadlock-hunt builds can enable it without a debug recompile. |
| `jfr` | bug | Wedged-consumer Drop frees slot array while a parked consumer may still dereference it (residual UAF window) | `jfr/src/repository.rs:838-849` | Do not free the slot storage while a wedged consumer can still touch it. Either (a) leak the entire Box (mem::forget the slots) on the wedged path rather than freeing it — trading a bounded one-ring memory leak for soundness — or (b) keep the ring's backing storage alive via the Arc the consumer already holds (move the drain-on-drop logic out of SpscEventRing::drop and into the registry's last-Arc-drop, so storage is freed only when no consumer reference remains). Option (a) is the minimal fix. |

Document that destroy must run on the creating thread. |
