### 1.3 Per-module summary, stubs, performance & strengths

#### `reader`

The reader module is the most heavily-hardened code I have reviewed in this VM and is in strong readiness shape for parsing untrusted .class/.jimage bytes. It contains no unsafe code, no functional stubs, and no reachable attacker-controlled panic/abort in production paths — every malformed-input case returns a typed error. The known-risk areas called out in the brief (CP-index bounds, malformed-input DoS, length-field integer overflow, jimage parsing, byte_view/buffer slicing) are each explicitly addressed with checked arithmetic, preallocation caps, recursion depth caps, and bounds-checked view construction, and the fixes are pinned by a dedicated vulnerability-fixes regression suite. Findings are all low/info: one spec-fidelity over-strictness on latest-major preview class versions, an intentionally-lazy (uninvoked-at-parse) constant-pool validate(), an O(n) cold-path in the signature cache, and a stylistic padding-cursor note — none are memory-safety or DoS vulnerabilities. Test coverage is judged at ~88%, clearing the 85% target, with the main gap being jimage malformed-header negative tests and the absence of a cargo-fuzz harness over read_class — the single highest-value addition I would recommend before declaring the untrusted-input surface fully closed."

- **Findings:** low:1, info:3
- **Stubs:** 60 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. ZERO functional stubs/fakes/NYI in production code. No `unimplemented!`, `todo!`, `NYI`, `FIXME`, `HACK`, `XXX`, or `placeholder` anywhere in scope; one TODO (lib.rs:4) is a doc-lint re-enable note. Every panic!/unreachable!/assert! in non-test code is either a test-builder helper (test_builder mod, used only by tests) or a provably-unreachable match arm. The single documented unsupported feature is jimage Zstd-compressed resources, which returns a typed JImageError::Compressed (graceful, not a panic) — production JDKs ship uncompressed. NO `unsafe` blocks exist anywhere in the module (grep confirmed). All untrusted-range slicing goes through bounds-checked APIs (ByteView::try_new, ClassFileBuffer::read_bytes/skip with checked_add, jimage read_redirect/read_offset_entry with checked_mul + slice::get).
- **Performance:**
  - Hot path is already optimized: CONSTANT_Utf8 entries are interned (cratonvm_types::intern_arc) so duplicate names share one Arc<str>; attribute bodies/bytecode are zero-copy ByteView refcount-bumps over the shared class-file Arc<[u8]> rather than per-attribute Vec<u8>+memcpy; bulk attribute tables (LineNumberTable/InnerClasses/Exceptions/ExceptionTable) use one read_bytes(N*size) + chunks_exact parse instead of per-u16 checked reads.
  - Signature parsing is memoized via a bounded (8192) FIFO cache returning Arc clones; the only non-O(1) op is the cold re-insert linear scan (info finding).
  - Vec::with_capacity is capped at PREALLOC_CAP (1024) / MAX_SWITCH_ENTRIES (16384) / MAX_STACK_MAP_ENTRIES so a hostile count field cannot trigger a multi-GB preallocation DoS.
  - jimage reads the whole file into an owned Vec (intentional, ~40MB, documented; mmap noted as future work) — acceptable.
  - No lock contention in the parse path; the only global lock is the signature cache mutex held briefly around map ops.
- **Strengths:** No unsafe code anywhere in the module — all slicing is via bounds-checked slice::get / ByteView::try_new / ClassFileBuffer, so there is no memory-safety attack surface to begin with.; Integer-overflow hardening is pervasive and deliberate: checked_add in buffer.rs read_bytes/skip and stack_map read_u16, checked_mul in jimage read_redirect/read_offset_entry/from_bytes, i64 accumulation for tableswitch count and u32 for StackMapTable absolute offsets — each with regression tests citing the audit round that added it.; Every recursive descent into untrusted input is depth-capped against stack-overflow DoS: FieldType array dimensions (255), generic Signatures (MAX_SIG_DEPTH 256), annotation/element_value mutual recursion (MAX_ANNOTATION_DEPTH 256), and nested attribute tables (MAX_ATTRIBUTE_DEPTH) — with both cached and uncached signature paths honoring the latched depth_exceeded guard.; Preallocation DoS is closed: all u16/u32 count fields are validated or .min(cap)'d before Vec::with_capacity; switch tables reject low/high/npairs blowups before allocating.; The parser is total — it returns typed ClassReaderError/JImageError on every malformed-input path instead of panicking; the panicking ByteView::new constructor is #[deprecated], pub(crate), and all runtime-offset call sites use try_new.; Lone-surrogate modified-UTF-8 is correctly recovered into a UTF-16 side table without corrupting name/descriptor readers.; Exceptionally well-tested for a parser: ~300 tests including a dedicated vulnerability_fixes.rs suite that pins each historical audit fix.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: buffer.rs, byte_view.rs, constant_pool.rs, class_reader.rs (full), field_type.rs, method_descriptor.rs, stack_map.rs (parse path + helpers), jimage.rs lines 1-820 (full Header/Location/hash/Reader parse + lookup; test_builder 820-1060 skimmed), signature.rs cache + depth-guard sections (470-692; the grammar productions 107-470 were read via targeted grep windows, not line-by-line), instruction.rs decode dispatch + switch/PC-arithmetic windows, class_file_version.rs support gate. SAMPLED via grep + windows (NOT line-by-line): attribute.rs (3440 lines) — I read the Code/StackMapTable/Unknown body decoders, both recursion guards (annotation + nested-attribute), decode_target_info, and the bulk-table parsers, and grepped all read_bytes/with_capacity/arithmetic sites; I did NOT read every one of the ~40 attribute-variant arms or the full 1500-line test module. NOT REACHED: class_file.rs, method.rs, field.rs, class_access_flags.rs, class_reader_error.rs, lib.rs (small glue/struct/enum files, no parsing of untrusted bytes — only confirmed via grep they contain no unsafe/panic/stub). No code was executed (review-only); coverage % is a judged estimate, not measured. I confirmed via grep that ALL panic!/unreachable! hits in scope are in #[cfg(test)] modules or test_builder, and that there are ZERO unsafe blocks in the entire module."

#### `types`

The `types` crate is a small (6,054 LOC across 7 files), foundational, and notably mature module: encoding/layout correctness is heavily documented, contract-annotated, and test-pinned (297 unit tests + proptest round-trips + concurrent stress). There are ZERO genuine stubs or faked behaviours. The dominant residual risk is by design, not by oversight: the NaN-boxing scheme cannot distinguish a primitive long from an object reference at the bit level, so the unchecked context-free decoders (to_value/is_object) can fabricate an ObjectRef from attacker-controlled long bits — memory safety then rests entirely on out-of-scope callers using the _checked variants. That HIGH-severity foot-gun is well-documented and instrumented with a release-countable degradation counter, but it warrants a cross-crate audit of every caller plus a fuzz assertion that the counter stays zero. Two further latent risks are documented but unenforced: ObjectRef's Send+Sync soundness depends on the single-OS-thread scheduler, and intern()'s &'static transmute depends on global-pool liveness (Drop-during-teardown hazard). No memory-safety bug is reachable within this crate's own code; all findings are contract-boundary or readiness concerns. Coverage comfortably clears the 85% target (judged ~92%). For docs/readiness: give Value an explicit #[repr] to harden the JIT field-cell contract, and elevate the Send/Sync re-audit to a gating item before preemptive multithreading.

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:1, low:2, info:1
- **Stubs:** 60 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. Exhaustive grep for unimplemented!/todo!/unreachable!/panic!/stub/NYI/TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX/placeholder across all 6 in-scope files found 60 hits; ~48 are inside #[cfg(test)] modules (test assertions/panic! match-arms), the rest are legitimate precondition panics, a documented error variant, and 2 provably-unreachable!() arms. ZERO genuine functional stubs: no native or decoder returns a hardcoded/fake/0/null/empty value where real behaviour is expected. This module is a foundational value/layout crate with no faked behaviour.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: compact_value.rs lines 1-1255 (all constructors, accessors, to_value/to_value_checked/is_object_checked, update_object_ptr[_unchecked], decode_by_descriptor, Debug) — every unsafe ObjectRef::from_raw site, all NaN-box masking/tagging, all long<->object collision arms; value.rs in full (ObjectRef unsafe ctors + Send/Sync rationale, encode/decode_value, layout asserts); heap_types.rs in full (ObjectHeader repr(C) layout, mark-word bit-packing, array_data_size overflow); intern.rs in full (RwLock fast/slow path, the &'static transmute); class_id.rs, access_flags.rs, lib.rs in full. error.rs: read lines 1-120 (MethodCallFailed/VmError hierarchy, Display dedup) + grepped the rest — it is a standard thiserror hierarchy with no unsafe/parsing/arithmetic, so SAMPLED not line-by-line beyond 120. STUB SCAN was exhaustive across all 6 files. Tests: read both integration tests in full and grepped/sampled the 105 compact_value unit tests (confirmed dedicated tests exist for each risky method) rather than reading all 297 test bodies line-by-line. NOT executed: did not run `cargo test`/`cargo build` (no compile/run performed) — coverage % is judged from source inspection, not instrumented. NOT reached: callers of to_value()/is_object() in the vm/gc/jit crates (out of scope) — the HIGH finding's exploitability depends on those call sites, which I flag for audit but did not inspect."

#### `native-api`

The native-api module is a mature, security-conscious, well-tested module — among the cleaner JVM-internals code I would expect to see. No memory-safety bug, no reachable panic/abort on the production path, and no silent-fake native was found in scope. The FFI off-heap allocator (generation handles vs UAF/confused-deputy, bounds-checked pointers, leak-free exhaustion/Drop) and the fd_table lifecycle (no double-free, reserved-fd protection, lock-free-of-blocking-IO design) are genuinely strong and thoroughly tested. charset transcoding is pure safe-Rust with correct bounds and HotSpot-matching error semantics. Findings are all low/info: (1) the trait-default copy_from/to_native_memory raw-deref a Java-supplied address with only an addr>0 guard — safe by the documented override design but a footgun worth hardening to fail-closed; (2) redefine_class is a clean-error stub; (3) the largest concrete gap is TEST coverage of registry.rs's NativeKind category-gating and real-mode register() drop branches, which is what keeps my judged coverage estimate (~83%) just under the 85% target. Recommended for readiness once the category-gating tests are added and the native-memory default impls are hardened or marked test-only.

- **Findings:** low:3, info:2
- **Stubs:** 10 markers, ~1 real functional gaps. Exhaustive grep for unimplemented!/todo!/unreachable!/panic!/stub/NYI/TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX/placeholder found 10 hits. After reading each in context: 0 reachable panics on the production path; 2 unreachable!() are in #[cfg(test)]; the rest are documentary comments (most explicitly explain why abort/error is used INSTEAD of panic to avoid unwinding across JIT/native frames). The single real functional gap is redefine_class's default Err. No native in scope was found silently faking a result (no hardcoded-0/null/empty masquerading as a real answer) — the module is notably mature and the SyntheticStub-classification system is explicitly designed to surface fakes elsewhere in the codebase, not here.
- **Performance:**
  - registry.rs dispatch hot path (find) is well-optimized: 128-bit precomputed hash, zero-alloc FxHashMap probe, and the descriptor-quirk rewrite path is #[cold]/#[inline(never)] and gated behind a cheap precheck so clean descriptors allocate nothing.
  - native_ring recording is off by default behind a single relaxed AtomicBool load; boot name registration was moved to a lazy fn()->String resolver to avoid ~3,100 format! allocations + mutex round-trips at startup.
  - ffi live_bytes() is O(1) via a running total instead of re-summing the allocation map.
  - fd_table get_entry clones an Arc<FileEntry> and drops the table RwLock read guard before blocking I/O, avoiding VM-wide serialization of fd-table mutations behind a slow read/write; close() removes under the write guard then flushes out-of-band.
  - Minor: fd_table.available() does two seeks (End + restore) per call to measure remaining bytes; fine for an occasional call but avoid in any per-read loop.
  - Minor: server_socket_ports uses std::sync::Mutex<HashMap> (SipHash) rather than parking_lot+FxHashMap used elsewhere; negligible given call frequency.
- **Strengths:** FFI off-heap memory table is exemplary: generation-tagged AllocHandle defends against use-after-free / id-reuse (M4b) and upcall slot reuse (M4c confused-deputy), bounds-checked get_ptr_checked with checked_add overflow handling, id-exhaustion frees the just-allocated block instead of leaking, and Drop frees all remaining allocations. All paths tested.; fd_table lifecycle is carefully reasoned: stdin/stdout/stderr (0/1/2) are never closed; fd counter aborts (not panics) near u32::MAX to avoid aliasing reserved fds; close() removes-then-flushes out-of-band to avoid leaking entries on flush error and to avoid holding the table lock across blocking I/O.; charset decoders are entirely safe-Rust (chunks_exact, .get(), slicing) with correct surrogate-pairing validation, BOM stripping, and matched strict(REPORT)/lossy(REPLACE) semantics mirroring HotSpot.; Pervasive defense against unwinding across the native/JIT boundary: error conditions return catchable Java exceptions or std::process::abort rather than panic! (atomic_fetch_add type-mismatch, fd overflow).; init_level uses fetch_max to eliminate a lost-update race and recovers from mutex poisoning instead of panicking the VM; correct condvar wait/notify ordering.; The unsafe surface is small (27 occurrences) and every block carries a SAFETY comment; the FFI poll/WSAPoll blocks are minimal and correct, including platform-correct nfds_t width.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: ffi.rs (all 1249 lines incl. every unsafe block and the generation-handle UAF logic), native_ring.rs (all), init_level.rs (all incl. condvar race analysis), intrinsic.rs (all), server_socket_ports.rs (all), lib.rs (all). charset.rs: read all decode/encode logic for UTF-8/16/32/ASCII/Latin1 and the lossy paths + tests (lines 1-640, 1040-1127); single-byte codepage reverse-table internals (cp1252/1251/koi8r/iso-8859-x tables, ~lines 640-1040) were SAMPLED not line-by-line, but their access pattern (rev[c as usize] into a [u8;65536]) is bounds-safe by construction. fd_table.rs (2417 lines): read the FileEntry enum, poll FFI, all open/read/write/close/seek/pread/pwrite/clone paths and udp/tcp I/O signatures (lines 1-877, 931-985, 1114-1135) + both unsafe blocks; the TLS-stream, child-process pipe, and TcpListener accept/poll_ready bodies (lines ~1135-2300) were SAMPLED via grep, not fully read. registry.rs (3301 lines): read the hash construction (full), register()/find()/find_with_descriptor_quirks() (full), NativeKind machinery + category fields (full), the two raw-memory-copy default impls (full), atomic_fetch_add_int/long defaults (full), redefine_class stub, and all inline tests; the ~250 other NativeContext trait method signatures/default bodies (annotations, reflection, allocation, the large middle of the file) were SAMPLED — I did not read every default-method body. STUB SCAN was exhaustive across all in-scope files. Coverage % is judged, not instrumented (no tarpaulin/llvm-cov run). I did not build or run the test suite. Cross-file claims (whether the real Vm overrides copy_*_native_memory and redefine_class) were NOT verified — those impls live outside native-api/src and out of scope."

#### `native-collections`

native-collections is a mature, nearly stub-free module (~35K LOC across lib.rs + a 58-line identity_hash.rs) with a deliberately small unsafe surface and a sound CHM concurrency design. It is NOT at risk from missing implementations. The real risks cluster in GC side-table lifecycle management. The standout issue: gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays — the function written specifically to bound side-table growth — is dead code with no caller anywhere in the repo, so the documented LHM/registry leak fix is not actually in effect; this also leaves the widened_obj_key recycled-identity aliasing path (finding 4) reachable, and TreeMap/TreeSet entries are not even in the prune sweep (finding 3). Separately, the GC root scan drops entire overlay tables on a poisoned lock (if let Ok), a latent use-after-free (finding 2). A capacity-cap path in ArrayList silently corrupts size at extreme sizes (finding 5), and the Properties \\uXXXX parser diverges from JDK on surrogates (finding 6). Test coverage is roughly 55% by judgment — happy paths are well covered but the highest-risk code (widened_obj_key disambiguation, prune, lock-poison handling, tm/ts pruning, capacity overflow) is entirely untested — below the 85% target. Recommended priorities for readiness: (1) wire and test gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays incl. tm/ts tables; (2) make the GC root scan poison-tolerant; (3) add the recycled-identity aliasing and capacity-overflow tests.

- **Findings:** high:2, medium:3, low:1, info:1
- **Stubs:** 12 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. Stub markers are nearly all perf/design TODO notes or the literal token 'XXXX' inside Unicode-escape parsing comments. ZERO genuine functional stubs: no native silently fakes a result via unimplemented!/todo!. The one production unreachable!() (32390) is provably dead; the one panic!() (34813) is in the test module. This module is mature/feature-complete, not stubbed. The real risks are in (a) GC side-table lifecycle (prune dead-code, lock-poison root drop, tm/ts non-prune) and (b) capacity/overflow edges, NOT missing implementations.
- **Performance:**
  - for_each_overlay_ref runs on EVERY GC and, because gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays is never called, degrades to O(total collections ever created) as dead entries accumulate — both a leak and a GC-pause regression (lib.rs:21560, 21718)
  - widened_obj_key does a linear scan `slots.iter().find(last_ptr==ptr)` per call (lib.rs:150); fine for the common single-slot case but O(n) under heavy 32-bit identity-hash collisions on a hot side-table path
  - map_resize re-hashes via per-element get/set when bulk_array_copy declines reference arrays (it always does per the audit note at 3305-3311), so resize is N set_array_element calls — acknowledged but still the live behavior
  - ArrayList grow copies element-by-element when bulk_array_copy declines (lib.rs:1232); relies on the VM intrinsic being available to avoid the slow path
- **Strengths:** Module is mature and effectively stub-free: zero genuine functional fakes; the only unreachable!/panic! in production is provably dead, the rest are perf TODOs; Memory-safety surface is tiny and well-justified: only 4 `unsafe` sites, each documented (lifetime transmute with PhantomData guard, ObjectRef::from_raw from a validated pointer map with debug_assert on null); GC-stable side-table keying via identity hash + generation packing (widened_obj_key) is a thoughtful, correct-in-principle solution to the moving-GC overlay-aliasing problem, with class-id disambiguation for recycled hashes; CHM concurrency design is sound: striped parking_lot RwLocks keyed by GC-stable identity hash (not raw pointer), readers take the read lock so the resize NEXT-splice race is genuinely closed; Capacity/overflow handled deliberately in the common paths: map_resize early-returns at MAP_MAX_CAPACITY before old_cap*2 can overflow i32; alloc_bucket_table caps eager allocation to avoid abort; map_bucket_index correctly handles negative hashes via u32 cast; GC-safety pin/read helpers (pin_value_slice/read_value_slice) correctly pin object Values across allocating calls and read back forwarded refs; Extensive in-code documentation of past bugs and rationale aids review and maintenance
- **Review depth:** Deep-read in full: identity_hash.rs (all 58 lines); widened_obj_key + pack_obj_key + obj_key_shard_for (lib.rs:130-191); all GC overlay machinery — for_each_overlay_ref, gc_scan/update/prune_*_overlays, ts_get/set_slot (lib.rs:21540-21827); all 4 unsafe sites and ChmMonitorGuard acquire/Drop (lib.rs:600-666, 21668, 34122); ArrayList capacity/add/add_at (lib.rs:1203-1241, 1480-1539); map_resize + map_bucket_index + map_alloc_node + node layout sniffing (lib.rs:3159-3500); pin_value_slice/read_value_slice/alloc_bucket_table (lib.rs:430-491); Properties decode_escape/utf8_char_end/props_read_input (lib.rs:26448-26540); chm_seg_get + CSLM read-lock sites (lib.rs:25148-25180, 30353-30433). Verified prune has no caller via repo-wide grep, and confirmed scan/update ARE wired (vm/src/memory/roots.rs:344, gc.rs:319). SAMPLED (grep + windows, not full read): the bulk of the 35K-line lib.rs — Vector/Stack/PriorityQueue/ArrayDeque/CompletableFuture(only thenApply read)/ForkJoinPool/ThreadPoolExecutor/ConcurrentSkipListMap natives were not read line-by-line; their per-method correctness vs java.util.concurrent semantics, and the merge_sort comparator-error path, were NOT exhaustively audited. NOT reached: the cross-crate is_live closure and GC epilogue in vm/src/memory/gc.rs (out of scope, only confirmed the call sites). Coverage % is a judged estimate (no instrumentation run; I did not execute `cargo test` or a coverage tool)."

#### `native-io-core`

native-io-core is mature, defensively engineered, and notably free of stubs in the audited scope — every TODO/FIXME/panic marker resolves to a test assertion, a comment about a prior fix, or one intentional fail-closed assertion. The outbound SSRF policy and connect-timeout are real and consistently enforced across both blocking and non-blocking connect paths, raw-FFI fd ownership is correct with no leaks, and untrusted native addresses are bounds-checked and routed safely. The one HIGH-severity issue is a concrete SSRF bypass: IPv4-mapped IPv6 literals (::ffff:169.254.169.254) slip past the ALWAYS-ON cloud-metadata block because is_link_local_metadata_ip never unwraps to_ipv4_mapped() — even though the very same fix is already present in the opt-in private-net classifier. That single gap should be closed (plus a regression test) before this module is considered production-ready for hosted/multi-tenant deployments. Lower-severity items: a documented residual TOCTOU in confinement-ON path validation, the format-then-reparse round trip on the per-IP re-vet, and a per-syscall heap allocation on the socket hot path. Test coverage is strong on the socket lifecycle and the policy classifier (~72% judged) but misses the security edge cases (mapped-IP metadata, nb_connect FFI state machine, confinement symlink/TOCTOU) and so does not clear the 85% target."

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:1, low:2, info:2
- **Stubs:** 33 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. No genuine functional stubs in scope. All TODO/FIXME/unimplemented-class markers resolve to either (a) #[cfg(test)] assertion panics, (b) one intentional fail-closed panic under the certified I/O profile (lib.rs:206), or (c) comments referencing other modules' history. The four scope files implement real behaviour: real FFI socket connect (nb_connect), real policy enforcement (outbound_policy), real path validation, real socket I/O. test_support.rs is a test-only NativeContext mock (#![cfg(test)]).
- **Performance:**
  - net_read0/net_write0 allocate+zero a fresh Vec per syscall (net.rs:783,832) — pool or read-into-native to remove per-op alloc on hot socket loops
  - Good: Arc-clone-under-brief-read-lock then drop the global map lock before the blocking syscall (net.rs net_read0/write0/shutdown/available) removes global-lock serialization of all socket I/O
  - outbound_policy hot path is a single atomic load + fn-ptr call + cheap string parse; BLOCK_PRIVATE_NETS env read is cached tri-state so it is a branch after first call — well optimized
  - regex_cache (lib.rs:557) is a bounded LRU over a Vec — O(n) scan per lookup with n<=32; fine, but a HashMap-backed LRU would be O(1) if delimiters churn
- **Strengths:** Outbound SSRF policy is genuinely enforced on BOTH the blocking (net.rs net_connect0 -> policy_connect) and non-blocking (socket_channel resolve_and_vet -> nb_connect::start) connect paths, with per-resolved-IP re-vet closing the common DNS-rebind/alias escape; Finite connect timeout (default 30s) on the blocking path prevents thread-pinning DoS against black-hole targets, matched to the async path's cap; Path validation always runs null-byte + escaping-parent-segment checks, with a clearly-documented opt-in CWD confinement (canonicalize-first-then-contain, TOCTOU-aware ordering) for untrusted deployments; Raw-FFI socket code (nb_connect.rs) handles fd/SOCKET ownership correctly: TcpStream::from_raw_* takes over on success/in-progress, explicit closesocket/close on every error path — no fd leak; All unsafe blocks are minimal FFI calls with documented SAFETY invariants and validated out-pointers; native read/write route untrusted Java Long addresses through ctx.copy_*_native_memory rather than dereferencing raw, with validate_native_range bounding length; Strong, well-named inline test suites for the socket lifecycle and the policy classifier; extensive audit-trail comments documenting prior security fixes (B4 bare-IPv6 host_part, V2 private-net, TOCTOU canonicalize ordering)
- **Review depth:** Inventory: lib.rs 16174 LOC, net.rs 1686, nb_connect.rs 339, outbound_policy.rs 657, test_support.rs 547. DEEP-READ in full: outbound_policy.rs (entire, incl tests), nb_connect.rs (entire). DEEP-READ targeted windows in net.rs: connect0/read0/write0/close/shutdown/available, all FFI decls, validate_native_range, fd helpers, and the test module (lines ~300-895, 1386-1683). DEEP-READ in lib.rs: the security-critical path-validation block (180-538) and FileChannel.map mmap arithmetic (11800-11905), plus every `unsafe` hit located by grep (10 sites: fcntl/LockFileEx file locking, volatile-read cache helper, 3 memmap2 map/map_mut/map_copy). #### `native-io-channels`

The native-io-channels module is mature, security-conscious, and largely free of stubs — the real path genuinely implements mmap, subprocess, zip, selectors, and channel I/O, with strong existing defenses (command-injection-safe spawning, zip-bomb guards, an ABA double-free guard, bounds-checked bulk-copy intrinsics, and SSRF gating). The most important finding is a GC-safety gap: SelectionKey attachments (and channel/selector refs) stored in the Rust-side sk_table are remapped after a moving collection but are NOT scanned as GC roots, so an attachment reachable only from that table can be collected and later resurrected as a dangling ObjectRef (UAF / wrong-object) under the compacting GC — this should be fixed by adding a gc_scan_selector_roots registration in vm/src/memory/roots.rs. The second notable issue is latent allocator UB in the DirectByteBuffer pool, where cross-bucket block reuse leads to a dealloc Layout whose size differs from the alloc Layout. Two lower-severity DoS/correctness items (datagram eager allocation from a guest-controlled ByteBuffer limit; StreamDecoder.read ignoring bad off/len) round out the actionable list. Test coverage is solid for direct_buffer/file_channel/process/stream_decoder/watch but is below the 85% target overall (~60% judged), with the highest-risk areas — zip-bomb guards, pool layout correctness, selector GC-root survival, and the async/socket channel state machines — essentially unexercised by unit tests; these are the priority additions for readiness."

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:1, low:2, info:2
- **Stubs:** 5 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. Exhaustive grep across all 14 scoped files for unimplemented!/todo!/unreachable!/panic!/stub/NYI/TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX/placeholder found only 5 hits, NONE of which is a functional stub in the real execution path: 4 are panic!() inside #[cfg(test)] assertion arms (file_channel.rs:1304, direct_buffer.rs:1166, async_socket.rs:1397, nio_selector.rs:3032) and 1 is the word 'placeholder' in a comment for a synthetic-jdk feature-gated test helper (nio_native.rs:1296, t16_dc_connect, behind #[cfg(feature="synthetic-jdk")]). The real-path natives are genuinely implemented (real mmap via memmap2, real subprocess via std::process::Command, real zip via the zip crate, real epoll/WSAPoll selectors, real sendfile/copy_file_range). A handful of ProcessHandleImpl natives intentionally return documented 'unknown' constants (parent0->-1, getProcessPids0->0, info0->no-op) matching JDK's graceful-degradation contract — these are deliberate, not stubs.
- **Performance:**
  - zip_real_jar entries()/getEntry()/getManifest() build a lazy name_index via file_names() (central-directory only, no inflate) and use by_index_raw to skip the decompressor chain — already optimized; documented carryover perf fixes are in place.
  - direct_buffer uses an FxHashMap (no SipHash) on the hot register/free path and a 16-bucket power-of-two free list with 100% hit ratio for the 4 KiB acceptance loop — good. The ABA freed_addrs FxHashSet retain() on every realloc (bump_generation, line 804) is O(set size) per allocation; under heavy churn with many distinct freed (addr,gen) pairs this could become a per-alloc hot-loop cost — consider keying freed-set by addr->current-gen instead of a flat (addr,gen) set so pruning is O(1).
  - datagram/RAF/stream_decoder use the bulk read_byte_array_into / write_byte_array_from memcpy intrinsics instead of per-element dispatch — already the fast path.
  - file_channel transferTo0 prefers copy_file_range (reflink) then sendfile then a 64 KiB userspace loop — optimal ordering on Linux; Windows always takes the userspace loop because FdTable does not expose SOCKET handles for TransmitFile (documented, acceptable).
  - process.rs holds the process_table mutex only briefly; clone_file is used to avoid holding the FdTable lock across blocking syscalls — good lock hygiene.
- **Strengths:** Process spawning uses std::process::Command with separate program+args (no shell interpolation) — structurally immune to classic command injection; adds NUL-byte rejection and a fail-closed CWD-confinement gate for both the executable and the working directory.; Zip reading has real decompression-bomb defenses: absolute per-entry size cap (configurable via CRATONVM_ZIP_MAX_ENTRY_BYTES), a 1000:1 compression-ratio check, ZIP64 size-truncation handling, a 16 MiB prealloc clamp so a lying declared size cannot pre-allocate huge, and validate_path sandboxing. It never extracts to the filesystem (reads into memory only), so zip-slip is not applicable.; DirectByteBuffer has a carefully-reasoned ABA double-free guard (per-address generation + (addr,gen) freed-set), Bits reserve/unreserve accounting with saturating add + CAS, and idempotent Cleaner firing — the result of multiple documented prior bug-fix rounds.; mmap (file_channel) is memmap2-backed with proper negative/zero length+position validation, real fsync/fdatasync for force(), and never returns a fake address; unmap is keyed by base address so forged addresses no-op.; The VM's write_byte_array_from / write_char_array_from / read_byte_array_into intrinsics are all internally bounds-checked, which neutralizes several off/len-from-guest paths that would otherwise be OOB.; Network paths (datagram, and per memory the socket paths) include an SSRF/outbound-target gate (check_outbound_target) blocking link-local cloud-metadata ranges.; Essentially zero functional stubs in the real execution path — the module genuinely implements its natives rather than faking results.
- **Review depth:** DEEP READ (full or near-full): direct_buffer.rs (1179L, end-to-end incl. pool/ABA guard/Cleaner), zip_real_jar.rs (1000L end-to-end), process.rs (1489L end-to-end), file_channel.rs map0/unmap0/force0/transferTo0 + copy_file_range/sendfile windows (lines 160-650), stream_decoder.rs decode path (300-625), datagram.rs send/receive0 (348-410), random_access_file.rs read/write bounds (370-460). Cross-file verification: traced sk_table GC-root handling into vm/src/memory/gc.rs:238 (relocation hook IS wired despite stale #[allow(dead_code)] comment) and vm/src/memory/roots.rs (confirmed sk_table is NOT in the root-scan list, unlike lang_math/jboss_msc/net_phase_e/collection overlays); confirmed write_char_array_from/write_byte_array_from are internally bounds-checked in vm_exec.rs (2521, 2380). STUB SCAN: exhaustive grep across all 14 scoped files for the full marker set — only 5 hits, all in #[cfg(test)] panic!() assertions or a synthetic-jdk test-helper comment; zero functional stubs in the real path. SAMPLED / NOT FULLY READ: nio_selector.rs (3161L — read the GC/from_raw section 1780-1870, sk_attach/attachment 2177-2210, and grepped all unsafe/epoll/WSAPoll FFI but did NOT read the full epoll/IOCP state machine 600-1170), socket_channel.rs (2357L — only grepped unsafe, not read), async_socket.rs (1404L — grepped only), watch.rs/pipe.rs/stream_encoder.rs (grepped, not deep-read). Linux sendfile/copy_file_range paths are cfg(linux) and were code-reviewed but not runtime-verified (dev host is Windows). No fuzzing/ASAN was run; findings are by inspection. Test coverage estimate is judged, not instrumented.

#### `nb-phases-late`

High-quality module for its size: very few true stubs, minimal and sound unsafe usage, careful GC pinning, real secret-zeroization, and good injection/path-traversal defenses with targeted tests. The most important issue is a HIGH-severity security gap: Certificate/X509Certificate.verify() are no-ops in default builds and never throw on a failed signature even when the legacy-crypto feature is on — silently accepting forged certs for any app that relies on them. Secondary issues are a recurring negative-length → with_capacity allocation-abort DoS in write([BII)/update([BII) (the author guards this pattern elsewhere but missed these sites), and identity-hash-keyed crypto state that can collide. Test coverage is good in density but narrow in breadth (~22%, well below the 85% target) — notably the verify/checkValidity security paths and HMAC correctness vectors are untested. For readiness: document the SSL custom-manager limitation, gate or implement real cert verification in the default build, add the bounds checks, and add HMAC/cert-verify negative tests."

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:1, low:2, info:2
- **Stubs:** 27 markers, ~3 real functional gaps. Of 27 marker hits: ~18 are panic!() inside the #[cfg(test)] module (lines 47763-50007) — legitimate test assertions, NOT production gaps. Several 'placeholder' hits are benign (e.g. line 19094 placeholder is overwritten immediately when cap==count==0; lines 13751/13781 describe MethodHandle arg-splice placeholders that ARE handled). The genuine functional gaps are the synthetic-crypto verify/checkValidity no-ops and the line-30438 null stub. The Windows WindowsNativeDispatcher comment (6755) flags an unrelated unimplemented area outside this file. Overall the module has FEW true stubs for its size; the bigger concern is feature-gated security checks that silently degrade to no-ops.
- **Performance:**
  - doFinal()[B and doFinal([B)[B already optimized to a single lock + mem::take (no Vec clones) — good, documented at 30802-30809.
  - getMacLength/getAlgorithm/clone clone the algo String (and full MacState in clone) out of the lock; acceptable but clones algo on every getMacLength call (line 30885) — could store algo length without cloning.
  - write([BII)/update([BII) read array elements one Value at a time via ctx.get_array_element in a loop (12867-12871, 30768-30772) instead of a bulk read like mac_read_byte_array uses — per-element virtual dispatch is O(n) calls; a bulk byte-array read would be faster on hot ZIP/HMAC paths.
  - mac_state_table is a single global Mutex<HashMap> guarding ALL Mac instances process-wide; under concurrent HMAC use (e.g. SCRAM auth storms) this serializes every update/doFinal across threads — contention hot spot.
- **Strengths:** Crypto Mac side-table has genuine secret-hygiene: Drop impl zeroizes key+data via write_volatile + compiler_fence, bounded-cap eviction (MAC_STATE_MAX_ENTRIES) with eviction-time scrub, and re-key scrubs the old key (30599-30655, 30736-30742).; JSON unescape parser is robust: correct UTF-16 surrogate-pair handling, U+FFFD for unpaired/malformed surrogates, char-based so never panics on bad UTF-8 boundaries — with dedicated edge-case tests (46236-46312, tests 49914-49946).; SubmissionPublisher subscriber-list growth pins all live roots across internal allocations (pin_native_root / read_native_pin) — GC-correct (19080-19115).; Strong input validation where it matters: is_safe_factory_class_name allowlist blocks path traversal/consecutive-dots/oversize with tests; SQLite savepoint names validated to ASCII-alnum/underscore before interpolation, defeating SQL injection (48700-48724, 33848-33854).; SSL connector fails SAFE — default platform trust store, never a trust-all bypass; the divergence (ignoring custom managers) is in the secure direction and documented.; Only 6 unsafe blocks in 50K LOC, all well-formed FFI (Win32 GetProcessMemoryInfo, libc getrusage) or a single-byte volatile write; zero transmute.
- **Review depth:** Stub-scanned the entire 50,007-line file (grep for all stub markers, unsafe, transmute, negative-len coercions, with_capacity-from-arg, TLS trust patterns). Deep-read (full windows): all 6 unsafe blocks (14690, 14716, 30619 + the FFI structs); crypto Mac side-table, HMAC dispatch, update/doFinal/clone (30565-30938); SSL/TLS connector + SSLContext.getInstance/init (30940-31200); X509 verify/checkValidity (32630-32940); JSON unescape + utf8-len (46236-46340); SubmissionPublisher pinning (19077-19115); factory-name allowlist (48700-48724); SQLite savepoint SQL construction (33836-33880); ZipOutputStream write paths (12855-12903); the full test module structure (47763-50007, all 66 test names). NOT deep-read: the bulk of NIO ByteBuffer get/put/slice and Selector natives (6200-12500, only sampled for the as-usize pattern), JDBC ResultSet/row mapping internals (33200-33620), Loom/continuation runtime (48229-48700), the MethodHandle arg-splice parser (13751-13800), and most of the 572 registrations between 14000-46000 — these were grep-sampled by risk pattern but not read line-by-line. HMAC primitive impls (hmac_sha256 etc.) live in another module (out of scope). Findings are representative, not exhaustive; the negative-len and identity-hash patterns very likely recur at additional unaudited sites."

#### `nb-lib`

The three in-scope files are largely well-engineered and unusually well-commented, with security-conscious hardening on the Unsafe surface (size caps, bounds-checked array paths, the recover_object_arg soundness fix) and an honest stub posture for JEP 484. No memory-safety vulnerability was proven reachable from attacker-controlled input within these files; the highest-impact issues are correctness bugs: (1) two SharedSecrets handlers (makeClassValueMap, parseCookie fallback) return uninitialized collections via the documented-elsewhere init-on-no-receiver anti-pattern, and (2) the BigInteger.modPow / modInverse natives silently produce WRONG results (sign-stripping; i128 fallback to 1; no ArithmeticException) which is dangerous if any crypto path reaches them. One low-severity gap remains in the non-array Unsafe.copyMemory/setMemory slot loop (no in-crate field-count bounds check). Test adequacy is the weakest dimension: ~45% judged coverage, well below the 85% target, because classfile_api and shared_secrets_bridge have ONLY registration-presence tests and zero behavior tests, and the crypto/recover/bounds helpers are untested. For readiness: document the no-SecurityManager privilege-bypass posture and the unimplemented java.lang.classfile surface, and add HotSpot-differential tests for the BigInteger natives.

- **Findings:** medium:4, low:4, info:3
- **Stubs:** 52 markers, ~4 real functional gaps. 47 marker hits in lib.rs but the large majority are legitimate: ~40 are `_ => panic!("expected Long/Int/condition")` precondition asserts inside #[cfg(test)] modules (lines 42939-44592) or value-tag asserts, and several `unreachable!()` sit at the tail of infinite CAS-retry `loop{}` bodies (17767/17802/17901/17935/17976) that always return before reaching them — none are reachable functional gaps. classfile_api.rs (3 markers) is an explicitly-flagged honest synthetic stub. shared_secrets_bridge.rs has 1 placeholder comment + 1 panic! on an unknown owner class in make_factory_callback (compile-time-exhaustive, not runtime-reachable). GENUINE functional gaps that silently fake a result: classfile_api accessor fabrication, modPow sign-stripping, modInverse i128 fallback, and the checkArray no-op. The bi_mod_pow_str negative-exponent panic is a latent (currently-unreachable) gap.
- **Performance:**
  - lib.rs:16188 Unsafe/atomic hot-path side stores are sharded (32-way parking_lot + FxHash) — good; synthetic_field_store and class_atomic_side_store remain unsharded (noted in-code) but low-contention so acceptable.
  - lib.rs:18016-18038 byte/boolean array read/write fast-path correctly routes through bulk read_byte_array_into/write_byte_array_from (copy_nonoverlapping) avoiding per-element Value boxing — good.
  - shared_secrets_bridge.rs:163 alloc_singleton allocates a fresh owner-class object on EVERY factory call (deliberate, documented). Fine because handlers are identity-agnostic, but SharedSecrets.getXxxAccess() is called frequently during boot; a per-interface cached singleton would cut boot-time allocation/GC pressure.
  - lib.rs:29460-29476 modPow/modInverse operate on DECIMAL STRINGS (bi_mul_unsigned/bi_div_unsigned per bit) — O(bits * len^2) string arithmetic; extremely slow for crypto-sized inputs even ignoring the correctness bugs. If these natives are ever on a real crypto path, prefer the limb-based bigint in bigint.rs/biginteger_intrinsics.rs.
- **Strengths:** recover_object_arg (lib.rs:16881) is a soundness win: it REFUSES to fabricate an ObjectRef from arbitrary Double bits (C20), failing loud rather than dereferencing Java-controlled bits — a textbook-correct fix.; Unsafe.copyMemory/setMemory enforce a 256 MiB cap (MAX_UNSAFE_COPY_SIZE) and route all array targets through bounds-checked byte pack/unpack, refusing to fall through to the unbounded slot loop (finding M3 remediation). Well tested for the size-reject case.; classfile_api.rs is an honest stub: the productive entry points (parse/build/buildTo/transformClass/ClassBuilder.build) throw catchable UnsupportedOperationException instead of returning empty/fabricated byte[] — no silent corruption, correctly tagged SyntheticStub.; shared_secrets_bridge factory dispatch uses a compile-time-exhaustive match over a static FACTORIES table with a vm-side in-sync #[test]; the runtime panic arm is unreachable by construction.; The Unsafe offset decode (unsafe_offset, unsafe_array_index_from_offset, unsafe_checked_array_index) is heavily hardened: negative->0, clamp of implausible offsets, arena-tag awareness, and a checked variant that returns None on OOB so callers fall back to benign sentinels.; GC root scan/remap for locale caches (gc_scan_locale_roots/gc_update_locale_refs) uses from_raw only with pointer_map keys derived from the live objects' own pointers — sound.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: classfile_api.rs (all 1679 lines) and shared_secrets_bridge.rs (all 1814 lines, both pages). lib.rs (44.8K LOC) was grep-targeted, NOT read end-to-end: I read windows around every hit for the stub-marker scan, all unsafe/from_raw/transmute/copy_nonoverlapping sites (13650-13690, 16850-16969, 15966-16195, 17755-18335), the Unsafe copy/set/allocate memory natives, the BigInteger modPow/modInverse/miller-rabin/mod_pow_str region (28640-29516), and the registration aggregation (2030-2060, 9950, 12107) plus the inline test region (42849-44600 sampled). NOT reached in lib.rs: the bulk of the 44K lines between ~2000-13000 and ~19000-28000 and ~30000-42000 (logging/keycloak/collection/charset/misc natives) were only seen via grep, so additional correctness bugs there are possible but outside the focused risk areas. I confirmed new_object vs new_object_initialized semantics by reading native-api/src/registry.rs:343-381. I did NOT run cargo test or build (read-only review); coverage % is judged from test inventory, not instrumented. The non-array copyMemory OOB finding's exploitability depends on VM-side set_field bounds behavior which lives outside these three files and was not verified."

#### `nb-phases-early`

Both files are mature, defensively written, and heavily spec-aware — extensive comments tie behaviour to HotSpot/JVMS semantics and to specific app-suite bugs. Memory safety is strong: phases_early.rs has ZERO production unsafe blocks and no production unwrap/expect; lang_class.rs's only production unsafe is a GC pointer-remap (7658-7679) that is correct (proper lock guard, debug_assert on null new addresses). Integer arithmetic in the BitSet helpers uses saturating ops plus a BS_MAX_WORDS clamp, neutralizing the MIN_INT/overflow DoS surface (with a test). The standout issue is a security INCONSISTENCY, not a memory bug: the primary Class.forName and the 3-arg forName overload skip the NUL/control-byte/path-separator validation that the codebase already implements, tests, and documents as anti-filesystem-probing on the Module overload (lang_class.rs:1345 vs 1675) — fix is a small shared-helper refactor. Remaining findings are quality/info: a dead duplicate array branch in getModifiers, a silent setSigners no-op, and a stale getConstantPool doc comment. Test discipline is good (183 #[test] fns across both modules with real edge cases), but I estimate ~78% coverage — below the 85% target — chiefly because the most-used forName path, the loadClass fallback ladder, and the GC remap/recursion-guard paths are untested. Recommended for readiness after (1) adding forName-side name sanitization + tests, and (2) closing the named test gaps.

- **Findings:** high:1, low:2, info:2
- **Stubs:** 62 markers, ~1 real functional gaps. Marker grep returned 2 hits in phases_early.rs (one TODO comment at 3096 re weak-key cleanup; one panic at 17403 inside #[cfg(test)]) and 60 in lang_class.rs. Of the 60 lang_class hits, ~50 are panic!() inside the #[cfg(test)] module (line 11838+) used as test assertions, and the rest are the word 'placeholder' appearing in doc comments describing synthetic-mode mirror behaviour. There are ZERO production unimplemented!/todo!/unreachable! and ZERO production .unwrap()/.expect() before the test boundaries in either file. The single genuine functional fake-success is Class.setSigners (silent no-op). WF7 synthetic-class injection is real but correctly gated off-by-default and scoped — consistent with the project's 'real-by-default, synthetic experimental opt-in' rule. Many natives deliberately return null vs empty-array to match OpenJDK semantics (e.g. getRawAnnotations null avoids downstream BufferUnderflowException) — these are correct, not stubs.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: all production unsafe (lang_class.rs:7632-7679 GC scan/remap; confirmed phases_early.rs has none); resource-name validation (840-917); BitSet range/capacity/get/set arithmetic (3650-3777); native_class_for_name full body incl. the loadClass fallback ladder and ensure_class_initialized sinks (1332-1611); for_name_3 (1613-1619); for_name_module (1644-1694); getModifiers full body (7392-7509) and array_element_access_bits (7374-7390); getDeclaredMethod (5671-5770 incl. cache-key build) and getDeclaredMethods (5617-5669 incl. depth guard); generic_signature/raw_annotations/raw_type_annotations/constant_pool (11058-11157); WF7 synthesize + minimal-classfile builder (1240-1330); setSigners (11819-11836). VERIFIED the panic/marker hits are test assertions by locating the #[cfg(test)] boundaries (lang_class 11838; phases_early 6135 helper + 16771 main test mod). SAMPLED test function inventories for both modules to judge coverage. NOT reached / sampled only: the full bodies of the ~50 other reflection natives in lang_class.rs (getCanonicalName, getNestHost/Members, getRecordComponents, getEnclosingMethod, annotation-by-type walkers, etc.) were read by signature/null-handling pattern but not line-by-line; the bulk of phases_early.rs collection/string/fork-join natives outside BitSet were not deep-read; I did not trace ensure_class_initialized into the VM core to confirm whether it independently sanitizes names (the finding stands on the documented sibling-overload defense regardless). No fuzzing or build/test execution was performed.

#### `nb-lang`

The nb-lang module is mature and defensively coded in its highest-risk surfaces — System.arraycopy, Unsafe.defineClass (untrusted bytecode), GC pointer remaps, and String bounds are all hardened with explicit overflow/underflow guards, checked arithmetic, and catch_unwind. The real `unsafe` footprint in production is small and well-annotated; most of the 109 stub/panic markers are comment words or test-only assertions, not functional gaps. The most serious finding is a HIGH-severity correctness bug: six AtomicXFieldUpdater accessors (getAndIncrement/getAndDecrement/addAndGet for both int and long) use bounded CAS loops that, on contention, silently lose the update AND return a fabricated 0 — the author fixed this exact pattern for the sibling getAndAdd methods but left these six unconverted, and the single-threaded test harness cannot catch it. Secondary issues: StrictMath delegates to non-reproducible platform libm (spec violation), MethodHandles.Lookup performs no access-control enforcement (correctness/spec gap, limited security impact under the trusted-bytecode threat model), and AbstractStringBuilder.getChars swallows invalid-destination errors instead of throwing (no OOB write, since the heap layer bounds-checks). Test coverage is uneven and does NOT meet 85%: estimated ~55%, with lang_invoke (7079 LOC / 12 tests), lang_reflect (1 test), and lang_misc (0 tests) effectively untested, and no concurrency tests anywhere. Top priorities for readiness: fix the atomic-updater lost-update bug (convert to atomic_fetch_add), add multi-threaded updater stress tests, add Lookup negative-access and lang_invoke MethodHandle tests, and either wire a real fdlibm for StrictMath or document the divergence.

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:3, low:3, info:1
- **Stubs:** 109 markers, ~2 real functional gaps. Of 109 marker hits, the overwhelming majority are the WORD 'placeholder'/'stub'/'panic' appearing in COMMENTS describing legitimate JVM semantics, or panic!/unreachable! inside #[cfg(test)] assertions. There are NO unimplemented!()/todo!() in production paths in scope. Genuine functional gaps (a native that fakes a real result): (1) the atomic_updater bounded-loop accessors that return a fabricated 0 on contention (high-severity finding); (2) the lang_reflect.rs primitive-return default-box fallback. Lookup.find* lacking access checks is a behavioral gap rather than a fake-value stub. The handful of real `unsafe` blocks in production (classloader GC remap lines 106/125, lang_math obj-remap 2565/2577/2589, lang_invoke pointer-recovery 377/396/3940) are all narrowly scoped with SAFETY notes; the rest of the 23 unsafe hits are in #[cfg(test)] mock code.
- **Performance:**
  - classloader.rs:1864-1873 Unsafe.defineClass copies up to 1 MiB byte-by-byte via per-element get_array_element trait dispatch (hot at Spring/Hibernate/cglib startup) — use a bulk copy
  - lang_string.rs substring/charAt paths are already optimized (peek length, partial decode, bulk copy_nonoverlapping noted at 1215/1518) — good
  - atomic_updater bounded CAS loops spin up to 1024 times before giving up; converting to atomic_fetch_add primitives (a single LOCK XADD) is both correct AND faster than the spin
- **Strengths:** System.arraycopy bounds checking is robust: end-offset additions widened to i64 to defeat release-mode wrap (V14 fix, lang_system.rs:131-158), full element-type/assignability checks, correct overlapping-copy direction handling; Unsafe.defineClass is well-hardened against untrusted bytecode: offset/length validation with checked_add, length cap, catch_unwind around the array read, magic+min-length check before backend (classloader.rs:1778-1897); GC pointer-remap unsafe blocks (classloader.rs:97-129) correctly repoint singleton/side-table ObjectRefs via the pointer map with debug_assert on null; String charAt/substring/last_index_of bounds are correctly checked, including the buf.is_empty() guard preventing usize underflow at lang_string.rs:1007-1010; Raw-pointer reconstruction in lang_invoke is gated by a non-dereferencing hashmap probe (class_id_from_mirror) with thorough SAFETY comments; Math.pow integer-exponent fast path correctly gates on is_finite()+fract()==0+|b|<64 to preserve JLS special-value semantics; Clock/time natives use map_or(0,..) on duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH) so a pre-epoch/erroring clock cannot panic

#### `nb-crypto-security`

The module's hard crypto primitives are in good shape: symmetric AES/GCM and the asymmetric VERIFY/padding paths delegate to constant-time, KAT-tested code and fail closed, and the X.509 chain validator, JKS loader, and SecurityManager policy engine are security-conscious with documented fixes for prior bypasses. The serious problems are two: (1) CRITICAL — the registered java.security.SecureRandom.nextBytes is a non-cryptographic splitmix64 DRBG seeded from a single 64-bit value, so every key/IV/nonce/token minted through it in the interpreter is predictable; this is the top priority and is not caught by the existing tests. (2) HIGH — RSA/ECDSA PRIVATE-key operations run on a hand-rolled variable-time BigUint::modpow with no blinding or CRT, leaking secret exponents/nonces to a timing adversary (honestly documented as VULN(2) but unfixed). Secondary issues: DRBG state keyed on collidable 32-bit identity hashes, no hostname/KeyUsage enforcement in the bare TrustManager path, and an over-broad policy codeBase '/-' match. Test breadth is strong (531 inline tests) but lands around ~72% and below the 85% bar, with the worst blind spots being SecureRandom unpredictability, ML-KEM, key-agreement, and constant-time behavior. Recommend gating readiness on fixing findings #1 and #2 before this module is considered production-grade for security-sensitive workloads."

- **Findings:** critical:1, high:1, medium:2, low:1, info:2
- **Stubs:** 61 markers, ~4 real functional gaps. 61 raw matches for stub/panic/TODO markers across scope. The overwhelming majority are NOT functional gaps: ~30 are panic!()/unreachable! inside #[cfg(test)] assertions (e.g. keystore.rs:1726/1734, security_manager.rs:1416/1581/2620+, x509_manager.rs:2076-2579, key_factory.rs:1978-2119, signature.rs:1262-1320, tls.rs:2526-2547), and many 'placeholder'/'stub' hits are doc comments describing PRIOR bugs that were FIXED (e.g. tls.rs:894/963 'No-validation placeholder removed', crypto_impl.rs:1266-1280 deleted dangerous single-shot stubs, validate_cert_chain VULN-fixed comment). The few genuine still-live functional gaps: (1) jca/cipher.rs:697 returns an error for non-AES/GCM modes via cipher_do_final_impl's narrow probe scope (though CBC/DES are routed to real SunJCE and GCM/ECB are real); (2) jca/key_factory.rs:1220-1262 Ed25519/X25519/PQC KeyFactory paths recognized-but-unimplemented (fail-closed with InvalidKeySpecException, no-synthetic-stubs); (3) x509_manager.rs verify_one_signature NotImplemented for PSS/Ed25519/DSA (fail-closed, finding above); (4) t3_impl.rs:1505 javac/jshell tooling stubs (non-crypto). keystore.rs:1679 'DUMMY1 placeholder' is a #[cfg(test)] JKS fixture builder, not production. No reachable production unimplemented!/todo! found.
- **Performance:**
  - secure_random_fill / secure_random_reseed take a global parking_lot::RwLock write lock on SECURE_RANDOM_STATE for every nextBytes call (crypto_impl.rs:1090) — global contention point under concurrent crypto; per-object DRBG state in a hidden field would remove the lock entirely
  - SECURE_RANDOM_STATE HashMap never evicts (crypto_impl.rs:1059) — unbounded growth across many short-lived SecureRandom instances
  - BigUint mul/div_rem are schoolbook O(n^2) with per-op Vec allocations; RSA-2048/4096 modpow allocates heavily per square-and-multiply step (crypto_impl.rs:1771) — slow private-key ops; the audited crates would also be faster
  - validate_chain re-parses every cert DER on each checkServerTrusted (x509_manager.rs:863) with no parsed-cert cache; fine for typical short chains
- **Strengths:** AES and AES-GCM are backed by the constant-time RustCrypto aes_gcm crate; the GCM auth-tag check is inside the crate (no hand-rolled tag compare) — crypto_impl.rs:8 header, :294 decrypt; RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signature verify is CONSTRUCTIVE (builds expected EM and byte-compares) so it is NOT vulnerable to Bleichenbacher/BERserk lax-parsing forgery, and rejects undersized moduli fail-closed — crypto_impl.rs:2011-2059; RSA OAEP/PKCS1 unpadding rewritten constant-time and Manger-resistant — crypto_impl.rs:2286, 2372 with ct_eq helpers; DER tag/length parser hardened against integer overflow and forged-length DoS (checked_add, width clamp, buffer bound) — crypto_impl.rs:3376-3421; JKS integrity MAC verified with a real constant_time_eq before structural parsing, and chain_count DoS-capped against the remaining buffer — keystore.rs:439-513, 752; rustls client path validates chains against the native root store and enables hostname verification via ServerName::try_from(host) — no dangerous/no-op verifier — t27_tls.rs:549/582; Signature verify and cipher init fail CLOSED: verify_dispatch(...).unwrap_or(false) (signature.rs:924/957), unknown cert sig OIDs -> CertificateException, missing cipher state -> IllegalStateException (no silent null ciphertext); Honest in-code documentation of known weaknesses (VULN(2) variable-time modpow) and of prior fixed bugs (deleted dangerous single-shot cipher/mac stubs)
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: crypto_impl.rs RNG core (881-1264), the per-instance DRBG (secure_random_fill/reseed 1089-1131), BigUint::modpow + private-key call sites (1757-1812, 2006, 2483), RSA PKCS1v15/OAEP verify+pad (2011-2059, 2286-2419), DER parsers (3376-3421); x509_manager.rs validate_chain + verify_one_signature (857-1028) and check_server/client_trusted (1568-1606); tls.rs TrustManagerFactory wiring + validate_cert_chain (880-990, 1618-1707); keystore.rs load_jks + constant_time_eq + jks key recovery (439-513, 709-761); jca/cipher.rs cipher_do_final_impl (488-700); jca/signature.rs sign/verify dispatch (394-421, 887-957); jca/kem.rs getInstance/encapsulator wiring (200-290); security_manager/policy.rs implies_full + codeBase/target matchers (211-340). SAMPLED via grep windows: t27_tls.rs rustls client/server config (used grep + targeted reads of build_rustls_client_config and ServerName sites), key_factory.rs unimplemented-algo fail-closed paths, security_manager.rs (grep for panics — all in tests), wildfly_security.rs (panic at 1409 is a test). NOT FULLY READ (grep-scanned only, large/lower-risk for this scope): the bulk of tls_impl.rs handshake byte-parsing (2772 LOC — only ServerName extension paths checked; the raw TLS record parser was not line-audited for OOB and is a recommended follow-up), t27_tls.rs server-side ResolvesServerCert SNI dispatch beyond the comment at :72, x500.rs/asn1.rs DN/ASN.1 decoders (parse untrusted input — only spot-checked, merit a dedicated OOB pass), message_digest.rs, key_agreement.rs (0 tests, not read in depth). The AES/AesGcm primitive bodies are thin wrappers over RustCrypto and were verified to delegate rather than hand-roll. I did not build or run the test suite; coverage % is a judged estimate."

#### `nb-net`

Code quality is high and the team has clearly done prior security hardening: untrusted-input integer math uses checked arithmetic, frame/body/header sizes are capped before allocation, and parsers fail closed. No memory-safety bug was found that is reachable on the default (non-moving) GC, and there are no production stubs that fake results. The most serious issues are (1) the server-side parser silently ignoring Transfer-Encoding, a real request-smuggling/body-desync vector, and (2) IoFuture result/notifier ObjectRefs stored cross-thread without the GC-root scan/update that the analogous HttpServer handler path already has — a latent UAF if/when the moving collector is used. A medium credential-leak exists in cross-host redirect handling, and two medium DoS gaps (unbounded chunked size-line/trailer buffering; HTTP/2 PADDED-HEADERS mishandling, which fails closed). Test breadth is good for pure helpers (~199 tests) but the highest-risk untrusted-input state machines — the HTTP/2 frame loop, decode_hpack_int overflow arms, server chunked/TE parsing, and IoFuture GC-safety — are essentially untested, so coverage lands around 68%, below the 85% target. Recommend fixing the TE smuggling gap and IoFuture rooting before readiness sign-off and adding fuzz/property tests over the network parsers.

- **Findings:** high:2, medium:3, low:2
- **Stubs:** 35 markers, ~2 real functional gaps. Stub scan was exhaustive across all 7 files. 35 grep hits total; 0 production unimplemented!/todo!. The only genuine FUNCTIONAL gaps are: (1) HTTP/2 connection is single-stream / not pooled (perform_request comment line 1083, http2_request returns keep_alive:false at 921) — a real-behavior limitation, not a faked result; (2) the server parser silently ignoring Transfer-Encoding (filed as a high finding, behaves as if body is empty). The exception path in IoFuture intentionally stores a message String instead of the ObjectRef (xnio_async.rs:242) — a deliberate simplification, not a stub. No native was found returning hardcoded 0/null where real behavior is expected, aside from the TE gap.
- **Performance:**
  - http_client.rs read_chunked: prefix.drain(..size+2) at line 617 is O(n) memmove per chunk; for many small chunks this is O(n*chunks). Consider an index cursor instead of draining from the front.
  - net_phase_e parse_http_request already fixed the O(n^2) header-terminator rescan with the `scanned` cursor (good); the equivalent body read is fine.
  - huffman_lookup (http2.rs:475) is a linear scan of the 257-entry table per symbol — acceptable per the comment, but a prefix/trie or length-bucketed map would cut decode cost for large Huffman header values.
  - IoFuture drain_notifiers re-locks state in a loop to catch add-during-drain (xnio_async.rs:283-303) — fine for small notifier counts; not a hot path.
  - No connection pooling for HTTP/2 (one stream per connection) forces a fresh TLS handshake per request — a throughput limitation, not a correctness issue.
- **Strengths:** HPACK integer decoder uses checked_add/checked_shl and shift>63 guard — no silent wrap of lengths/indices (http_client.rs:1005-1038); HPACK Huffman decoder fails closed on EOS-in-stream, invalid padding, and unmatchable codes rather than returning wrong data (http2.rs:426-471); only the static table is used (no dynamic-table state to corrupt); HTTP/2 frame reader enforces SETTINGS_MAX_FRAME_SIZE before allocating the payload, and caps header-block + body accumulation (http_client.rs:798, 849, 868, 895); Server parser is hardened against the CL smuggling vectors it does cover: rejects conflicting/duplicate/unparseable Content-Length with 400, caps body via CRATONVM_HTTP_MAX_BODY (413), caps header size at 1 MiB, fast-rejects non-HTTP first byte (net_phase_e.rs:6035-6116); http_decode_chunked is overflow-safe: validates hex digits, caps per-chunk at 64 MiB so n+2 cannot wrap, bounds every slice (net_phase_e.rs:3156-3201) with explicit overflow/truncation tests; Byte[]<->buffer copy helpers validate negative offset/length and use checked_add against array_length before any element access (net_phase_e.rs:800-847; xnio_conduits.rs:410-466); Event loops bound their task queue and timer heap (MAX_PENDING_TASKS, push_bounded) and the moving-GC handler-root scan/update is correctly implemented for the HttpServer handler refs (net_phase_e.rs:5905-5942); Module is almost entirely safe Rust: only a handful of unsafe blocks, all narrow ObjectRef::from_raw/Box::from_raw with documented invariants; no transmute or raw pointer arithmetic on untrusted lengths

#### `nb-serial-concurrency`

Mature, security-hardened module with multiple documented audit rounds (V5/V9 fixes, JEP-290 enforcement, GC-stable side-table keying). No genuine stubs and essentially no raw unsafe in production code — memory access is funnelled through bounds-checked accessors. The deserialization surface, the historically highest-risk area, is well-implemented: the class-name filter runs before instantiation and the four numeric DoS limits are genuinely enforced and tested. The most actionable finding is a MEDIUM default-reachable DoS: ois_read_array (serialization.rs:2121-2132) lacks the filter-independent array-length ceiling that the sibling TC_LONGSTRING path has, so a hostile stream with a 2.1-billion-element array and no installed maxarray filter (the default) can drive a multi-GB allocation. Secondary items are a low-severity non-atomic compareAndExchange witness value, the inherent (and acceptable, but undocumented) raw-pointer fall-through trust boundary in the long-addressed Unsafe get/put, a per-byte locked off-heap memcpy/memset performance cliff, a stale/misleading TODO claiming the JEP-290 limits are unenforced, and unbounded-until-cap identity-keyed side-tables. Tests are strong (445 inline + integration) but I judge coverage at ~82%, just under the 85% target, with the array-DoS path, CAE-under-contention, and mixed-direction copyMemory bounds failures as the notable gaps to close."

- **Findings:** medium:1, low:3, info:2

Production-path panics are limited to register-time invariant guards (e.g. deprecated_io_util.rs:1606 / deprecated_util.rs:1643 / deprecated_internal.rs:1365 'should be registered', and unsafe_natives.rs:2223 which is in tests). These are legitimate precondition/registration-invariant panics, NOT runtime-reachable from attacker input. The XXX/uXXXX hits in properties_sidetable.rs are documentation of \uXXXX escape handling, not stub markers. The deprecated_internal.rs off-heap store (lines 33-50) is intentionally dead code (#[allow(dead_code)], superseded by the consolidated arena store per SECURITY FIX V5), retained deliberately, not a stub.
- **Performance:**
  - Off-heap setMemory/copyMemory iterate byte-by-byte through a locked, HashMap-locating arena accessor (unsafe_natives.rs:770-784,887-918) — up to 256M locked calls for a 256MiB op; needs a bulk fill/copy accessor in lib.rs.

- **Strengths:** Deserialization is security-conscious and matches a real JEP-290 implementation: the class-name filter is consulted BEFORE ensure_class_initialized/instantiation (serialization.rs:1971, 2118, 1937), closing the synthetic-read filter-bypass that would otherwise let gadget classes load unfiltered.; Robust hardening against deserialization DoS: ois_buf_read uses checked_add and a 64-byte capped short-read pad (serialization.rs:651-686); TC_LONGSTRING clamps to remaining + 256MiB hard ceiling.; Unsafe off-heap memory was consolidated onto a single bounds-checked arena store (SECURITY FIX V5), eliminating the prior dual-allocator aliasing/UAF; setMemory validates the whole [off,off+bytes) window up-front with checked_add before any write (unsafe_natives.rs:744-760).; Unsafe.defineClass no longer hard-skips bytecode verification — it gates the skip behind the native-access trust gate (V9 fix, unsafe_natives.rs:1124-1138), verifying attacker-influenced bytes by default.; Production code in all 7 files contains essentially no raw `unsafe` blocks — memory access is funnelled through validated NativeContext/crate accessors; the only unsafe{} are in test mocks.; joinUntil honours the deadline with a 2ms-bounded park slice (no busy-spin) and guards Instant overflow with checked_add (jdk25_concurrency.rs:968-996).; Properties side-table re-keyed to a GC-stable identity hash with a generation registry to disambiguate collisions (properties_sidetable.rs:104-148), fixing a prior moving-GC pointer-reuse contamination bug.

SAMPLED: serialization.rs OOS/write-side and the 6000+ line of tests (verified security tests exist by name, did not read each); jdk25_concurrency ScopedValue/Carrier and ~2700 lines of tests (read structure, sampled). LIGHT/STRUCTURAL ONLY: deprecated_util.rs, deprecated_io_util.rs, deprecated_internal.rs — confirmed no production unsafe blocks and that the off-heap store there is intentional dead code (V5), but did NOT line-by-line audit their java.beans/java.rmi/java.util.zip native bodies (RandomAccessFile/zip fd-leak and Introspector/RMI correctness were NOT exhaustively verified). NOT REACHED: the lib.rs arena store internals (unsafe_arena_* / ArenaStore) that the bounds-checking ultimately relies on — out of scope but is the trusted base for every Unsafe memory finding here; heap.alloc_array cap behaviour was checked only at the call boundary (vm_exec.rs:2259), not inside the allocator. Test coverage % is a judged estimate (no instrumentation run)."

#### `nb-frameworks`

The nb-frameworks module is generally well-engineered with strong test coverage on the highest-risk code (Panama FFM has real bounds/overflow/downcall/upcall tests and the unsafe blocks are carefully checked; the XML StAX layer is XXE-safe by construction because quick-xml never fetches external entities or expands custom entities). Most of the ~250 stub/panic markers are test mocks or precondition NPEs, not functional gaps. The genuine concerns are: (1) MemorySegment.copy uses copy_nonoverlapping on possibly-overlapping regions (UB + data corruption); (2) a reachable VM-aborting panic in the OpenMBean/Introspector type-descriptor parser on malformed input; (3) the Panama native-access gate is process-global rather than per-module, widening the trust boundary vs HotSpot; (4) Quarkus ArC is a default-ON synthetic CDI shim that fakes bean discovery (getBeans->empty), contrary to the project's opt-in-synthetic rule; and (5) the AOT cache's integrity check is a forgeable keyless hash that becomes a code-injection vector the moment compiled_code is ever executed (it currently is not). AOT code caching and the Vector API are scalar/placeholder and should be documented as such for readiness. Test coverage is judged ~72%, short of the 85% target, dragged down mainly by xml_stax (0 inline tests, no XXE regression) and missing negative/fidelity tests for the synthetic framework shims and the two memory/parse bugs found.

- **Findings:** medium:5, low:6
- **Stubs:** 250 markers, ~4 real functional gaps. Of ~250 grep hits for stub/panic/TODO markers, the overwhelming majority are NOT functional gaps: ~95 in cds.rs are panic!("... not implemented for testing") inside a #[cfg(test)] MockNativeContext, and most others (jmx_openmbean, logmanager, wildfly_core, jboss_module_loader, quarkus_arc) are panic!() inside #[test] assertions or legitimate precondition NPEs on null receivers. Only the jmx.rs:2847 unreachable! is in a test. AFTER excluding tests, the genuine synthetic-fake / unimplemented gaps are the 4 examples above (plus the scalar Vector API and the AOT placeholder fingerprint). The framework shims (Servlet NIO/socket layer, JBoss MSC/module-loader, WildFly, Infinispan local cache) are real reimplementations rather than no-op fakes; Spring startup-metrics shims were already removed per project history and the remaining Spring/app-stubs no-ops are default-OFF feature-gated. Quarkus ArC is the one default-ON synthetic that fakes framework semantics.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: panama.rs unsafe surface (validated_fn_ptr, pe_segment_access_addr, get/set/copy/fill bounds checks, pe_downcall_invoke libffi call, upcall_dispatch arg decode), aot.rs serialize/deserialize + integrity hash + AotCacheEntry, xml_stax.rs parse_to_events + setProperty/factory config (XXE assessment), jmx_openmbean.rs descriptor parsers (read_one_descriptor + type_descriptor_to_class_mirror), spring_startup_bootstrap.rs env/bean-factory shims + feature gating, quarkus_arc.rs container/BeanManager resolution. SAMPLED via grep + targeted windows: jmx.rs (MBeanServer registration, confirmed real reimpl; only marker is a test unreachable!), graalvm_compat.rs (substitution registry + image-info booleans), logmanager.rs (markers all in tests), infinispan_local.rs (real CHM-backed local cache), jboss_module_loader.rs / jboss_msc.rs / wildfly_core.rs / quarkus_staticinit.rs / servlet.rs (stub-marker scan + unsafe-block scan only). NOT exhaustively read end-to-end (each 1.7K-4.4K LOC): servlet.rs NIO/socket dispatch bodies, jboss_module_loader resource/jar walking internals, logmanager handler formatting, jmx query/notification dispatch, vector_api lane ops (confirmed zero unsafe, scalar). Tests were counted by #[test] markers and integration-test filenames, NOT executed or instrumented — coverage % is a judged estimate. I did not run cargo test or build."

#### `nb-misc`

The nb-misc catch-all is mostly mature, real implementations (FIPS-197 AES, ChaCha, NewHope, sun EC, libffi-backed Panama, flate2-backed zip, OS-CSPRNG SecureRandom) rather than fakes — only ~4 genuine functional gaps survive the stub scan, and the 50 panic!/1 unreachable! sites are all test assertions or dead shims, none reachable from production input. Parsing/marshalling boundaries I deep-read (zip inflate, copySwapMemory, FFI struct-by-value, EC bigint decode, getnameinfo FFI) are correctly bounds-checked. The one HIGH-severity defect is a classic stale-OOP use-after-free in scheduled_pump.rs: it stores a Java runnable as a raw integer pointer and re-materializes it on a later timer tick with no GC root or post-compaction remap — the codebase clearly knows the correct pin/read pattern (sunec_point uses it), so this is an oversight, not a missing capability. Two MEDIUM/LOW from_raw-from-integer sites (concurrent_extras dead-but-present, cglib_enhancer reachable) fabricate ObjectRefs from alignment-checked integers and should be removed. Test breadth is good (514 inline tests + 95 integration tests + a solid mock) but I estimate ~72% — below the 85% target — chiefly because MockNativeContext's force_gc is a no-op, so the entire stale-pointer bug class (the most dangerous findings here) is structurally untestable; adding a relocating-GC test mode is the single highest-value test investment.

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:1, low:3, info:3
- **Stubs:** 66 markers, ~4 real functional gaps. Marker scan over all 58 in-scope files: 93 'stub' tokens (almost all are NativeKind::SyntheticStub category tags or doc text, NOT functional fakes), 50 panic! (ALL verified to sit after each file's #[cfg(test)] boundary — they are test assertions of the 'expected X got Y' form, none reachable in production), 9 'placeholder'/3 TODO/2 'not implemented'/1 unreachable!/1 XXX (the lone production unreachable! is zip_real.rs:468 in a dead #[allow(dead_code)] _unused_ret shim, never called). Genuine functional gaps reduce to ~4: direct-buffer Deflater no-ops, fake zip Adler, the feature-gated CUDA backend (always-Failed), and the wildfly placeholder-handle path. Crypto (bc_aes FIPS-197 tables, bc_chacha, sunec EC, securerandom LCG) and FFI marshalling are real implementations, not stubs.
- **Performance:**
  - zip_real read_byte_array/write_byte_array and craton_gpu/sunec array rebuilds do per-element virtual dispatch (get/set_array_element) on hot JAR-load/CRC paths — add a bulk byte[] copy API
  - securerandom SEED_TABLE and scheduled_pump task list grow monotonically until shutdown (resource creep under churn)
