#### `vm-exec-init`

Mature, defensively-written module. The unsafe surface (raw array/Unsafe memcpy paths, JNI dispatch, GC root fixups) is consistently bounds-checked with accurate SAFETY comments, and the JVMS §5.5 init machinery is correct on the common paths. No genuine functional stubs were found (all 60 marker hits are tests, documented invariants, or env-gated strict assertions). The most important finding is a HIGH liveness bug: when a class's initialization fails via an early-error path in initialize_class_shared (verify error, prepare error, superinterface-init error, or stdin-object error), the init claim and the condvar waiter are leaked — finalize_init only runs on the <clinit>-execution path — so a concurrent thread parked as a waiter stalls for the full 30s timeout. A MEDIUM spec/maintainability risk is the hardcoded JDK-25 java.lang.Class field-slot layout in the mirror builder, which will silently corrupt reflection on any JDK with a different field order. Test coverage is judged ~55% overall (lower, ~40%, when risk-weighted): excellent for layout/helper code but the clinit ordering integration tests are feature-gated off the default build, and neither the concurrent-init-failure path nor the unsafe bulk-copy bounds/humongous paths are tested — it does NOT clear the 85% target. Recommended priorities: (1) make init-failure cleanup unconditional (RAII/finalize-on-Err), (2) resolve Class-mirror slots by name, (3) add the concurrent-init-failure and bulk-copy bounds tests, and (4) un-gate real-JDK clinit ordering tests.

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:1, low:2, info:2
- **Stubs:** 60 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. 60 raw marker hits across scope, but ZERO are genuine functional stubs/fakes returning hardcoded results where real behavior is expected. Breakdown: vm_init.rs 43 (1 TODO comment, 2-3 unreachable on real invariants, remainder are panic! assertions inside the #[cfg(test)] module at 5152+), vm_exec.rs 12 (4 are 'placeholder' in comments, rest are test-module panics at 11675+ plus 2 doc-comment 'unsafe' references), vm_util.rs 3 (1 env-gated strict panic, 2 test panics), vm_object.rs 2 (both test panics). The only non-test, non-comment control-flow markers are legitimate precondition unreachable!()/panic! and the env-gated strict-swallow panic.
- **Performance:**
  - ensure_class_initialized_shared fast path is well-optimized: single class_manager.read() + Arc<AtomicU8>::load(Acquire), no side-table lock on the steady-state hit (vm_util.rs:241-285). Good.
  - Class mirror lookup caches num_fields in a process-wide atomic and the dbg-toarray env flag in a OnceLock to avoid per-call std::env::var allocation (vm_object.rs:480-535). Good.
  - read_byte_array_bulk / decode_java_string_value_array use a single copy_nonoverlapping instead of N boxed get_array_element calls for compact strings (vm_object.rs:262-336). Good fast path.
  - Minor: modstatic_dbg()/env probes inside ensure_class_initialized_shared (vm_util.rs:267-282) take class_manager.read() and to_string() even when the debug class name doesn't match; gated on env_cache so cheap, but the get_class+to_string runs on every slow-path entry when the flag is on.
  - The 30s condvar wait (vm_util.rs:387) is a latency cliff under concurrent init failures (see high-severity finding) rather than a throughput issue.
- **Strengths:** JVMS §5.5 initialization is faithfully implemented: super-before-self, same-thread reentry short-circuit, TOCTOU-safe claim under a write lock, default-method (not static-field) superinterface criterion with documented rationale, and correct Error-vs-ExceptionInInitializerError propagation.; Every unsafe block is bounds-checked with checked_add against array_length before the raw copy, handles the G1 humongous (no flat base) fallback, and carries an accurate SAFETY comment (vm_exec.rs:2390-2660, vm_object.rs:262-289).; JNI dispatch guards against descriptor/arg arity mismatch before entering the unsafe raw-fn-pointer call, returning UnsatisfiedLinkError instead of building a malformed C frame (vm_exec.rs:11421-11515).; verifier_skip_eligible correctly gates the Pass-3 verification shortcut on the defining-loader IDENTITY being Bootstrap (not just the name prefix), preventing a forged java/lang/* from bypassing verification (vm_util.rs:519-547).; The lenient-clinit swallow path is opt-in (CRATONVM_LENIENT_CLINIT), narrowed to a documented recovery allowlist, keeps an accurate swallow counter, and has a strict-escalation gate — JVMS-divergent behavior is well-contained and observable.; Class-init <clinit> execution is wrapped in catch_unwind so a Rust panic (e.g. operand-stack underflow) is converted to an error rather than aborting the VM (vm_util.rs:854-866).
- **Review depth:** Deep-read in full: vm_util.rs initialization machinery (ensure_class_initialized_shared 236-497, initialize_class_shared 584-866, finalize_init closure, lenient-clinit swallow 1060-1354, verifier_skip_eligible, interface_has_default_method) — this is the core focus area and was read end-to-end. Deep-read every unsafe block in scope: vm_object.rs (read_byte_array_bulk 262-336), vm_exec.rs raw-copy helpers (copy_to/from_native_memory 2169-2227, write/read byte/char array 2390-2568, bulk_array_copy 2570-2660), GC root-fixup loop (1225-1291), JNI dispatch + arity guard (11400-11530), native library dlopen (5277-5331), module_base debug (2086). Read get_or_create_class_mirror (479-600) and the lock-order verifier (4120-4160). Stub-scanned all four files exhaustively and classified every hit. SAMPLED (not exhaustive): vm_exec.rs is 12.8K lines and vm_init.rs 11.1K lines — I did not read them end to end; I grepped to locate risk (unsafe/transmute/panic/TODO) and read windows around each hit. NOT REACHED: the bulk of vm_exec.rs opcode-dispatch interpreter body and vm_init.rs Vm::new bootstrap wiring beyond new_object/new_array (4549-4575) were not line-by-line read — I relied on grep for risk markers there and on the init-ordering free functions in vm_util.rs which they delegate to. Tests: read clinit_order_tests.rs and the vm_util inline test region; counted inline #[test] across all four files; did NOT run the suite or measure real coverage (estimate is judged). I did not verify the GC root-fixup list (vm_exec.rs:1240-1290) against the GC's own root-scan set — a mismatch there would be a UAF but confirming it requires reading the GC module, which is out of scope.

#### `vm-jni-jvmti`

Solid, security-conscious JNI/JVMTI layer with well-documented unsafe invariants and several real bugs already fixed (array-element length recording, forged-local-ref validation, agent-panic isolation, arity guard). The dominant remaining risks are GC-rooting/pinning gaps at the native boundary: (1) the ClassFileTransformer chain holds live Java objects that are neither rooted nor remapped across a moving GC (UAF on redefine), (2) local refs created in a native method without an explicit PushLocalFrame are not rooted (no implicit frame is pushed on native entry), and (3) direct critical/array pointers are handed out on a 'heap never moves' assumption the VM does not actually hold. Functionally, the C-varargs JNI call family and DefineClass-from-bytes are silent stubs that a caller cannot detect, and weak global refs are strong. Test breadth is good for ordinary paths but the highest-risk code (ABI trampoline with FP/spill, moving-GC interactions, transformer rooting) is essentially untested, so risk-weighted coverage sits around 60-65% — below the 85% target. Recommend addressing the three GC-safety findings and adding GC-interaction + trampoline-ABI tests before declaring this module production-ready.

- **Findings:** high:3, medium:2, low:3, info:1
- **Stubs:** 9 markers, ~3 real functional gaps. Stub markers are sparse and mostly legitimate (precondition returns, the single jni_stub fallback, and panic!/fake_* confined to #[cfg(test)] blocks). Three are genuine FUNCTIONAL gaps a caller cannot detect: (1) the entire C-varargs JNI call family returns 0/null; (2) DefineClass silently substitutes a classpath class for the supplied bytecode; (3) weak global refs never clear. The non-x86_64 FP-dispatch refusal and the arity-mismatch guard fail LOUDLY (UnsatisfiedLinkError) and are correct defensive behaviour, not silent stubs.
- **Performance:**
  - Global-ref resolve/remove are O(n) Vec scans under a mutex on a very hot path (jni.rs:550-580) — switch to a HashSet/HashMap for O(1).
  - Get<Type>ArrayElements fast path correctly avoids the old O(n) per-call copy by handing out the live body pointer (good), but the GC-safety of that is unproven (see finding).
  - parse_param_types_cached caps the descriptor cache at 1024 then clear()s wholesale (jni.rs:289-293) — a periodic full-flush thrash under churn; an LRU/bounded map would avoid cold re-parse spikes.
  - jobject_to_obj takes the global-ref mutex AND, for local refs, calls heap.is_heap_addr on every resolve; both are per-handle-access costs — a real indirection handle table (the documented follow-up) would make resolve a bounds-checked array index.
- **Strengths:** Ref lifecycle ownership is carefully documented and the Box<ObjectRef> global-ref allocations are freed on remove and in a Drop impl (no leak on table teardown).; Release<Type>ArrayElements records (initialised_len, capacity) at Get time and never re-derives length from the handle, correctly avoiding the wrong-length from_raw_parts heap corruption (vm-jni-roots #2).; Local-ref resolution now validates the raw pointer via heap.is_heap_addr before deref, mitigating forged/stale handles (V3 fix).; Region accessors do explicit overflow-safe bounds checks (checked_add) and raise a real AIOOBE instead of silently truncating.; The naked x86-64 trampoline is carefully written with matching CallBlock offsets, correct shadow-space/alignment handling, and separate Win64/SysV variants; non-x86_64 fails loud on FP signatures rather than fabricating results.; JVMTI event fire_* paths wrap every agent callback in catch_unwind so a panicking agent cannot abort the VM.; The descriptor-arity guard in vm_exec.rs rejects descriptor/arg-count mismatch before the unsafe dispatch, closing a malformed-frame UB hole.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: jni.rs ref tables and lifecycle (lines ~88-200, 500-738, 759-810), jvalue/descriptor parsing (263-382), array elements/critical/region paths (2138-2595, 2762-2828, 3091-3271), the naked x86-64 trampoline + dispatch (3700-3973), function-table assembly (4980-5050), DefineClass; agent_loader.rs in full (spec parse, manifest attrs, premain dispatch, Boot-Class-Path); instrument.rs transformer-chain storage + invoke path (89-121, 339-420, 840) and confirmed (via grep of roots.rs/gc.rs) it is unrooted; vm_exec.rs native dispatch entry/exit (11400-11460) to confirm no implicit local frame. SAMPLED: jvmti.rs (4710 LOC) — capabilities/error enums, fire_* catch_unwind verified, redefine/retransform capability flags; did NOT read every JVMTI function body. serviceability.rs (3813 LOC) — header/role understood, HprofWriter usage, NOT read end-to-end. hprof.rs read for structure + intern/serial counters (no overflow guard on next_string_id/next_serial u64/u32 but practically unreachable); heap-walk record emission only skimmed. jvmti/mod.rs object-tag update_after_gc verified present. NOT REACHED: full bodies of every JVMTI/serviceability native, the complete HPROF segment-emission/heap-walk arithmetic, and the exception-state preconditions on every JNI accessor (spot-checked Throw/ThrowNew/ExceptionCheck wiring only). GC-safety findings are based on static cross-referencing (collect_roots/gc.rs grep) not a running repro; the moving-GC-vs-critical-pointer and transformer-chain findings are contingent on a moving collector being active during the window, which the project's GC zoo makes reachable.

#### `vm-threading`

The vm-threading module is mature and unusually careful for a from-scratch JVM concurrency layer. The monitor implementation (thin-lock fast path + inflation + heavyweight Monitor) is the strongest part: CAS orderings are correct, the notify+interrupt lost-wakeup hazard is explicitly forwarded, monitorexit raises the spec IMSE before decrement, and GC re-keying of monitors / async-exception slots / blocked-thread roots is rigorous and STW-bounded. The stub scan is essentially clean — no production unimplemented!/fake-natives, and all 10 unsafe blocks are well-justified raw-address round-trips with sound invariants. The most actionable issues are: (1) the un-gated AtomicOperations::get_and_* helpers that silently never publish to the heap (medium — latent footgun, only test-called today but inconsistent with their properly-gated CAS siblings); (2) the per-VT-sleep OS-thread spawn that defeats virtual-thread scaling (medium perf, already a known TODO); and (3) poisonable std-Mutex registries whose .expect() turns any panic-while-locked into a VM-wide abort (low availability). Test breadth is high (~290 inline tests + 6 integration suites) but multi-threaded interleaving coverage of the hardest invariants (lost-wakeup forwarding, contended inflation, GC-remap-while-blocked) is thin, so I estimate ~80% coverage — just under the 85% target for a concurrency-critical module. No memory-safety or reachable-DoS-from-untrusted-input bug was found in the reviewed paths. Recommended before sign-off: gate/delete the get_and_* helpers, add loom-style stress tests for wait/notify/inflation/GC-remap, and consider parking_lot or poison-recovery for the registries."

- **Findings:** medium:2, low:1, info:1
- **Stubs:** 5 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. Stub scan across the whole scope returned only 5 marker hits and ~10 `unsafe` occurrences (very low for ~11.6K LOC). Of the 5 markers, 4 are in test code (panic isolation / assertion arms) and 1 is a genuine performance TODO that is already captured as a finding. There are ZERO unimplemented!/todo!/unreachable! in production paths and NO natives that silently fake a result in this module — the one 'sketch that never publishes' code (AtomicOperations) is documented and partially gated; the un-gated get_and_* subset is the medium finding above. All 10 unsafe blocks are confined to monitor.rs (2: ObjectHeader cast from ObjectRef, Arc-ptr cast for inflation) and thread_registry.rs (5: ObjectRef::from_raw round-trips of GC-pointer-map / async-exception-slot addresses, all scanned-as-root and remapped at STW) and forkjoin.rs (2: Send/Sync for PoolTask whose task_id is a never-dereferenced usize). Each carries a SAFETY comment and the invariants hold under review.
- **Performance:**
  - schedule_wakeup spawns one OS thread per VT Thread.sleep (virtual_threads.rs:817-859) — replace with a single timer-wheel thread; defeats VT scaling for sleep-heavy workloads.
  - Monitor registry / cas-lock registry are global FxHashMaps behind a single L6 mutex (monitor.rs:787,797); every inflated monitorenter/exit/wait does a map lookup under that one lock — fine for the thin-lock fast path (which never touches it) but a contention point for inflation-heavy workloads. Consider sharding the registry by address bucket if inflation churn shows up in profiles.
  - remap_after_gc drains and rebuilds both registries on every moving GC (monitor.rs:1477,1524); O(n) in registry size per collection. Acceptable, but the reclaim path is gated off by default (reclaim_dead_monitors_enabled) so dead-object monitors accumulate unless the opt-in is set — a slow leak / growing O(n) GC walk for long-running monitor-heavy apps (consistent with the LHM-overlay GC-walk note in project memory).
- **Strengths:** Thin-lock fast path (try_thin_lock/try_thin_recursive_lock/try_thin_unlock, monitor.rs:156-243) is CAS-based, allocation-free, and correctly uses Acquire/Release/Relaxed; the uncontended and single-thread-reentrant cases never touch the global registry.; The lost-wakeup hazard in Object.wait under simultaneous notify+interrupt is explicitly recognized and handled by forwarding the single notification (monitor.rs:634-651, 682-691) — a subtle JLS-conformance point most implementations get wrong.; GC safety of monitor and async-exception state is rigorous: monitors re-key on object move (monitor.rs:1477-1510), async-exception slots are scanned as roots AND remapped (thread_registry.rs:566-572, 620-629), and blocked-thread roots use ABA-safe fixup chaining (thread_registry.rs:692-714); all remap work runs at STW.; Inflation is CAS-published under the registry mutex with re-snapshot, and the pathological 'INFLATED mark but missing registry entry' case is surfaced as an error rather than silently leaking a Monitor / breaking per-object identity (monitor.rs:841-913).; monitorexit checks entry_count==0 BEFORE decrement to raise the spec-correct IllegalMonitorStateException instead of a silent saturating wrap (monitor.rs:522-525).; Lock-ordering is enforced via OrderedMutex at LockLevel::Monitors with a documented hierarchy (docs/lock-order.md), catching descending-order violations in debug builds.; The genuinely-unsafe sketch (AtomicOperations CAS variants) was already identified and neutralized (#[cfg(test)] + deprecated + runtime panic, monitor.rs round-9 CRIT-3), showing prior concurrency-audit hygiene.
- **Review depth:** Deep-read in full: monitor.rs thin-lock + Monitor enter/exit/wait/notify + MonitorTable inflate_locked/enter_or_contend/exit/remap_after_gc (lines 140-1147, 1453-1560) — the core deadlock/lost-wakeup/GC-safety surface; varhandle.rs ordering map (260-344), fences (350-383), and the AtomicOperations block (385-595); jvm_thread.rs park/unpark/park_interruptible (121-195); thread_registry.rs all 5 unsafe sites and GC remap paths (255-330, 555-640, 680-716); forkjoin.rs Send/Sync + pool construction (40-114); lock_order.rs OrderedMutex (239-300). Sampled via grep (not read end-to-end): jmm.rs (only the fence/happens-before/race-detect signatures, 1-120 + grep) — its 44 tests and fence mappings looked correct but I did not line-audit the happens-before vector-clock arithmetic; virtual_threads.rs (read carriers, park_virtual, schedule_wakeup/cancel, ~575-915; the remaining ~1400 lines incl. continuation mounting and the 75 inline tests were sampled by grep only); event_loop.rs (1419 LOC — only confirmed no real unsafe and inventoried, NOT read for logic); gc_barrier.rs, scheduled.rs, stamped.rs, virtual_scheduler.rs (inventoried + stub-scanned only, not logic-reviewed). NOT reached: the full virtual-thread continuation/yield state machine, jmm vector-clock correctness, event_loop task scheduling internals, and any cross-module interaction with the JIT safepoint emitter (out of scope path). Coverage % is a judgement from test counts + which invariants have stress coverage, not instrumented. No build/test run was performed (review-only).

#### `vm-jit-integ`

This module is mature, defensively coded, and unusually well-documented for a from-scratch JIT/GC glue layer — the live unsafe surface (JIT array/field/alloc helpers, conservative root scanner, root remap) is consistently guarded with null + bounds checks, correct GC barrier ordering, and spec-aligned exception signalling, backed by a solid null/OOB unit-test battery and a large external differential suite. The most material code issue is HIGH: jit_anewarray_object and jit_multianewarray_2d still call the non-fallible heap.alloc_array, which can assert!/abort on oversized lengths or OOM and — because the crate is panic=\"unwind\" — would unwind a panic across an extern \"C\" boundary into JIT frames (UB / DoS reachable from JIT-compiled anewarray/multianewarray with attacker-influenced sizes); jit_newarray was already hardened against exactly this and these two siblings must be brought to parity. Secondary findings are a pair of unused parallel-model modules (gc_integration.rs with a simulated address allocator, jit_integration.rs) that masquerade as production GC/JIT infrastructure and inflate apparent test coverage, plus the explicitly-unimplemented cross-thread STW JIT root scan and the convention-dependent JIT-scan-cache invalidation invariant (both documented and mitigated, but fragile). Risk-weighted test coverage of the *live* code is judged ~78%, below the 85% target, with the precise RBP-chain remap, the full conservative frame walk, the alloc-OOM/large-array paths, and most of gc.rs's 20+ root tables lacking direct tests. Recommended for readiness: fix the multianewarray/anewarray alloc hardening, quarantine or delete the dead simulation modules, and add the allocation-OOM, precise-remap, and JIT-scan-cache-staleness regression tests."

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:1, low:2, info:2
- **Stubs:** 11 markers, ~2 real functional gaps. Stub-scan hits: 5 panic!/unreachable! (all in #[cfg(test)] modules: conservative_roots.rs:743/1350, gc.rs:813/869/877/884), 4 TODO/FIXME that are documented-and-correct fallbacks not gaps (helpers.rs:682/710 = return None → interpreter bail for 4+/5+-arg callees, correct-but-slower; helpers.rs:1158/1207 = resolved FIXMEs now routed through orchestrated STW), 1 TODO(vec_pool_stats) at alloc_fastpath.rs:39 (cosmetic, gated behind AtomicBool already). No unimplemented!/todo!/NYI in production code. Genuine functional gaps: (1) the unused gc_integration.rs + jit_integration.rs parallel models (simulated allocator returning fake addresses, no-op vs real GC) — flagged as stub/readiness findings; (2) the explicitly-unimplemented cross-thread STW JIT root scan (documented, mitigated). NOT counted as gaps: every JIT array/field helper that 'returns 0' does so deliberately and pairs it with a pending-NPE/AIOOBE/exception flag drained by the interpreter (verified across baload/iaload/aaload/bastore/iastore/aastore/getfield/putfield) — spec-correct, not silent fabrication (the comments document the prior silent-0 bugs that were fixed).
- **Performance:**
  - DeoptimizationManager.record_deopt rescans the unbounded deopt_history Vec per call (O(n) per deopt → O(n^2)) and never trims it — only matters if jit_integration.rs is ever made live (currently dead).
  - jit_putfield_object reads num_slots from the header twice in trace mode and once otherwise via jit_putfield_slot_in_bounds — fine; but the bounds read is a separate header load on every reference-field store (acknowledged in comments as cheaper than heap.num_fields virtual dispatch).
  - Conservative JIT-frame scan range [scanner_sp, entry_sp] can span megabytes when a long-lived JIT frame sits near the stack bottom; the JIT-scan cache (conservative_roots.rs) is the mitigation and is correctly keyed, but its soundness depends on the invalidate-before-authoritative-scan convention (see Info finding).
  - gc.rs update_all_roots step 14 rebuilds the entire class_mirrors_reverse map (clear + re-insert all) on every moving GC even when few mirrors moved — O(classes) per GC.
- **Strengths:** JIT array/field unsafe helpers (baload/iaload/aaload/bastore/iastore/aastore/getfield/putfield_*) are uniformly null-checked AND slot/index bounds-checked before any raw read/write, with the OOB path matching interpreter semantics (pending NPE/AIOOBE flag + i64::MIN sentinel) instead of silently fabricating values — and each has a dedicated unit test.; jit_putfield_object / jit_aastore implement the correct GC barrier triad and spec ordering: NPE → AIOOBE → ArrayStoreException covariance → SATB pre-write barrier (read old ref before overwrite) → store → card-table post barrier.; conservative_roots.rs is exceptionally well-documented about its safety envelope: reads stack qwords as opaque addresses validated via is_object_address before use (no deref-as-reference), bounds the scan to MAX_SCAN_BYTES, aligns reads to 8 bytes, and self-heals leaked JIT entries via the provably-returned (entry_sp < scanner_sp) predicate.; Env-var gates on hot paths (skip-list allow-packages, dbg_no_prune, jit_scan_cache_enabled, bisect hooks) are cached behind OnceLock/AtomicBool to avoid per-call GetEnvironmentVariableW syscalls.; alloc_fastpath.rs VecPool is a clean, well-tested sharded pool (parking_lot, power-of-two shard mask, stat-gating) with thorough concurrency and cap tests.; skip_list.rs is a single-source-of-truth eligibility predicate with no unsafe, clear roadmap-tied rationale per ban, and 30 unit tests.; gc.rs update_all_roots is a comprehensive, heavily-commented remap of 20+ root categories with debug_assert!(new_addr != 0) guards on every pointer-map application.
- **Review depth:** Deep-read in full: conservative_roots.rs, gc.rs, alloc_fastpath.rs, gc_integration.rs, jit_integration.rs (the four smaller files end-to-end incl. tests). helpers.rs (5386 lines) was NOT read end-to-end — I grepped for all unsafe/transmute/from_raw_parts/raw-write/get_unchecked/alloc sites and deep-read the windows around every hit: the call-table transmutes (656-714), the allocation helpers (1114-1552, incl. jit_newarray/anewarray/multianewarray), every array load/store helper (1561-1852), getfield/putfield bounds + barriers (1928-2244), getstatic/checkcast/instanceof (2253-2660), and the invoke-dispatch arg-slice construction (3150-3185, 3543-3575). I did NOT line-by-line read: the bulk of jit_invoke_dispatch/jit_invoke_virtual_mic dispatch logic (2700-3072, 3185-3543, 4193-4300 — skimmed for lock usage and the args null-guard only), jit_uncommon_trap, and the helpers.rs test module beyond enumerating test names. skip_list.rs: read the predicate + env parsing and skimmed the long per-package ban table (not every entry). I did not run the test suite or build. The High finding (multianewarray/anewarray non-fallible alloc → panic across extern \"C\" under panic=unwind) was cross-verified against gc/src/heap.rs and Cargo.toml. Cross-thread STW JIT root correctness and the JIT-scan-cache staleness were assessed from the (extensive) in-source documentation plus MEMORY.md history, not from a running multi-thread repro.

#### `vm-runtime-misc`

Solid, security-conscious module overall. The async-signal-safety discipline in crash_handler.rs and the input-hardening in debug/protocol.rs are above average and clearly had prior review passes. config, signals (data structures), lock_order, jdk_layout and the logging/threading helpers are clean. ONE high-severity memory-safety defect stands out: the GPU zero-copy direct_xfer! path (default-on under the gpu-offload feature) bypasses the element-type/kind assertions that the staged path enforces and feeds an unvalidated array_data_ptr into from_raw_parts[_mut], enabling out-of-bounds heap reads (info leak to the device) and, on download, out-of-bounds heap WRITES (corruption) if a wrong-typed/non-array object ever reaches it. It is currently masked by element-type-keyed dispatch in the single caller, but the invariant is unchecked and the functions are pub. Fix by re-adding the kind+element_type assert (and/or making array_data_ptr type-aware). Secondary items: harden the signal-handler write_all EINTR loop, optionally enable lock-order checks in release for deadlock hunts, add a JDWP handshake read timeout, and switch unified_logging to a HashMap handle table. Test coverage is strong for pure-logic code but the riskiest unsafe surfaces (GPU marshalling mismatch, the signal/VEH crash paths, device dispatch) are the least tested; judged ~78%, below the 85% target, with the specific missing regression test being exactly the wrong-element-type gpu_marshal case.

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:2, low:3, info:2
- **Stubs:** 14 markers, ~2 real functional gaps. Stub-marker grep across scope found 14 hits (13 in runtime/*, 1 in debug/mod.rs); config.rs had zero. The OVERWHELMING majority are NOT functional gaps: (a) the documented 'placeholder' comments in offload.rs describe the analyzer's fixed estimated_work=1<<20 heuristic (a design note, not a missing impl); (b) jdk_layout.rs placeholders are an intentional feature (generating fake JDK dir stubs so IDEs recognize the runtime); (c) every unreachable!()/panic! in signals.rs/soak_test.rs/offload.rs is either a legitimate precondition (lock_order.rs:224 level_from_u8 only called with i<COUNT; protocol length-checked) or lives in #[cfg(test)] mock impls. GENUINE functional gaps: (1) soak_test.rs:239 cpu_percent hardcoded 0.0 (CPU sampling unimplemented); (2) signals.rs NPE analyzer emits placeholder CP-index names instead of resolved names (but it is the fallback, not the live path). No native returning fake-0/null where real behaviour is expected on a hot correctness path was found in scope.
- **Performance:**
  - unified_logging.rs:664 O(paths) linear handle lookup under mutex on every log line; switch to HashMap
  - offload.rs:496 usize->u32 truncation in the work gate (correctness-leaning, low perf impact)
  - gpu_marshal bulk copy_nonoverlapping paths are already the fast path (good); zero-copy avoids a full array memcpy per direction but at the cost of the missing type guard (the high finding)
- **Strengths:** JDWP protocol parser (debug/protocol.rs) is well hardened against malicious input: length<HEADER_SIZE rejected (no subtraction overflow at :139), read_bounded caps payload allocation (prior VULN fix at :48-70), PayloadReader is fully bounds-checked (:330-389); Unix crash signal handler (crash_handler.rs:1313-1397) follows strict async-signal-safety discipline: stack-only buffers, allocation-free itoa_into_buf, raw libc write/open/close only, CAS re-entry guard, documented whitelist in the module header; gpu_marshal staged paths (host_view_*/write_back_*) assert kind+element_type and length equality before any unsafe copy_nonoverlapping; SafepointToken marker enforces GC-paused invariant at the type level; Windows VEH is_readable() probes memory via VirtualQuery with saturating arithmetic before dumping raw bytes, and symbolize() sizes its SYMBOL_INFO buffer (2560B) larger than max_name_len(2000)+header so dbghelp cannot overflow it; config.rs env parsing is robust: RJ_MAX_STACK_DEPTH is range-filtered (64..=65536) and falls back on parse failure; XverifyMode/CdsMode parsers return Option and warn-and-default on unknown input; Debug JDWP transport binds to 127.0.0.1 (not 0.0.0.0), limiting remote attack surface; Very strong inline unit-test density across the data-structure/parsing modules (545 test fns in scope)
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: signals.rs (1033 LOC), gpu_marshal.rs (830, incl. the direct_xfer! macro that yielded the high finding), debug/protocol.rs (591), debug/transport.rs (181), lock_order.rs core (180-337), crash_handler.rs unsafe surfaces (Unix signal handler, Windows VEH, is_readable, symbolize, itoa). SAMPLED via grep + windowed reads: offload.rs (2976 LOC — read the transparent-dispatch gate, work-len gate, try_unbox, resident_arm! Arc-as-ptr closure; did NOT read all 2976 lines, the kernel-codegen/analyzer internals 900-2400 only grepped), config.rs (parsers read, classpath-normalization logic only grepped), unified_logging/soak_test/jdk_layout (targeted windows around grep hits, not end-to-end). NOT DEEP-READ (test-count + grep only, no window reads): tck.rs (6055 LOC, an internal javac/junit harness — not on the attack surface), threading_integration.rs (1278), debug/commands.rs (2434), debug/events.rs (839), debug/mod.rs (1759), debug/ids.rs (276). I confirmed gc/src/vm_heap.rs:665 to establish that array_data_ptr does not type-check, which sets the high finding's severity. The high finding's real-world reachability depends on offload.rs callers always dispatching by element_type (they currently do, at :2720); I did not exhaustively prove no other pub caller exists across the whole crate beyond the grep that found only vm.rs/vtable.rs/gpu_marshal.rs referencing _direct. Coverage % is a judgment from test density + risk weighting, not instrumented (no tarpaulin/llvm-cov run)."

#### `vm-misc`

The vm-misc catch-all is in good shape. It is overwhelmingly clean helper/metadata code with strong inline test coverage and zero genuine functional stubs (all 23 stub-markers are legitimate placeholders/platform-fallbacks/debug-panics). The raw-pointer surface is small, debug-env-gated, and carries documented SAFETY invariants — no attacker-reachable memory-safety defect was found. The one finding that matters is HIGH: the ObjectStreamClass cache (osc_cache) stores heap ObjectRefs but, unlike every other process-global ObjectRef side-table in the VM, is neither scanned as a GC root nor remapped after a moving collection (roots.rs sections 15-20 scan all the others). Under the default moving young-gen GC a discarded-then-re-looked-up descriptor can dangle — a textbook instance of the stale-pointer SIGSEGV class this codebase has fought repeatedly, and serialization-heavy apps (Keycloak) are the trigger. Fix is mechanical: add the scan + remap pair mirroring gc_scan/gc_update_annotation_proxy_refs. Two LOW issues (an unused FxHash-keyed ResolutionCache with a collision footgun; VarHandle Opaque->Relaxed) and two INFO nits round it out. Tests land ~80% — just under the 85% target, dragged down by the untested debug-gated pointer modules and the absent multi-GC-cycle osc_cache regression. Recommend: ship the osc_cache GC fix + its regression test before readiness sign-off; optionally delete the dead fx_collections::ResolutionCache."

- **Findings:** high:1, low:2, info:2
- **Stubs:** 23 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. Stub scan across the full in-scope set found 23 marker hits and ZERO genuine functional gaps. Every hit is either (a) a benchmark-baseline 'placeholder' (a real, documented bootstrap convention in the two bin/ harnesses), (b) a legitimate platform fallback (container.rs non-Linux), (c) a deliberate precondition/debug panic, or (d) a test-only panic!()/unreachable. No native in scope silently fakes a result where real behavior is expected. The CRATONVM_REAL differential switch (env_cache.rs) correctly implements the project's real-bytecode-by-default policy.
- **Performance:**
  - fx_collections::ResolutionCache.get_method recomputes 3 FxHash digests per lookup (fx_collections.rs:198-200) but this type is not on the hot path (unused in production); no action needed beyond removal.
  - roots.rs collect_roots() is O(roots) and acquires ~10 RwLock read guards + iterates every static-field vector and every process-global side-table on each collection (roots.rs:99-403). This is inherent to a precise+conservative hybrid root scan and is correct; the main cost driver (statics iteration over all classes) is unavoidable without per-class dirty tracking. Noted, not a defect.
  - OscCache.get_or_insert_with builds the descriptor outside the write lock then drops the loser on a race (oscache.rs:87-98) — correct and contention-friendly; no change.
  - container.rs read_cgroup_file canonicalizes on every read (container.rs:115) — fine, detection runs once at startup.
- **Strengths:** Pure-logic helper modules (proxy, varhandle, call_stack, stackwalker, lang_reflect_constructor, container, fx_collections, diagnostics, unsafe_helpers) are clean, panic-tolerant on malformed input (descriptors return 0/None rather than panicking), and densely unit-tested.; Every raw-pointer / unsafe module in scope is either debug-env-gated (stwhang_watch, ec_watch, memwatch) with documented SAFETY invariants, or a thin well-justified FFI shim (jvmti/agent via libloading). No unguarded transmutes or attacker-reachable OOB found.; roots.rs demonstrates a mature, exhaustively-documented GC-root discipline: 20+ enumerated root sources each with an explicit remap companion reference in gc.rs and a paper-trail of the SIGSEGV each one fixed. The osc_cache omission is the single gap in an otherwise complete pattern.; The CRATONVM_REAL differential switch (env_cache.rs) cleanly implements the project's 'real Java bytecode by default, synthetic = opt-in' policy with O(1) per-class lookup.; Bench harnesses (bin/) parse only trusted local files, are exit-code-driven, and carry 37 inline tests between them.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: oscache.rs, roots.rs, container.rs, varhandle.rs, stackwalker.rs, call_stack.rs, proxy.rs, fx_collections.rs, diagnostics.rs, unsafe_helpers.rs, memwatch.rs, ec_watch.rs, and the high-risk windows of stwhang_watch.rs (raw CONTEXT/stack walk), jvmti/agent.rs (FFI dlopen path), build_tool_compat.rs (XML parser), dispatch_trace.rs (ring-buffer index math), env_cache.rs (CRATONVM_REAL selector), lang_reflect_constructor.rs (descriptor parse). SAMPLED (head + targeted grep, not end-to-end): shared_secrets.rs, lambda_proxy.rs, gpu_residency.rs, lib.rs, bench_gate.rs/bench_hotspot_compare.rs, jck_capture.rs, jvmti/events.rs, jvmti/capabilities.rs. NOT deeply read (trivial re-export/facade shims, <35 LOC each): runtime/mod.rs, serialization/mod.rs, native/{registry,io,ffi,collections,mod}.rs, memory/mod.rs, classloading/mod.rs, types/{mod,value}.rs, error.rs. The osc_cache GC finding was confirmed by cross-referencing roots.rs (no scan), grepping all of vm/src+memory+native-builtins for any osc remap (none found), and reading serialization.rs:3060-3086 to confirm the descriptor is a discardable heap object. I did NOT execute the test suite or a moving-GC repro to dynamically confirm the osc_cache UAF — the finding is by static analysis against the established GC-root pattern. Excluded per scope: vm.rs, interpreter.rs, all vm/{vm_exec,vm_init,vm_util,vm_object}.rs, threading/*, jit/*, jvmti/mod.rs, native/jni.rs, config.rs, memory/gc.rs, and the other named runtime/* files owned by sibling agents."

#### `vm-cli`

vm-cli is a single 4.4K-line entrypoint (main.rs) plus 9 integration tests, and it is one of the more mature, security-aware modules in the tree. The argument-parsing layer — the module's primary risk area — is excellent: a carefully staged pre-clap pipeline with ~50 unit tests covering JDK-launcher positional edge cases, overflow-safe size parsing, and class-name validation. There are NO functional stubs and only two minimal, correct unsafe blocks (one Windows FFI, one gated self-test); no raw-pointer/transmute/JIT/GC code lives here. No critical or high-severity findings. The findings are all low/info: a mitigated-but-imperfect predictable temp-jar staging path (readability + DoS residue on a shared host), the always-on watchdog that can abort a slow-but-live compute job past 120s, an uncapped @-argfile read, and the order-coupled -D extraction. The main readiness gaps are test-coverage holes, not bugs: the ~950-line run() body, the ~650-line uncaught-exception/cause-chain renderer, and the untrusted-input @-argfile parser have essentially no assertions, putting judged coverage around 62% — below the 85% target. Recommended priorities for readiness: (1) add output-asserting tests for the exception renderer and argfile tokenizer, (2) reconsider/scope the default-on abort watchdog, (3) harden temp-jar staging into a 0700 per-process dir."

- **Findings:** low:2, info:4
- **Stubs:** 4 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. Exhaustive grep for unimplemented!/todo!/unreachable!/panic!/stub/NYI/TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX/placeholder across vm-cli/src and vm-cli/tests. In src/main.rs there are ZERO functional stubs: no unimplemented!/todo!/unreachable!, and no native faking hardcoded results (this is the CLI entrypoint, not a native impl). The only `panic!` occurrences are in test helpers (tests/common/mod.rs:39,63,75) as legitimate fixture-setup failures, and tests/cli_uncaught_exception.rs:20 has a TODO in a doc-comment describing a future renderer improvement (cfg(real_jdk_renderer)). Two places in main.rs are explicitly-reviewed intentional degradations, NOT silent wrong-result stubs: skipping initPhase2/3 (lines ~2171-2178, level contract preserved end-to-end) and omitting `\tat` frames when stackTrace[] is null (lines ~2493-2501, real class/message/cause always printed, frames never fabricated). Both are documented and roadmapped. The intentional null-deref at main.rs:3174 is gated behind CRATONVM_TEST_SEGV=1 for fault-handler self-test (dead on normal runs).
- **Performance:**
  - Cold-path only: the ~650-line uncaught-exception renderer re-walks superclass field lists under repeated class_manager.read() guards (main.rs:2523-2879) — runs once at process teardown, negligible.
  - Hot-path conscious: the per-native-call recording rings are gated behind explicit opt-in (ring_recording_requested) rather than armed by the always-on default watchdog (main.rs:1860-1933) — a deliberate perf fix.
  - quarkus_signature_present sniff (main.rs:386-425) gates the expensive multi-dir canonicalize+read_dir classpath walk behind a few cheap Path::exists() stats so non-Quarkus -jar startups skip it — good startup-latency win.
  - expand_aggregate_jars does a cheap filename match before any stat/read_dir syscall (main.rs:435-450).
  - mimalloc installed as global allocator for the VM's tiny-object workload.
- **Strengths:** Argument-parsing pipeline is exceptionally careful and well-tested: insert_program_args_separator, normalize_java_launcher_argv, extract_system_properties, extract_hotspot_flags each have thorough unit tests including regression guards for subtle JDK-launcher positional semantics (-- handling, -mp, -Xms separate-token swallow, unknown -X not consuming the main class).; Security-conscious temp-file staging: uses OpenOptions::create_new(true) with a 16-attempt retry to avoid symlink-follow/clobber when materializing non-.jar archives — the classic local arbitrary-write vector is explicitly closed.; parse_size uses checked_mul and rejects 0/overflow rather than wrapping or panicking; covered by an overflow test.; validate_class_name rejects empty/double-slash/leading-trailing-slash/special-char names before load, with good negative tests.; Only two unsafe blocks (Windows GlobalMemoryStatusEx FFI with a correctly-sized #[repr(C)] struct, and a gated CRATONVM_TEST_SEGV self-test) — both minimal and correct; no raw-pointer arithmetic, transmute, or JIT codegen in this module.; Honest, well-documented degradations (initPhase2/3 skip, missing stack frames) that explicitly avoid fabricating results — labelled INTENTIONAL (reviewed) with roadmap references.; Visibility-first panic hook + Windows VEH hardware-fault handler ensure crashes/hangs are never silent — a clear readiness win.; No functional stubs in the CLI: clean stub scan.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ IN FULL: all of vm-cli/src/main.rs (4447 lines, read across 3 windows — arg-parsing helpers + run() body + error renderer + main()/panic-hook + parse_size/physical_ram_bytes/ergonomic heap + the entire #[cfg(test)] module) and all 9 integration test files plus tests/common/mod.rs. STUB SCAN: exhaustive grep across the whole scope (4 hits, all benign, categorized). UNSAFE AUDIT: both unsafe blocks read in full (Windows FFI struct at 3375-3409 verified #[repr(C)] field-correct and size-correct; gated SEGV self-test at 3170-3178). VERIFIED EXTERNALLY: MethodCallFailed enum is 2-variant so the final match is exhaustive (types/src/error.rs); Cargo edition is 2021 so std::env::set_var is safe (no compile/UB concern). NOT REACHED / OUT OF SCOPE: the cratonvm_vm / cratonvm_native_builtins crates that run() calls into (Vm::new, invoke, heap, dump_missing_natives_json, agent_loader, env_cache, panama gate) were treated as trusted boundaries — I confirmed call signatures but did not audit their internals (outside the stated vm-cli scope). Coverage % is a judged estimate (no instrumentation run); the heavy run() body and the exception renderer are the main untested risk and drive the sub-85% estimate."

#### `jfr`

The jfr module is one of the more mature, security-conscious subsystems I have reviewed in this codebase. The attacker-facing surface (parsing untrusted .jfr files and the hand-rolled lock-free SPSC ring) is the right thing to worry about, and it is genuinely well-defended: the parser systematically uses checked arithmetic, record-boundary-bounded slices, an OOM count guard, offset validation, and a file-size cap, each with a SECURITY rationale; the ring's unsafe is documented invariant-by-invariant with correct Acquire/Release pairing and runtime-enforced SPSC discipline. There are no functional stubs — the module does real work end to end, and its two real limitations (bespoke non-stock metadata encoding and absent stack-trace serialization) are loudly documented rather than hidden. The one finding that warrants action before calling this hardened is the medium-severity residual use-after-free in the wedged-consumer Drop path (repository.rs:838-849): freeing the slot Box while a parked consumer can still dereference into it is UB, and the code's own comment downgrades it to a 'leaked slot' when it is in fact a (narrow, hard-to-hit) UAF; the minimal fix is to leak the storage on the wedged path or tie storage lifetime to the consumer's Arc. The remaining findings are low/info. Test coverage is strong on the encoder/decoder, malformed-input attack cases, and basic ring concurrency, but I estimate ~82% — just under the 85% target — held down by the absence of a loom/stress model for the unsafe ring, no test of the wedged-Drop UAF window, and no record-boundary-crossing or fuzz coverage of the field decoder. Adding those three test classes would both raise coverage past 85% and gate the finding-1 fix."

- **Findings:** medium:1, low:1, info:2
- **Stubs:** 12 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. Stub scan found ZERO genuine functional stubs (natives faking results). All 12 markers are either legitimate test-side panics or one 'placeholder header' that is a real back-patch pattern, not a stub. The two real feature gaps (non-stock metadata encoding, no stack-trace serialization) are loudly documented in dump.rs:480-521 and reported as info findings — they are honest, scoped limitations rather than hidden stubs.
- **Performance:**
  - Hot emit path (recording.rs:362-374 record_event) is well-optimized: a single running_ids.is_empty() check short-circuits when no recording is active, and the per-thread SPSC ring keeps push O(1) and uncontended; the producer's cached_tail fast path (repository.rs:537-563) avoids the Acquire load of the consumer counter in the common non-full case — good.
  - Serialization avoids per-call allocation: serialize_event_into reuses a single scratch Vec (dump.rs:857) and write_compressed_int_into/encode_compressed_int_to_buf write into stack/caller buffers instead of allocating a Vec per integer (dump.rs:140-174). The StringPool dedups repeated string payloads to a single Arc<[u8]> entry + pool index, cutting both bytes-on-wire and heap churn (dump.rs:237-282).
  - EventStream::poll/next_event were already converted from O(n) VecDeque front-walks (iter().skip / iter().nth) to O(1) repo.get(rel) indexing (stream.rs:143-151, 223-231) — prior quadratic-drain regressions are fixed.
  - Minor: kinds_by_type (dump.rs:870-885) rebuilds FieldKind vectors per dump via registry lookups; fine for typical type counts. drain_all allocates a fresh Vec per drain pass — acceptable given drains are infrequent relative to pushes.
  - No lock on the emit hot path at all (lock-free SPSC shards); locking is pushed to the much-rarer dump/drain path under the caller's RwLock. This is the right contention profile for JFR.
- **Strengths:** The untrusted-input parser (read_events / parse_checkpoint_pool / decode_event_value) is exceptionally well-hardened: every length/offset from the file is checked_add + usize::try_from before use, slices are bounded against record_end so a decode cannot cross record boundaries, the constant-pool entry count is rejected when it exceeds bytes-remaining (OOM guard), checkpoint/metadata offsets are validated <= data.len() before use, the varint decoder rejects >64-bit encodings, and a 2 GiB file cap is enforced before allocation. Each guard carries a SECURITY comment explaining the attack it blocks.; The SpscEventRing unsafe is carefully reasoned with explicit Acquire/Release pairing documented at every atomic access, a runtime-enforced single-producer invariant (THREAD_REGISTERED_RING) and a consumer_busy CAS gate serializing consumers — and the rationale for each unsafe impl Sync/Send is written out.; Zero genuine stubs: the module ships real behavior end-to-end; the two real limitations (non-stock metadata, no stack-trace capture) are honestly and prominently documented rather than silently faked.; Atomic durable writes: dumps go to a <name>.jfr.part file with a Drop-guard cleanup and an fsync-then-atomic-rename, so a crash mid-dump never corrupts an existing .jfr.; Strong regression-comment discipline — each fix records the bug it closed (silent event loss now counted via dropped, Null self-describing fixed-width encoding to keep the reader byte-aligned, CRIT filter-drop fixes), which materially aided this review.
- **Review depth:** DEEP-READ in full: the entire SpscEventRing (repository.rs:336-849) including all 11 unsafe blocks, push/try_pop/drain_into and the wedged-consumer Drop; the complete varint encode/decode (dump.rs:114-216); the full untrusted-input parser path — decode_event_value, parse_checkpoint_pool, read_events, read_jfr_header, read_jfr_file_capped (dump.rs:949-1522); serialize_event_into + write_size_prefixed + StringPool (dump.rs:371-468, 237-282); the GlobalMemoryStatusEx FFI (builtin.rs:2835-2866); EventStream cursor logic (stream.rs:1-243); recording drain/filter fan-out (recording.rs:315-440). SAMPLED via grep: builtin.rs (5214 LOC — only the one FFI unsafe and stub markers inspected; the bulk is JVM-native event-emit glue I did not read line-by-line), recording.rs beyond line 440 (dump_recording body, lifecycle state machine — read signatures and key branches, not every method), dump.rs metadata/checkpoint WRITERS (write_metadata_section/write_checkpoint_section bodies skimmed, not traced byte-by-byte). NOT REACHED: full line-by-line of builtin.rs event-type registration tables; the inline test bodies were enumerated/counted and spot-read but not each executed-by-reading. No code was run/compiled (review-only). The finding-1 UAF is identified by reading the Drop SAFETY reasoning and the trailing acknowledgment comment; I did not build a loom/Miri repro to prove the window empirically — severity is judged medium on reachability+consequence, and I flagged that the existing wedged test does not cover it.

#### `libcratonvm`

The two in-scope files are a small (2190 LOC), well-documented, and unusually disciplined FFI surface — panic-across-boundary containment and null-pointer handling are correct and uniform across all ~20 entry points, and there are ZERO stubs, todos, or fake-result natives. The dominant risk is architectural rather than a code defect: the public CratonRef/CratonClass handles are bare heap addresses (ObjectRef::from_raw with release-build-only validation), so any caller-supplied garbage handle, or any valid handle held across a moving-GC collection, is dereferenced into arbitrary/dangling memory — a real memory-safety hole reachable from the public API (HIGH). Secondary issues: cratonvm_destroy leaves a stale thread-local JNI context (MEDIUM), with_vm has no reentrancy guard so a Java->native callback re-entering the same handle is aliasing UB (MEDIUM), parse_mem_size can overflow in release (LOW), and a couple of minor spec/UX deviations. Test breadth on the error/null surface is good, but the entire happy path is gated behind --cfg flat_api_live_vm and excluded from the default run, and cratonvm-embed has only a compile-time assertion — so judged coverage is ~60%, below the 85% target. Readiness for crates.io: the panic/null hygiene is publishable, but the raw-handle lifetime contract must be either hardened (a validated handle table marked as GC roots) or very prominently documented before external consumption, and the live-VM tests must be wired into CI."

- **Findings:** high:1, medium:2, low:3, info:1
- **Stubs:** 0 markers, ~0 real functional gaps. Exhaustive grep across libcratonvm/src and cratonvm-embed/src for unimplemented!/todo!/unreachable!/panic!/"stub"/not implemented/NYI/TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX/placeholder found ZERO genuine stub or panic markers in production code. No native returns a hardcoded/fake value where real behaviour is expected — every entry point delegates to real cratonvm-vm machinery (Vm::new, invoke, load_class, heap.get_field, create_java_string, read_java_string). The only `.unwrap()`/`.expect()` hits are inside #[cfg(test)] code on known-good CString literals. The two design 'follow-ups' mentioned in doc comments (a C-varargs shim for invoke; name-based field resolution) are NOT unimplemented gaps: typed-array invoke is the deliberate stable form, and name-based field resolution IS implemented (cratonvm_field_index / *_by_name, lib.rs:1350,1403,1505). cratonvm_last_error's `vm` param is intentionally unused (thread-local error model) — documented, not a stub.
- **Performance:**
  - Hot-path cost in this module is negligible — it is a thin marshalling shim. Per-call allocations exist (Vec<Value> in invoke_static/virtual at lib.rs:837,1127; CString in error/string paths) but each wraps an actual Java method invocation, so they are not the bottleneck.
  - cratonvm_field_count and cratonvm_get_field each take class_manager.read() and look up the class fresh (lib.rs:1259,1311); a host iterating fields calls field_count once then get_field N times, taking the read lock N+1 times. Minor; could pass a cached field count. Not worth optimizing at this layer.
  - set_last_error allocates+sanitizes a CString on every error (lib.rs:634-639); only on the error path, fine.
  - No lock contention, redundant cloning, or O(n)-where-O(1) issues in the FFI layer itself worth flagging.
- **Strengths:** Panic-across-FFI is handled correctly and uniformly: every #[no_mangle] extern "C" entry wraps its body in catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(..)) and converts a caught panic to a defined error value (JNI_ERR / CratonValue::error / null / -1) — the single most important FFI-safety property, and it is consistent across all ~20 entry points.; Null-pointer handling from C is thorough: every handle, out-pointer, and C-string argument is null-checked before use, with a descriptive thread-local last-error set; out-params are documented and tested as 'untouched on error'.; cratonvm-embed is #![forbid(unsafe_code)] and is a genuinely thin, well-documented curated facade — good crates.io hygiene.; C-string handling is robust: interior NULs are stripped before CString::new (set_last_error, string_utf8, class_name) so a Java string with embedded NUL cannot panic the conversion; ownership split (caller frees via cratonvm_free_string vs library-owned last-error buffer) is clearly documented.; Sound API design choices: typed CratonValue array instead of C varargs (correctly noted as unsound for non-int/double across FFI); out-pointer + return-code instead of sentinel handles because 0 is a valid ClassId; thread-local last-error mirroring JNI's per-thread pending-exception model.; Doc comments are unusually complete — each function carries its C prototype, a # Safety contract, and rationale; the ParkedVm Send rationale and the one-VM-per-process registry are explicitly reasoned.
- **Review depth:** FULL read of both in-scope files end-to-end: libcratonvm/src/lib.rs (2027 lines, the only .rs under libcratonvm/src) read across three windows (1-1347, 1348-2027) plus the inline test module; cratonvm-embed/src/lib.rs (163 lines) read in full incl. its single test. Stub scan was exhaustive (grep for all requested markers across both src trees) — zero production markers. For the dominant memory-safety finding I followed the handle path OUT of scope into types/src/value.rs:100-158 to confirm ObjectRef::from_raw does no release-build validation. I did NOT deep-read the cratonvm-vm internals that the shim delegates to (Vm::new, invoke, heap.get_field/class_id_of/set_array_element, create_java_string, set_jni_context_arc, the moving-GC root machinery) — those are out of scope and reviewed by other module passes; my UAF/stale-handle and stale-TLS findings are reasoned from the in-scope call sites plus the from_raw confirmation, and assume the documented moving-GC behaviour from project memory. I did not run the build or the test suite (static review only); the coverage estimate is judged, not instrumented, and the --cfg flat_api_live_vm happy-path tests were read but not executed."

### 2.2 Test gaps & recommended additions (per module)

**`reader`**
- gap: jimage malformed-input negative tests are thinner than class-file ones: no explicit test for a truncated/oversized locations_size or strings_size in the header that passes from_bytes section math but yields a bad redirect->offset->location chain; BadResourceRange and a location offset that decodes but reconstructs a colliding path (the Ok(None) hash-collision branch in find_location:670-673) appear untested.
- gap: No fuzz/property test harness (cargo-fuzz / arbitrary) over read_class on random bytes — all malformed-input tests are hand-crafted single cases.
- gap: decode_target_info type-annotation target_type byte layouts (0x40/0x41 localvar tables, the 0x13..0x4B range) have limited negative-length coverage.
- gap: class_file_version preview-minor (0xFFFF) behavior is untested (relates to the spec-compliance finding).
- add: Add a cargo-fuzz target on read_class(&[u8]) asserting it never panics/aborts and always returns Result — the strongest guarantee for an untrusted-input parser.
- add: Add jimage negative tests: header with locations_size/strings_size that overflow or under-run the file; a location offset whose decoded path mismatches the query (exercise the collision Ok(None) path); a compressed (compressed_size>0) entry returning JImageError::Compressed.
- add: Add a property test that any class file truncated at every byte boundary yields Err, never panic.
- add: Add a test asserting a latest-major class with minor==0xFFFF (preview) is handled per HotSpot semantics.

**`types`**
- gap: No test asserts the release-build behaviour where ObjectRef::from_raw skips the null/align check (debug_assert elided) — the degrade is only tested where upstream filters catch it.
- gap: update_object_ptr_unchecked debug_assert for >47-bit pointer in release is not (cannot be) exercised; its silent-mask corruption path is untested.
- gap: No fuzz/proptest specifically targets the to_value() SUB_OBJECT-collision boundary with adversarial aligned-non-null long payloads to assert it never fabricates a reference that a heap predicate would reject (the security-critical path is only unit-tested with hand-picked patterns).
- gap: intern() Drop-during-teardown hazard (intern.rs:164-182) is documented but has no negative/guard test.
- gap: ObjectRef Send+Sync soundness has no multi-OS-thread test (by design, since the model is single-threaded today).
- add: A proptest over arbitrary i64 long bit patterns asserting that to_value_checked(predicate=false) NEVER returns Value::Object and ALWAYS bumps the degradation counter when the SUB_OBJECT pattern is hit — pins the security boundary.
- add: A release-profile (or cfg(not(debug_assertions))) gated test confirming decode_value/to_value gracefully degrade null/unaligned SUB_OBJECT/VTAG_OBJECT slots to Object(None)/Long rather than constructing a bogus ObjectRef.
- add: A test that update_object_ptr_unchecked with a >47-bit pointer in a release-equivalent path is either rejected or its truncation is explicitly characterized.
- add: An end-to-end assertion (in the regression-suite) that object_degradation_count() stays 0 over a representative app run, turning the counter into a regression tripwire.

**`native-api`**
- gap: registry.rs NativeKind category gating: drop_synthetic_stubs, with_category, kind_of, dump_registrations untested
- gap: registry.rs real-mode register() drop branches (Socket/ServerSocket, ForkJoinPool task-family, StringJoiner, ThreadPoolExecutor) untested
- gap: registry.rs copy_from/to_native_memory default impls (raw deref) have no test for the addr<=0 guard or override routing
- gap: fd_table.rs socket error paths (bad-fd on each typed op) and available()/pread/pwrite seek-restore-on-error
- gap: fd_table.rs close() flush-error propagation and the alloc_fd_or_abort overflow ceiling
- add: registry: with_category(SyntheticStub)+set_drop_synthetic_stubs(true) drops find(); Bridge/Intrinsic survive; kind_of round-trips the set category
- add: registry: each real-mode flag drops only its targeted class and nothing else (dump_registrations assertions)
- add: registry: copy_from/to_native_memory default returns false for addr<=0 and (after hardening) for any address without override
- add: fd_table: assert every typed op returns NotFound for a wrong-type/absent fd; assert available() does not move the cursor when stream_position errors
- add: fd_table: open_read on a missing path consumes an fd but never aliases 0/1/2; close() returns the flush error while still removing the entry

**`native-collections`**
- gap: widened_obj_key collision/recycled-identity disambiguation (the subtlest, highest-risk code in the module) is completely untested — the harness still imports the OLD obj_key, not widened_obj_key; case 2/case 3 (class-id match/mismatch, generation allocation) have zero coverage
- gap: gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays has NO test (and no caller) — dead-key collection, registry shrink, overlay removal all unverified
- gap: for_each_overlay_ref lock-poison behavior (root drop) untested
- gap: TreeMap/TreeSet/CSLM overlay pruning untested
- gap: ArrayList capacity-cap / overflow behavior (1<<30) untested; no add-past-cap test
- gap: Properties \uXXXX surrogate/malformed decode untested
- gap: CompletableFuture exceptional propagation, ForkJoinPool, ThreadPoolExecutor, ConcurrentSkipListMap custom-comparator behavior untested (registration smoke only)
- gap: concurrency stress beyond basic put-poll: no concurrent resize-vs-read race test for CHM, no ChmMonitorGuard panic-path test
- add: widened_obj_key tests: same-hash distinct-class collision → distinct generations; same-hash same-class recycled-identity (dead A not pruned) → B must NOT inherit A's overlay; relocation (same class, ptr change) → generation preserved
- add: gc_prune_dead_collection_overlays test: populate all overlay tables incl tm/ts, run prune with is_live closure, assert dead entries removed and registry/lhm/ll/tm/ts/cslm shrink
- add: lock-poison test: poison an overlay mutex, run gc_scan_collection_overlay_roots, assert backing refs still rooted (not silently dropped)
- add: ArrayList add past 1<<30 cap: assert throws OOM or leaves size consistent (no phantom slot)
- add: Properties.load \uD800 lone-surrogate and malformed \uZZ → assert JDK-equivalent value/throw
- add: CHM concurrent resize-vs-get stress (loom or thread spray) asserting no spurious miss
- add: ChmMonitorGuard: panic inside protected block → VM degrades, no abort

**`native-io-core`**
- gap: No test for IPv4-mapped IPv6 metadata literal (::ffff:169.254.169.254) — the exact input that bypasses the always-on block (the high-severity finding)
- gap: nb_connect.rs raw-FFI start()/poll() have no direct unit tests (in-progress vs connected vs refused state machine, fd-close-on-error paths, IPv6 sockaddr layout) — only indirect coverage
- gap: validate_path confinement-ON branch: symlink-escape and not-yet-existing-leaf (createNewFile) paths have at most one test; the TOCTOU/realpath cases are untested
- gap: net_read0/net_write0 native-address validation (validate_native_range rejects addr<=0, len<=0, len>NET_MAX_TRANSFER) not directly asserted
- gap: CRATONVM_BLOCK_PRIVATE_NETS env-gated default_policy path is tested only at the pure-classifier level, not end-to-end through default_policy (the env-cache race is acknowledged in a comment)
- add: default_policy("::ffff:169.254.169.254") and ("[::ffff:169.254.170.2]:80") assert Deny (regression for the SSRF gap)
- add: nb_connect unit tests: start() against a local listener returns Connected/InProgress; poll() transitions Pending->Connected; start() to a closed port returns Failed and does not leak the fd; V6 build_sockaddr byte layout
- add: validate_path: confinement-ON rejects an absolute out-of-sandbox path, a symlink whose target escapes, and accepts createNewFile of a new leaf inside the sandbox
- add: validate_native_range boundary tests (addr=0, len=0, len=NET_MAX_TRANSFER+1) and net_read0/write0 invalid-address error mapping

## Part 3 - Documentation & scripts review

### docs-governance

Owner name (Craton Software Company), license (Apache-2.0), repo URL (github.com/craton-co/cratonvm), homepage (craton.com.ar), version (0.3.0), MSRV (1.77), supported JDK (17+ to build / Java 8-25 to run), and contact emails (hello@/security@/support@/conduct@craton.com.ar) are CONSISTENT across all 16 docs and Cargo.toml — that core metadata is in good shape. The serious problems are factual drift between the docs and the actual repo, plus doc-vs-doc disagreements. The single biggest issue is the workspace crate count: every doc says "17 member crates plus fuzz = 18 Cargo.toml," but Cargo.toml actually declares 19 members (adding libcratonvm and cratonvm-embed, which are undocumented everywhere) and explicitly EXCLUDES fuzz as a non-member standalone workspace — directly contradicting CHANGELOG's "fuzz is now a workspace member / all 18 workspace members incl. fuzz." Several cross-references point at moved/renamed paths (docs/internal/gaps/*, app-jvm-bugs/*) or a missing file (docs/lock-order.md), and CHANGELOG references THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md / CITATION.cff that don't exist (file is THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md; no CITATION.cff). ARCHITECTURE.md describes the GC as full "semi-space, G1, ZGC" while README/CONTRIBUTING say "no G1, experimental zgc stub." SECURITY.md's crypto status (ML-KEM/HKDF/PBKDF2 throw NoSuchAlgorithmException) conflicts with the shipped code, which implements ML-KEM encaps/decaps and HKDF. Build/run instructions in README and BUILD_GUIDE are accurate and the scripts/regression-suite they reference exist.

**Consistency issues:**
- CRATE COUNT: README.md:219 / ARCHITECTURE.md:8,63 / BUILD_GUIDE.md:94 all say '17 member crates plus fuzz = 18 Cargo.toml files', but Cargo.toml:2 declares 19 members (incl. libcratonvm + cratonvm-embed) and Cargo.toml:3-6 + fuzz/Cargo.toml:7-18 state fuzz is NOT a member. CHANGELOG.md:54-56 directly contradicts the manifest by calling fuzz a workspace member ('all 18 workspace members, incl. fuzz' / 'fuzz/ is now a workspace member').
- UNDOCUMENTED CRATES: libcratonvm and cratonvm-embed are real workspace members (Cargo.toml:2) but appear in NO root doc crate table or ARCHITECTURE layout/dependency graph.
- GC MATURITY: ARCHITECTURE.md:25,97 ('Garbage collectors (semi-space, G1, ZGC)') and CHANGELOG.md:76 ('G1 and ZGC garbage collectors' Added) vs README.md:236 / CONTRIBUTING.md:66 ('experimental zgc-gated stub, no G1') and CHANGELOG.md:62 ('G1 / ZGC remain experimental').
- CRYPTO STATUS: SECURITY.md:11-15 says ML-KEM/ML-DSA and HKDF/PBKDF2 'throw NoSuchAlgorithmException', but jca/kem.rs implements ML-KEM encaps/decaps and crypto_impl.rs implements HKDF (RFC-5869 vectored). Doc is stale vs code.
- FILE-NAME DRIFT: CHANGELOG.md:50 says 'THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md' (underscores) + 'CITATION.cff'; actual file is THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md (hyphens, per NOTICE:9) and CITATION.cff does not exist.
- CRATON-GPU DESCRIPTION: ARCHITECTURE.md:23 ('GPU offload runtime integration') vs README.md:234 / CONTRIBUTING.md:64 ('build-time Java annotation sources, @Parallel etc.').
- AARCH64 PATH: CHANGELOG.md:28 references 'jit/src/aarch64/mod.rs'; ARCHITECTURE.md:20,122 + CONTRIBUTING.md:109 use 'jit/src/aarch64.rs' (the file that exists).

**Findings:**
- **[HIGH]** Workspace crate count wrong everywhere: docs say 17+fuzz=18, Cargo.toml declares 19 members and excludes fuzz - `Cargo.toml:2-6 vs README.md:219, ARCHITECTURE.md:8, ARCHITECTURE.md:63, BUILD_GUIDE.md:94, CHANGELOG.md:54-56` - Recount against Cargo.toml: 19 workspace members; fuzz is a separate (non-member) standalone workspace. Update the count and crate tables in README.md, ARCHITECTURE.md, BUILD_GUIDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, and the prose in ARCHITECTURE.md:63. Add libcratonvm and cratonvm-embed to the crate-layout tables and dependency graph (or explain why they're omitted). Fix CHANGELOG.md:54-56 to stop describing fuzz as a workspace member.
- **[MEDIUM]** CHANGELOG references files that do not exist: THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md and CITATION.cff - `CHANGELOG.md:50` - Change 'THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md' to 'THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md' in CHANGELOG.md:50. Either add the promised CITATION.cff (recommended for an OSS research project that wants citations) or remove the CITATION.cff claim from the changelog.
- **[MEDIUM]** Broken cross-references: several linked docs live at different paths than referenced - `SECURITY.md:99, README.md:166, README.md:253, README.md:254` - Update the four links to their actual paths (the docs were relocated into docs/internal/gaps/ and docs/internal/app-jvm-bugs/). Consider a CI link-check (e.g. lychee) to catch this class of drift.
- **[MEDIUM]** ARCHITECTURE.md describes GC as full G1+ZGC while README/CONTRIBUTING say no G1 and only an experimental zgc stub - `ARCHITECTURE.md:25, ARCHITECTURE.md:97 vs README.md:236, CONTRIBUTING.md:66` - Make ARCHITECTURE.md:25/:97 match README/CONTRIBUTING — describe the default generational/semi-space + non-moving sweep collector and mark G1/ZGC as experimental/feature-gated (and clarify whether G1 is usable at all, since README says 'no G1').
- **[MEDIUM]** SECURITY.md crypto status conflicts with shipped code (ML-KEM and HKDF described as throwing, but implemented) - `SECURITY.md:11-15 vs native-builtins/src/jca/kem.rs:36-56, native-builtins/src/crypto_impl.rs:791-866` - Reconcile SECURITY.md against docs/CRYPTO_STATUS.md and the jca code: state accurately which PQC/KDF algorithms resolve vs fail-closed. If the intent is a conservative 'do not rely on this in production' posture, say that explicitly rather than claiming the algorithms throw when they don't.
- **[LOW]** CHANGELOG [Unreleased] references docs/lock-order.md which does not exist - `CHANGELOG.md:29` - Add docs/lock-order.md documenting the lock ordering, or drop the reference from the changelog entry.
- **[LOW]** AArch64 JIT path referenced as jit/src/aarch64/mod.rs but file is jit/src/aarch64.rs - `CHANGELOG.md:28 vs jit/src/aarch64.rs` - Change 'jit/src/aarch64/mod.rs' to 'jit/src/aarch64.rs' in CHANGELOG.md:28 (or note if the module is mid-split).
- **[LOW]** RELEASING.md step numbering skips (3 -> 5) in two ordered lists - `RELEASING.md:93-99, RELEASING.md:121-130` - Renumber RELEASING.md section 3 so the steps run 1,2,3,4 (currently 1,2,3,5).
- **[LOW]** ARCHITECTURE.md crate-table descriptions disagree with README for two crates - `ARCHITECTURE.md:23 vs README.md:234` - Pick one accurate description of craton-gpu (the README/CONTRIBUTING 'build-time annotation sources' wording, if that is what it is) and use it consistently in ARCHITECTURE.md:23.
- **[INFO]** GOVERNANCE/SUPPORT reference github.com/craton-co (org) while NOTICE/badges use github.com/craton-co/cratonvm (repo) — consistent, noted for completeness - `GOVERNANCE.md:4, SUPPORT.md:5, MAINTAINERS.md:14 vs NOTICE:5, README.md:3` - No change needed; this confirms the owner/license/repo/homepage/email axis is consistent.
- **[INFO]** Missing standard OSS files: CITATION.cff (promised), CODEOWNERS present, no top-level INSTALL - `repo root` - Add CITATION.cff (it is referenced and expected for a citable research JVM). Optionally expand AUTHORS as outside contributors join, per the MAINTAINERS roster plan.

**Add:**
- CITATION.cff at repo root (promised in CHANGELOG.md:50, currently missing; expected for a citable research JVM).
- docs/lock-order.md (referenced by CHANGELOG.md:29 but absent), or remove the reference.
- Crate-table + ARCHITECTURE layout/dependency-graph entries for the two undocumented members libcratonvm and cratonvm-embed.
- A CI link-checker (e.g. lychee/markdown-link-check) to catch broken doc cross-references like the four relocated paths.
- An explicit statement of supported run-time JDK / Java language levels in one canonical place (Java 8-25 to run, JDK 17+ only to build test classes) — currently spread across README/BUILD_GUIDE and easy to drift.

**Remove / fix:**
- Fix the '17 member crates plus fuzz = 18' claim to '19 workspace members; fuzz is a separate non-member workspace' in README.md:219, ARCHITECTURE.md:8 & :63, BUILD_GUIDE.md:94, and remove the 'fuzz is a workspace member' claims in CHANGELOG.md:54-56.
- Fix CHANGELOG.md:50 'THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md' -> 'THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md'.
- Repoint SECURITY.md:99 -> docs/internal/app-jvm-bugs/real-raf-segv-root-cause.md; README.md:166 -> docs/internal/gaps/javafx-status.md; README.md:253 -> docs/internal/gaps/embedding.md; README.md:254 -> docs/internal/gaps/gc-tuning.md.
- Reconcile ARCHITECTURE.md:25 & :97 GC description with README/CONTRIBUTING (mark G1/ZGC experimental/feature-gated; clarify 'no G1').
- Update SECURITY.md:11-15 crypto status to match the shipped jca code (ML-KEM and HKDF are implemented, not throwing) or state the conservative posture explicitly.
- Fix CHANGELOG.md:28 'jit/src/aarch64/mod.rs' -> 'jit/src/aarch64.rs'.
- Renumber RELEASING.md section 3 steps (currently 1,2,3,5 -> should be 1,2,3,4).
- Unify the craton-gpu description in ARCHITECTURE.md:23 with README.md:234/CONTRIBUTING.md:64.

### docs-technical

Reviewed CratonVM's docs/ tree against the code. The docs are well-organized and the env-vars that ARE documented in CONFIG.md all exist in code (no documented-but-absent vars), but three classes of problems stand out. (1) ACCURACY: docs/CRYPTO_STATUS.md is materially stale — it marks PBKDF2, ML-KEM, and DES/DESede as "Stub-throws"/"Not supported", but the code now implements all three (native PBKDF2 in phases_early, real SunJCE ML_KEM_Impls$K* KEM SPI in jca/kem.rs, DES/DESede/CBC routed to real SunJCE in jca/cipher.rs). This is the most serious accuracy issue: the doc actively tells users crypto is missing that is present. (2) ENV-VAR COVERAGE: the codebase defines ~280 distinct CRATONVM_* env vars; CONFIG.md documents only ~6. Most are debug toggles, but ~20 user-facing behavior switches (CRATONVM_NO_STUBS, CRATONVM_REAL_NET_SOCKETS, CRATONVM_REAL_AQS, CRATONVM_REAL_FORKJOINPOOL, CRATONVM_PRECISE_JIT_MAPS/NO_*, CRATONVM_DISABLE_JIT, CRATONVM_JIT_THRESHOLD, CRATONVM_ROOTSNAP_CACHE, CRATONVM_SELECTIVE_PROMOTE/NO_*, CRATONVM_SYNTHETIC_*, CRATONVM_REAL_PROXY*, CRATONVM_BLOCK_PRIVATE_NETS, CRATONVM_CONFINE_IO, CRATONVM_UNTRUSTED_CODE, CRATONVM_HTTP_MAX_BODY) are undocumented. (3) LINKS/TRIAGE: ~30 broken internal links across the docs tree; two known-issues docs (kafka-bug-B-mockito, kafka-bug-C) are referenced from known-issues/README.md as OPEN/partial but the files actually live under docs/internal/fixed-suite-bugs/ — a fixed-vs-open contradiction violating the no-fixed-bugs-in-known-issues rule, plus two dangling OPEN refs (bug06-fam6, spring-bug-08) that don't exist anywhere. docs/internal is 448 tracked .md files (the 2700+ .log files are .gitignored, not shipped); it contains only test/example PEM keys (no production secrets), but its own oss-readiness audit flags committed TLS private keys (native-builtins/src/t27_certs/*.key, outside docs/) as a release blocker.

**Consistency issues:**
- CRYPTO_STATUS.md (0.3.0) contradicts code on PBKDF2 (stub vs implemented), ML-KEM (stub vs real SPI), DES/DESede (not supported vs routed to SunJCE).
- known-issues/README.md marks kafka-bug-B (mockito dispatch) and kafka-bug-C as open/partial while the canonical docs live in internal/fixed-suite-bugs/ — fixed-vs-open status conflict.
- docs/README.md links internal/<file>.md for several gap docs that actually live at internal/gaps/<file>.md.
- known-issues/README cites bug06-fam6 and spring-bug-08 as OPEN bugs but no doc file exists for them anywhere in the repo.
- CONFIG.md env-var table (~6 vars) is a tiny subset of the ~280 CRATONVM_* vars in code; ~20 user-facing behavior switches are undocumented (no documented-but-absent vars, so the gap is one-directional).

**Findings:**

Keep HKDF/Scrypt/Argon2 accurate.
- **[HIGH]** CRYPTO_STATUS.md marks ML-KEM as 'Stub-throws' but the real KEM SPI is wired - `docs/CRYPTO_STATUS.md:169-179` - Update ML-KEM rows to reflect that javax.crypto.KEM encaps/decaps is backed by the real ML_KEM_Impls SPI. Verify ML-DSA / KeyPairGenerator status separately and state it precisely rather than blanket 'Stub-throws'.
- **[MEDIUM]** CRYPTO_STATUS.md marks DES/DESede as 'Not supported' but they route to real SunJCE - `docs/CRYPTO_STATUS.md:73` - Either reclassify DES/DESede as 'Implemented (real SunJCE SPI, CBC)' or, if the routing is meant to be removed, remove the routing. As written the doc and code disagree on a security-relevant cipher.
- **[MEDIUM]** CONFIG.md documents only ~6 of ~280 CRATONVM_* env vars; key user-facing switches undocumented - `docs/CONFIG.md:146-157` - Add a 'Behavior / experimental toggles' subsection to CONFIG.md documenting the ~20 user-facing vars (especially the real-vs-synthetic gates and the security/sandbox vars CONFINE_IO/BLOCK_PRIVATE_NETS/UNTRUSTED_CODE). Optionally add a one-line note that CRATONVM_DBG_*/DIAG_* are internal debug toggles not intended for users.
