### scripts-ci

Active ci.yml covers the Rust side adequately (workspace build, cargo test --workspace, cargo fmt --check, clippy -D warnings on ubuntu+windows, plus a JDK-backed real-path smoke job). But every depth/conformance gate is PARKED and unenforced on PRs: JCK, coverage (fail-under 65), miri, cargo-audit, the no-default-natives/synthetic-stub census, hotspot-baseline, and the RI smoke matrix all live in .github/.wf or .github/_disabled-workflows and never run. Two parked workflows have wrong cargo targets (the package is cratonvm-cli, the binary is cratonvm; jck.yml uses -p vm-cli and pgo-build.yml uploads cratonvm-cli). The ACTIVE census-guard step points at a test-infra/native-census path that does not exist at repo root (masked by continue-on-error). Most .bat build scripts plus verify-oom-fix.sh and many test-infra/*.sh scripts hardcode machine-specific absolute paths (C:/Users/Victor, C:/craton worktree target dirs, hardcoded JDK paths, a personally-renamed rsc_cv20/cgo_cv20 toolchain) and are single-developer scaffolding. No committed secrets or curl|bash-of-untrusted-remote patterns were found (only a test-fixture password in arquillian.xml). The README-claimed forbid-debug-trace-prints gate (scripts/check-no-diag-prints.sh) is NOT wired into the active ci.yml; only the disabled variant runs it.

**Findings:**
- **[HIGH]** Parked jck.yml builds wrong cargo target / nonexistent binary - `.github/.wf/jck.yml:96,106` - Change to `cargo build --release -p cratonvm-cli` and CRATONVM_BIN=$(pwd)/target/release/cratonvm before this workflow is promoted.
- **[MEDIUM]** Active census-guard step references a path that does not exist - `.github/workflows/ci.yml:71` - Fix the path or move the census tool+baseline to test-infra/native-census/, then drop continue-on-error to make it blocking once a JDK is wired in.
- **[MEDIUM]** pgo-build.yml uploads a binary that is never produced - `.github/.wf/pgo-build.yml:79` - Remove the target/release/cratonvm-cli line; keep target/release/cratonvm.
- **[MEDIUM]** Conformance/depth CI gates all parked, not enforced - `.github/.wf/jck.yml:1; .github/_disabled-workflows/ci.yml:76` - Promote at least cargo-audit and a coverage job into the active ci.yml (they need no JDK or external fixtures); wire JCK behind its existing JCK_HOME skip guard so it is harmless when unset.
- **[MEDIUM]** forbid-debug-trace-prints gate not in active CI - `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1; .github/_disabled-workflows/ci.yml:61` - Add a non-windows step `run: bash scripts/check-no-diag-prints.sh` to the active build-and-test job.
- **[MEDIUM]** Hardcoded personal toolchain and registry paths in build .bat scripts - `scripts/build-maindev.bat:5,14,15; scripts/build-cpu-evade.bat:10,11; scripts/checkmaindev.bat:4,13` - Use cargo/rustc on PATH and %USERPROFILE%-relative dirs, or move these one-off variants into a gitignored personal scratch directory.
- **[LOW]** verify-oom-fix.sh hardwired to sibling worktrees - `scripts/verify-oom-fix.sh:7-11` - Parameterize via env (VM, JDK, REPO_ROOT) or move out of scripts/ into a scratch/ area.
- **[LOW]** Hardcoded Windows JDK default across many scripts, inconsistent - `scripts/app-checker.sh:122; scripts/bench-4way.sh:15; scripts/run-h2-testall.bat:9` - Standardize on ${JAVA_HOME:?set JAVA_HOME} with no hardcoded fallback; align app-checker.sh:61 help text with the actual :122 default.
- **[LOW]** Large family of near-duplicate isolated-build .bat variants - `scripts/build-cpu-bj.bat:1; scripts/check-bj.bat:1; scripts/check-wt.bat:1; scripts/build-cpu-isolated.bat:1` - Collapse to one parameterized build.bat taking target-dir and profile args; delete the dead per-task variants.
- **[INFO]** jck.yml differential job is ungated if the workflow is promoted - `.github/.wf/jck.yml:149` - Gate differential behind the same availability check (or workflow_dispatch only) before promotion.
- **[INFO]** No committed secrets; download/secret hygiene is sound - `scripts/smoke/common.sh:37` - No action needed; continue using secrets/vars indirection and avoid embedding remote curl|bash.

- Active ci.yml: add a 'bash scripts/check-no-diag-prints.sh' step to actually enforce the documented no-debug-prints gate

## Part 4 - OSS / crates.io / Apache-2.0 readiness

CratonVM is in remarkably good legal/community shape but has ONE hard crates.io blocker and several repo-hygiene blockers. LICENSING is essentially complete: LICENSE is the full Apache-2.0 body (END OF TERMS present; the optional APPENDIX boilerplate is replaced by a filled-in copyright notice, which is the recommended way to apply it), NOTICE is Apache-§4(d) compliant, AUTHORS present, and per-file SPDX headers cover 556/558 .rs files (~99.6%; only native-api/src/server_socket_ports.rs and vm/src/runtime/stwhang_watch.rs lack one). CARGO METADATA across all 19 members is excellent — every member supplies name/version/description/license/repository/homepage/authors/keywords/categories via inheritance or directly, version is uniformly 0.3.0, and crucially every inter-crate path dep already carries version="0.3.0" (the #1 crates.io footgun is avoided). THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES is thorough and accurate; Cargo.lock (362 deps) contains NO GPL/LGPL/AGPL/SSPL — only permissive (MIT/Apache/ISC/BSD/Zlib/Unicode) plus the documented BouncyCastle MIT-style port and ring's ISC/MIT/OpenSSL aggregate; openssl is only a transitive native-tls dep (Apache-2.0, fine). 

THE ONE STRUCTURAL CRATES.IO BLOCKER: cratonvm-vm is marked publishable but has an UNCONDITIONAL, non-optional dependency on cratonvm-native-awt, which is publish=false (used unconditionally at vm/src/vm/vm_init.rs:1938). crates.io forbids publishing a crate whose normal deps aren't on the registry, so vm — and everything downstream of it (libcratonvm, cratonvm-embed, cratonvm-cli) — CANNOT publish. This directly refutes the workspace Cargo.toml comment claiming 'every library crate is publishable.' (Confirmed empirically: `cargo publish --dry-run -p cratonvm-vm` fails; leaf `cratonvm-types` dry-run succeeds.) The other 4 publish=false crates (craton-gpu, cuda-bridge, jit-cuda, fuzz) are correctly withheld and only enter vm via the optional gpu-offload feature, so they don't block.

REPO HYGIENE is the other weak spot: 595 compiled .class files and 383 bench/ files are git-TRACKED even though bench/, docs/, test-infra/, scratch/ appear in .gitignore (gitignore doesn't untrack already-committed files). dd1.out is tracked. The .git history is 848MB. Root-level build-*.log / hs_err_pid*.log / test-final.log are present on disk but correctly untracked. COMMUNITY FILES are all present and consistent (CODEOWNERS, FUNDING, dependabot, ISSUE_TEMPLATE, PR template, SECURITY.md, TRADEMARKS.md, CoC, CONTRIBUTING, dco.yml); owner/homepage/repo strings are consistent everywhere (8× craton-co/cratonvm, 2× craton.com.ar, 6× 'Craton Software Company', 0 variants).

**Findings:**
- **[CRITICAL]** Publishable cratonvm-vm has an unconditional dep on publish=false cratonvm-native-awt → blocks vm + all downstream crates from crates.io - `vm/Cargo.toml:67` - cratonvm-vm sets no publish gate (publishable) but declares `cratonvm-native-awt = { path = "../native-awt", version = "0.3.0" }` as a normal, non-optional dependency. native-awt/Cargo.toml:370 sets `publish = false`. The dep is used unconditionally at vm/src/vm/vm_init.rs:1938 (cratonvm_native_awt: -> Either (a) make native-awt publishable (remove its publish=false, give it a real description/keywords — it already has them — and publish it), or (b) gate the AWT registration behind an optional feature so the dep becomes `optional = true` and vm publishes without it, or (c) mark vm/cli/libcratonvm/cratonvm-embed publish=false too and only ship the leaf library crates at 0.3.0. Until resolved, correct the misleading workspace comment claiming all library crates are publishable.
- **[HIGH]** 595 compiled .class files and ~383 bench/ files are git-tracked despite being .gitignored - `.gitignore + git ls-files (bench/, **/*.class)` - `bench/` is listed in .gitignore but 383 files under it are already committed (gitignore does not untrack committed paths). 595 *.class bytecode files are tracked across bench/, bench-tornado/, and test fixtures. These are opaque binaries that bloat the repo and ship in clones. Note: the per-crate ` -> `git rm -r --cached bench/ **/*.class dd1.out test-infra/` (keep needed .java/.sh bench sources by re-adding selectively, or move benches to a separate repo). Decide intentionally whether docs/ (513 tracked files, also gitignored) should be tracked — the contradiction between .gitignore listing `docs` and 513 tracked docs files should be reconciled. Consider a history rewrite (git filter-repo) before going public to shed the 848MB history if it contains large removed blobs.
- **[HIGH]** dd1.out junk artifact is tracked at repo root - `dd1.out` - A 124-byte stray developer probe-output file `dd1.out` is git-tracked at the repo root. It is not referenced by build or tests and looks like an accidental commit. -> `git rm dd1.out` and add `*.out` (already partially covered by build-*.out) or an explicit `/dd1.out` to .gitignore.
- **[MEDIUM]** Two source files lack the SPDX/Apache license header - `native-api/src/server_socket_ports.rs, vm/src/runtime/stwhang_watch.rs` - 556 of 558 .rs files carry `// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` + copyright header (~99.6% coverage — excellent). These two files are the only gaps. Apache-2.0 recommends a header on every source file. -> Prepend the standard 2-line header (`// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` / `// Copyright 2024-2026 Craton Software Company`) to both files to reach 100%.
- **[LOW]** libcratonvm and cratonvm-embed omit the recommended `readme` field and have no README.md - `libcratonvm/Cargo.toml, cratonvm-embed/Cargo.toml` - All 17 other members declare `readme = "README.md"` and ship one; these two declare no readme and have no README.md file. Not a publish blocker (readme is optional), but crates.io renders a blank landing page without it, and these are the embedding-facing crates most likely to be browsed. (Both are  -> Add a short README.md to each and `readme = "README.md"` to their Cargo.toml for a complete crates.io page.
- **[LOW]** 7 test-infra/ files tracked despite .gitignore listing test-infra/ - `test-infra/ (git ls-files)` - test-infra/ is in .gitignore but 7 files (run-*.sh helper scripts, es-repro/XcRepro.java, bcperf-experiment.sh) remain tracked. Minor inconsistency between intent (ignored) and reality (tracked); these scripts are harmless but contradict the ignore rule. -> Decide whether these helper scripts should be tracked. If yes, remove test-infra/ from .gitignore (or add a !negation for the kept files); if no, `git rm --cached` them. Same reconciliation applies to bench/ and docs/.
- **[INFO]** LICENSE replaces the Apache APPENDIX boilerplate with a filled-in copyright notice - `LICENSE (190 lines)` - LICENSE contains the complete Apache-2.0 TERMS AND CONDITIONS through 'END OF TERMS AND CONDITIONS', then substitutes the optional 'APPENDIX: How to apply the Apache License' template with the actual applied notice for Craton Software Company. This is fully compliant — the appendix is informational  -> No change needed. Optionally restore the verbatim APPENDIX text above the applied notice if you prefer a byte-identical canonical Apache-2.0 file for license scanners.
- **[INFO]** Third-party licenses fully accounted for; no copyleft in Cargo.lock - `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md, Cargo.lock (362 deps)` - Scanned Cargo.lock: no GPL/LGPL/AGPL/SSPL/proprietary crates. ring (ISC AND MIT AND OpenSSL), openssl/openssl-sys (Apache-2.0, transitive via native-tls), p12/zip/rusqlite/cudarc/fontdue/mimalloc/libffi all permissive and documented. The BouncyCastle-ported source files (bc_aes.rs etc.) are correctl -> Optional: wire up `cargo deny check licenses` or `cargo about` in CI to keep the notice authoritative as deps change. No blocker.

**Pre-publish checklist (remove/fix, ordered):**
- [ ] BLOCKER 1 (crates.io, must fix before any publish): Resolve the cratonvm-native-awt publish=false vs cratonvm-vm unconditional-dep contradiction (vm/Cargo.toml:67, native-awt/Cargo.toml:370, use site vm/src/vm/vm_init.rs:1938). Pick one: make native-awt publishable, OR gate it behind an optional feature so vm's dep becomes optional, OR mark vm/cli/libcratonvm/cratonvm-embed publish=false. Then fix the now-false workspace Cargo.toml comment.
- [ ] BLOCKER 2 (repo hygiene, before making repo public): git rm --cached the 595 tracked *.class files, the ~383 tracked bench/ files, the 7 test-infra/ files, and dd1.out — all are already in .gitignore but were committed earlier. Reconcile the docs/ contradiction (513 tracked files vs `docs` in .gitignore).
- [ ] BLOCKER 3 (repo size): the .git history is 848MB. Before going public, audit history for large removed blobs and consider `git filter-repo` to shrink the clone footprint.
- [ ] FIX 4 (publish ordering): publish leaf crates first in topological order (types → reader/native-api → jit-api → jit/gc/native-collections → native-io/classloading → native-builtins → jfr → [native-awt or its replacement] → vm → libcratonvm/cratonvm-embed/cratonvm-cli). The vm dry-run currently stops at 'no matching package cratonvm-classloading' purely because deps aren't published yet — that part is normal ordering, not a defect.
- [ ] FIX 5 (polish, non-blocking): add the 2-line SPDX header to native-api/src/server_socket_ports.rs and vm/src/runtime/stwhang_watch.rs (reach 100% header coverage).
- [ ] FIX 6 (polish, non-blocking): add README.md + `readme=` to libcratonvm and cratonvm-embed so their crates.io pages render.
- [ ] VERIFIED-GOOD (no action): full Apache-2.0 LICENSE; Apache-compliant NOTICE; AUTHORS; ~99.6% SPDX header coverage; all 19 members have complete required+recommended Cargo metadata at version 0.3.0; every inter-crate path dep carries version= (publishability prerequisite met); THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES complete with zero copyleft in Cargo.lock; all community/governance files present; owner/homepage/repo strings consistent across the tree.

## Part 5 - New features & strategic directions

CratonVM is a remarkably complete from-scratch Rust JVM (~323k LoC, 6,000+ tests, custom x64 JIT with 26 optimization rounds, generational GC with concurrent-GC scaffolding already present, JFR, a proven libjvm-substitute embedding layer, and a CUDA GPU-offload path) whose stated mission is running 50+ real server apps on real bytecode. The evidence — especially docs/known-issues/, MEMORY.md, and the partially-landed feature-designs — shows the gating problem is no longer breadth of native coverage but CORRECTNESS UNDER LOAD: a recurring family of register-resident/JIT-only GC-root bugs and GC/thread timing races that surface app-by-app. The highest-leverage moves are therefore foundational: (1) make precise JIT stack maps the validated default and build deopt/OSR/concurrent-GC on top of it; (2) industrialize the HotSpot-differential loop the project already runs by hand into a behavioral differential fuzzer + cargo-llvm-cov coverage + deterministic replay + a CI-tracked JCK number; and (3) productize the already-proven embedding SDK (crates.io publish, CI artifacts, foreign-thread safepoint attach) as the project's clearest differentiator. Secondary directions broaden the addressable surface along axes the repo already half-supports (polyglot JVM languages, java.sql/JDBC, container awareness, observability, an OS-level seccomp sandbox replacing the deprecated SecurityManager) and pick one forward technical bet (a non-NVIDIA GPU backend or a Valhalla/FFM/AOT frontier). Every proposal is tied to concrete repo evidence: existing-but-default-off shadow_stack.rs, the gated g1/zgc crates, the parsing-only fuzz targets, the absent coverage tooling, the 5-increment embedding-api.md, the stub-throwing crypto, and the CUDA-only GPU comparison the project itself authored.

### Proposals

| Title | Cat | Effort | Impact | Rationale |
|-------|-----|:------:|--------|-----------|
| **Precise JIT stack maps as a default-on, first-class subsystem** | technical/runtime — correctness foundation | XL | Very high — retires a whole class of nondeterministic GC-corruption crashes blocking the app gauntlet (WildFly/ES/Hibern | This is the single highest-leverage item given the evidence. A large fraction of the open known-issues and MEMORY.md war-stories are the SAME root cause: register-resident/JIT-only GC roots that the conservative sweep misses (docs/known-iss |
| **Real-frame deopt + precise OSR completion (finish the keystone)** | technical/runtime — tiering | L | High — speculative optimization (uncommon-trap guards), true OSR for long loops, and a real C1/C2 pipeline; closes the l | docs/feature-designs/real-frame-deopt.md and wire-tiered-manager.md are explicitly the 'keystone' and are PARTIALLY landed: IR-path deopt resumes precisely for pure-int methods only (object/cat-2 slots are unit-tested but never reach a live |
| **Differential fuzzing harness vs HotSpot (semantic, not just parsing)** | correctness/quality | M | High — converts the manual 'run-on-HotSpot-and-eyeball' loop that dominates this project's bug history into an automated | The fuzz/ crate exists but every target (fuzz_classfile, fuzz_instruction, fuzz_stack_map, fuzz_descriptor, fuzz_verifier, fuzz_asn1, fuzz_keystore, fuzz_tls_record, fuzz_jimage) is a PARSER/reader fuzzer — none execute bytecode and diff be |
| **Code-coverage instrumentation with cargo-llvm-cov in CI** | correctness/quality | S | Medium-high — cheap to add, immediately surfaces untested JIT/GC/native paths that the gauntlet keeps tripping over, and | With ~323k LoC of Rust and 6,000+ tests, there is no coverage signal anywhere in the repo — .github/workflows/ci.yml runs fmt/build/clippy/test only, and no llvm-cov/tarpaulin config exists. ### Strategic directions

- Make correctness foundational before chasing more apps: land precise JIT stack maps as the DEFAULT and build them once, then let real-frame deopt, precise OSR, and concurrent GC all stand on that single foundation. The known-issues corpus shows the same register-resident-root and timing-race root causes recurring app after app — invest in the mechanism, not the symptom.
- Industrialize the HotSpot-differential loop. The project's entire bug history is 'run on HotSpot, eyeball the diff, fix one native.' Turn that into machinery: a behavioral differential fuzzer, cargo-llvm-cov coverage gates, a deterministic record/replay harness, and a CI-tracked JCK pass-rate. Conformance should be a measured number, not an aspiration.
- Productize the embedding SDK as the flagship differentiator. The libcratonvm cdylib + JNI_CreateJavaVM + cratonvm-embed facade is already proven against live JDK-25 — publish it to crates.io, ship CI artifacts, and finish foreign-thread safepoint attach. A Rust-native, embeddable, libjvm-substitutable JVM is something no mainstream JVM offers and is the cleanest external-adoption on-ramp.
- Mature one concurrent GC (G1 or ZGC) from the existing gated scaffolding into a selectable, gauntlet-validated collector. The server-app targets (WildFly/ES/Kafka/Keycloak) are allocation-heavy and pause-sensitive; the generational sweep is both the throughput bottleneck (Binary Trees 23x) and the source of the timing-race heisenbugs.
- Meet the server apps where they actually run: container/cgroup awareness, sane resource defaults, observability (JFR streaming + flamegraphs), and an OS-level seccomp sandbox that replaces the deprecated SecurityManager story. Deployment ergonomics are currently an afterthought relative to raw bytecode coverage.
- Broaden the addressable surface deliberately on two axes the repo already half-supports: JVM polyglot languages (Kotlin/Scala/Clojure/Groovy — the project already debugs all of them ad hoc) and the database tier (java.sql/JDBC to unblock Hibernate/H2/Neo4j). Both convert work already being paid for into systematic, marketable coverage.
- Pick a forward-looking technical bet to signal direction-of-travel: either widen the GPU story beyond NVIDIA/CUDA (SPIR-V/OpenCL from the existing PTX analyzer) to credibly compete with TornadoVM, or open a frontier the comparison table ignores (Valhalla value types, FFM/Panama maturity, an AOT/Leyden-style cache, or a WASM/RISC-V backend). Choose one and resource it, rather than spreading thin.
- Keep honesty as a competitive asset. SECURITY.md, CRYPTO_STATUS.md, and the GPU COMPARISON.md are unusually candid about gaps (stub-throwing PBKDF2/ML-KEM/TLS, narrow GPU analyzer). That credibility is rare and valuable — back each documented caveat with either a real implementation or a deliberate, sandboxed posture, and never let a synthetic stub silently regress into a correctness claim.
